# xdev — Product Requirements Document (lightweight Go coding agent)

**Product:** xdev · **Repo:** `FreePeak/xdev` · **Module:** `github.com/FreePeak/xdev` · **Binary:** `xdev` · **Data dir:** `~/.xdev/agent/`

Derived from the [omp/pi Go-rebuild architecture research](research/2026-09-09-omp-pi-architecture-go-rebuild.md) (2026-09-09). That research's proposed codename `adze` is **superseded by the repo name `xdev`**: every module path, binary name, and data dir below uses the product name. The session format stays compatible with omp's.

---

## 1. Product goals & non-goals

### Goals

1. **Single static CGO-free Go binary.** Cross-compiles to all six platforms omp supports, ~22 MB dev / ~29 MB installed with the pure-Go SQLite (mnemopi) memory backend linked in — measured 2026-09-12; the backend is a build-time choice, so a build without it keeps the original ~15 MB footprint (amended goal, see M15 row) — instant startup, no runtime install (research IV.0.2, Part V). **2026-09-13 fx cross-check:** neither the size nor the startup claim is enforced by anything. fx gates the same promises with numbers — 2 ms mean startup per command on one authoritative Linux runner, a 52,429-byte per-PR base-vs-head *delta* warning, and a 7.800 MiB release ceiling with 0.250 MiB reserved headroom. Enforcement is #118 (time) and #119 (size delta + ship gate + leak audit); the measurements above stay manual until those land.
2. **Hard <100 MB RSS budget.** Realistic working target 30–70 MB; worst case must stay under 100 MB — roughly 3–8× lighter than a JS-runtime harness. Achieved by having no JS runtime, windowing the in-memory session to the post-boundary tail, bounding every queue, streaming tool output through fixed-size sinks, and keeping the terminal as the scrollback archive (research Parts III, IV.1).
3. **Port the proven pi/omp data model, not the code.** The JSONL session tree (append-only + mutable leaf pointer), the unified `AssistantMessageEvent` stream contract, context reconstruction, compaction, the OutputSink, and the frame-plan TUI are ported with identical semantics and — where possible — identical entry names, for interop with existing session tooling (research IV.0.1).
4. **pi's minimal philosophy.** <1,000-token system prompt including tool descriptions; exactly four core tools (`read`, `write`, `edit`, `bash`); YOLO by default — no permission theater; containment belongs to external sandboxing. (Primary-source check 2026-09-09: pi v0.85 actually ships 8 built-ins — `read`, `write`, `edit`, `bash`, `powershell`, `grep`, `find`, `ls` — with a measured ~460–510-token prompt and MCP only via the `pi-mcp-adapter` extension; xdev keeps the four-core-tool surface and ships grep/find/ls as rg/fd shell-outs, docs/research/parity-pi-internals.md.)
5. **Extensions are processes, not in-process code.** Never load foreign code in-process; extensions are executables speaking JSONL on stdio with per-event timeout + SIGKILL (research IV.0.4, IV.8).
6. **Bounded everything.** Queues, buffers, session windows, and output sinks have hard limits; backpressure is a feature, not a bug (research IV.0.5).
7. **Inspectable sessions.** Fully documented, post-processable JSONL; the harness gives the user full control and inspection of what enters the model context (research I.2.5).

### Non-goals

- No JS/Bun runtime, no in-process plugin VM, no Rust N-API natives.
- No built-in permission/security system: YOLO by default; sandboxing documented externally (container/seatbelt/bubblewrap recipes, like pi). **2026-09-13 fx cross-check:** fx ships no OS sandbox either — searched, zero mechanisms, and its `sandbox` config key is proven inert (`config_runtime.zig:3141`) — so the non-goal holds. Two refinements are adopted anyway because they cost no sandbox and no permission theater: an *opt-in* bounded second-model approval tier (#117, §5.2) and a static-grammar read-only allowlist that auto-runs in a **3-variable scrubbed environment** (#114).
- No `stats.db`-scale sidecar accumulation (77 MB on the reference machine) — a tiny rollup table only.
- ~~No marketplace~~ → **Deferred to M15** (2026-09-11 full-parity directive: user wants all omp features; Go port over subprocess extensions, no bun — issue #53/#22).
- No tree-sitter embedding (CGO + memory); AST work shells out to the `ast-grep` binary when present.
- No in-tree Git library; shell out to `git` (no go-git for v1).
- No built-in LSP server management beyond opt-in-lazy launch: `gopls`/`rust-analyzer` start only when the `lsp` tool is used (omp's `lsp.lazy` default, research IV.10).
- ~~No desktop control (`computer`), full DAP debugging, `security_scan`, `tts`, `generate_image`~~ → **Deferred to M15** (2026-09-11 full-parity directive; per-OS Go backends / adapter-based DAP / scanner shell-outs designed per issue #65–#69).
- ~~No embedded local tiny models~~ → **Deferred to M15 as a decision issue** (#70): ONNX/MLX in Go breaks CGO-free or the memory budget; ship/record a decision, never silently.
- ~~No omp ecosystem services~~ → **Deferred to M15** (#63, #71, #72): Go services/equivalents per issue; the tiny-stats-rollup stance inside the agent binary is unchanged.
- ~~No `sharpshooter` memory backend~~ → **Deferred to M15** (#73) — friction-gated decision files behind the memory backend seam.

## 2. Scope

### In scope

- **Session core** — JSONL tree store: `EntryBase`/`Entry` union, fixed-width 256-byte title slot, append-only leaf-pointer tree, `buildContext` reconstruction, sha256 blob store, compaction (thresholds + overflow/incomplete recovery + context promotion), listing via 4 KiB prefix reads + stat-keyed cache, durability model (per-entry write + flush, optional strict fsync, latched errors, atomic stage-then-rename rewrites, fail-closed on indeterminate writes; **no foreign-writer length guard, no truncate rollback, no unfinished-turn discard at open** — #122).
- **Provider layer** — four wire adapters (Anthropic Messages, OpenAI Completions, OpenAI Responses, Google GenAI) hand-rolled over one shared SSE reader; unified `AssistantMessageEvent` contract; 256-byte partial-JSON throttle with relaxed repair parser; first-progress + idle watchdogs; model-host preconnect; bounded 1,024-event queue with delta coalescing; stop-reason maps + per-provider quirk tables; classified-error taxonomy (Flag bitmask + retriable kinds + overflow regexes, omp-context-resilience §3); failed-request dumps to disk; stalled-stream partial tool-call stubs are never executed (hermes pattern).
- **Agent loop** — one goroutine per turn; bounded tool worker pool (~4–8); steering channels (`steer`/`followUp`/`aside`, queued messages preserved, never discarded); persistence on `message_end` only; layered cancellation via `context.Context` chains; `todo` tool (9-op table, 5 statuses with `blocked`+`reason`, phased init) + TodoTracker loop engine (stop-time reminders max 3, mid-run nudge 12 mutations / 2 per cycle, blocked excluded) ported from omp v18; out-of-band user steering markers appended to tool results; `<untrusted_tool_result>` wrapping for web/browser/MCP output; per-result/per-turn tool-output budgets with disk spill (omp-todos-internals.md, hermes-internals.md).
- **TUI** — tcell + omp frame-plan port: `TerminalFramePlan { history?, viewport }`, active/settled/committed block states, history retirement batches, DSR-anchor resize recovery, alt-buffer overlays; pi-tui editor feature list (path completion — directory-scoped, never a repo walk: see §3's editor row, bracketed paste, multi-line, IME-safe cursor tail).
- **Tools** — `read`/`write`/`edit`/`bash` exactly per pi's input schemas; omp hashline edit UX (`PUT N.=M:`, `MV`, `CUT`, `REM`) + line-numbered snapshots; one `edit` tool carrying the hashline grammar (replace/patch/apply_patch/sloppy are NOT implemented — schema drift is **#267**); OutputSink fixed 32 KiB head + 32 KiB tail windows with omp's truncation marker as the record (**no artifact spill — the middle bytes are discarded**, #115); bash env-hardening list; tier approvals (read-only/write/exec) + `bash.patterns` with conservative compound-command combination; grep/glob/find/ls via rg/fd shell-outs under omp's caps; file-freshness (modified-since-read) rejection; optional backgrounded bash; hidden tools (`yield`/`goal`/`think`) mounted behind activity gates (parity-omp-internals.md).
- **RPC** — JSONL-over-stdio embedder contract: `ready` frame advertising protocol versions + frame limits (1 MiB; v2 chunked reassembly to 64 MiB); id-correlated inbound commands (prompt/steer/follow_up/abort/new_session/state/set_model/…); outbound responses, session/agent events, extension-UI dialogs, host-tool calls, host-URI requests, subagent frames; headless/print modes share the same session core with no UI context.
- **MCP client** — official Go SDK (`github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk`), stdio/HTTP transports, `list_changed` notifications, per-server enable/disable, tool filtering (e.g. browser-automation servers filtered when a built-in browser exists). MCP stays optional — pi ships without it for a reason (context poisoning; CLI tools beat MCP when equivalents exist).
- **Ext subprocess protocol** — extension = any executable speaking JSONL on stdio: capability handshake (tools, commands, event subscriptions, renderers as declarative specs) → event frames (`tool_call` may block/revise fail-closed; `tool_result` may patch) → runtime action requests (steer/followUp/aside, register provider). Per-event timeout + SIGKILL.
- **Subagents** — same session core with restricted tool sets (no ambient MCP/extensions/LSP), structured `outputSchema` (permissive/strict), a `yield` tool, artifact handoff; child = same binary in `print`/`rpc` mode or in-process goroutine sessions with their own `SessionStore`; fork (context-inheriting) vs fresh agent types; stall detection with retry escalation; subagent JSONL + meta.json persistence with orphan auto-resume (claude-code-internals.md).
- **Model resolution, providers, auth & config layering** — `defaultModel` plus an inline `:effort` suffix (the `modelRoles` alias table is gone); per-provider `models.yml` (baseUrl/apiKey/auth/headers/discovery/overrides); credential resolution chain (CLI flag → models.yml → stored OAuth → /login key → env + `.env` layering); Claude Pro/Max + Codex OAuth (PKCE S256, refresh, quota-aware rotation); settings layering engine + `xdev config` CLI; env framework (process env → project `.env` → agent `.env`); approval modes always-ask|write|yolo + per-tool approval record + `bash.patterns` (docs/research/parity-tools-providers.md); role-selector grammar `provider/id:effort@upstream` with alias loop guard; durable credential-block table + quota rotation; failover chains; `promptCacheKey` = sessionId with fork inheritance cleared on model/thinking/tool overrides (omp-context-resilience §1).
- **Session UX** — slash commands (native markdown discovery project+user, quote-aware `$1..$n`/`$@`/`$ARGUMENTS` expansion, unknown-command fall-through); lifecycle `/new` `/fresh` `/clear` `/drop` `/fork`; `--continue`/`--resume` + picker + `switchSession` runtime; `/tree` `/branch`; context files (AGENTS.md/CLAUDE.md hierarchy, `@path` imports) + SYSTEM.md/APPEND_SYSTEM.md variants + `--system-prompt` flags; keybindings remap; `/dump` transcript (docs/research/parity-session-ux.md); session titles (`/rename`, ai-title small-model generation, custom > ai > first-prompt cascade, picker metadata); context-file prompt-injection scanning; three-tier stable→context→volatile prompt-cache assembly (claude-code-internals.md, hermes-internals.md).
- **Agent system** — task-agent discovery (markdown+frontmatter, first-wins merge), spawn policy (allowlist, depth guard, plan-mode read-only children), model precedence; `hub` tool (messaging, jobs, process supervision) + Agent Hub TUI roster; hooks event bus with fail-closed `tool_call`/`tool_result` interception; advisor/watchdog steering; prewalk big→`@smol` model handoff; magic keywords (`ultrathink`/`orchestrate`/`workflowz`); plan mode + `xd://propose`/`xd://resolve` finalization (docs/research/parity-agent-system.md); parent-owned `todo` policy (stripped from subagent tool sets except prewalk-armed children); cross-session messaging (mailbox dirs, SendMessage, InboxPoller idle delivery, accept/hold/off inbound policy) as NICE (claude-code-internals.md).
- **Knowledge & chrome** — memory backend seam + local backend (extraction → session-model consolidation → MEMORY.md + learned.md) + `memory://` read seam; skills (SKILL.md discovery, `skill://` protocol, `/skill:` commands, managed skills); `learn` tool; theme engine (66-token JSON themes, vars, color detection, dark/light auto, symbol presets, live reload); TUI chrome (status-line/HUD segments, overlays, custom tool renderers) (docs/research/parity-knowledge-ui.md); frozen memory snapshots injected at prompt build (not per-turn) to protect prefix cache (hermes pattern).
- **Extended tools & ecosystems (demand-driven)** — eval kernel (py persistent NDJSON cells), notebook virtual text, `web_search` (22-provider chain with constraint relaxation), `github` tool, `ast_grep`/`ast_edit`, browser via chromedp, checkpoint/rewind (session-tree branch + report entry, NOT a git shadow repo), custom subprocess tools, MCP config extensions, LSP tool + `lsp-config`, secrets redaction, context promotion, deferred tool-catalog bridge (`tool_search`/`tool_describe`/`tool_call`), marketplace/plugin manager; v2 modes: vibe (director + workers), collab (E2E encrypted), multi-provider discovery, profiles (docs/research/parity-tools-providers.md).
- **Typed judgment backend** — the `typesafe` tool speaks TypeSafe System One over `POST /v1/systemone`; a self-hosted Laya server can use the same wire contract through `TYPESAFE_BASE_URL` or `typesafe.baseUrl`. Local authentication is optional (`LAYA_API_KEY` or `typesafe.apiKey`); hosted Jev remains the default with `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` and `jev-latest`. The Laya process stays external, so xdev remains CGO-free.

### Out of scope (research IV.10)

- `stats.db`-scale sidecar databases — keep stats in a tiny rollup table.
- In-process extension loading — replaced by the subprocess protocol.
- Rust N-API natives — Go stdlib + subprocesses cover ~90%; the FS-scan shared cache is the one concept worth re-implementing (M7).
- Marketplace and composer-shape systems until demand exists (theme engine itself is now IN scope — M12 CORE; marketplace/plugin manager is the M13 tail).
- Built-in LSP server management — opt-in-lazy only.
- `go-git` — shell out to `git`.

The rows after them come from the **Claude Code full-surface re-sweep** (2026-09-14, §5.3, [cc-resweep-2026-09-14.md](research/claude-code/cc-resweep-2026-09-14.md)): 197 verified gap drafts curated into issues #128–#239 (112 filed + scope comments on #91/#92/#105/#117/#123).

## 3. Architecture (HLD summary)

### 3.1 Module layout

```text
xdev/
  cmd/xdev/            # CLI entry: tui | print | rpc modes
  internal/ai/         # provider adapters (anthropic, openai-completions, openai-responses, google)
    internal/ai/sse/   #   shared SSE frame reader, unified AssistantEvent, partial-JSON w/ 256B throttle
  internal/agent/      # agent loop: steering channels, tool scheduling, cancellation layers
  internal/session/    # JSONL tree store, entries, buildContext, compaction, blob store, listing
  internal/tool/       # read/write/edit/bash + registry, approvals, OutputSink, env hardening
  internal/tui/        # frame-plan renderer over tcell
  internal/ext/        # subprocess extension protocol (JSONL capability handshake)
  internal/mcp/        # MCP client via official Go SDK
  internal/store/      # pure-Go SQLite: history FTS, stats rollup; blob store in stdlib
  internal/protocol/   # RPC v1/v2 wire types (compatible with omp's where possible)
```

### 3.2 Session core port spec (the heart)

- **On-disk layout:** `~/.xdev/agent/sessions/<encoded-cwd>/<timestamp>_<sessionId>.jsonl` (cwd-bucketized so symlink aliases share a bucket) and `~/.xdev/agent/blobs/<sha256>`. Listing reads only a 4 KiB prefix (plus a bounded 32 KiB tail for lifecycle status), stat-keyed cached, bounded parallel scan — never parses whole files.
- **File format:** JSONL, one JSON object per line. Physically begins with a **fixed-width 256-byte `title` slot** (rename/list never rewrite the body), then a `type:"session"` header (id, timestamp, cwd, title/titleSource, additionalDirectories, previousSessionFiles, providerPromptCacheKey, parentSession), then entries.
- **Entry model:** every entry has `{type, id (8-char), parentId, timestamp}`. History is an **append-only tree + mutable leaf pointer**: an append creates one entry whose `parentId` = current leaf and becomes the leaf; `branch(entryId)` moves only the leaf pointer — nothing is mutated or deleted; `resetLeaf()` starts a new root (`/clear` writes a payload-free `reset_boundary` marker).
- **Entry types:** `message` (full AgentMessage incl. usage/cost), `thinking_level_change`, `model_change` (model map), `service_tier_change`, `compaction` (summary + `firstKeptEntryId` + `tokensBefore`), `branch_summary` (context for abandoned branches), `reset_boundary`, `custom` (opaque, extension-owned via namespaced `customType`; core reserves lifecycle values such as `tool_execution_start`, `session_exit`), `custom_message`. Go shape: `EntryBase` + one pointer per entry type (single-union decode).
- **Context reconstruction** (`buildContext(entries, leaf)`): walk `parentId` leaf→root (cycle-bounded), reverse, derive runtime state from the path (thinking level, the model, service tiers, mode), pick the emission boundary (latest `reset_boundary` or latest compaction: emit summary + entries from `firstKeptEntryId`), convert `message`/`custom_message`/`branch_summary` to model messages, neutralize dangling tool calls, drop unsafe turns. Pure function, unit-testable against pi/omp-generated session files. **Acceptance test: xdev must resume an omp-generated session file and produce an equivalent LLM context** — that one test pins the entire port (research IV.3).
- **Blob store:** sha256 content-addressed files; externalize images/data-URLs to `blob:sha256:<hash>` at omp's thresholds; truncate >500k-char strings with byte-exact exceptions for signed provider blocks; files >8 MiB load via streaming JSONL.
- **Compaction:** thresholds + overflow/incomplete recovery + context promotion first; `handoff` doc generation and provider-native streaming compaction later (LLM prompt engineering, portable as-is).
- **Delta from ZCode (2026-09-22, [research/zcode-internals.md](research/zcode-internals.md) §6.1, §6.3):** the peer runs **three compaction tiers, cheapest first**, and xdev has the top one only. (a) *microcompact* — no model call, runs before the request, replaces old tool results with `[Old tool result content cleared]` keeping the newest 5, over a declared tool set, with a `minTokenSavings` floor and a provider-visible `microcompact_boundary` event so the transcript knows content was dropped. (b) *auto-compact* with a circuit breaker (`MAX_CONSECUTIVE_AUTOCOMPACT_FAILURES = 3`) and a decision record carrying `reason` + `tokenSource: estimate|provider_usage` (provider-reported usage overrides the estimate, and the decision says which it used). (c) the boundary ladder xdev already has. The third ZCode idea is loop-level and absent here: a **rapid-refill breaker** (`RAPID_REFILL_TOOL_TURN_THRESHOLD = 3`, `MAX_CONSECUTIVE_RAPID_REFILLS = 3`) that trips when compaction does not buy space — a compaction followed by fewer than 3 tool turns before the window refills again — and fails the boundary loudly instead of compacting at full provider cost forever. xdev's `maybeCompact` logs a failure and returns; nothing counts. Filed.
- **Durability model (deliberately pragmatic):** appends are synchronous in-memory + writer hand-off with **no fsync** (`bufio.Writer` + explicit `Flush()` per append; optional strict-fsync flag for paranoid durability); new ordinary sessions stay memory-only until the first assistant message or `ensureOnDisk()` (no junk files for aborted starts); full rewrites (migrations, moves, repair) are atomic stage-then-rename with an EPERM-safe fallback and commit guard — if a rewrite's durability cannot be proven, the manager **fails closed** rather than silently losing data; persistence errors are **latched** and rethrown on later flush/close; crash forensics via `session_exit` + `tool_execution_start` entries, with the loader synthesizing an `aborted` assistant message on resume.
- **Torn-tail prevention (#122, added 2026-09-14) — what is actually guaranteed:** the store counts the bytes it has committed and verifies the file ends exactly there before every write; a write, flush or fsync failure cuts the file back to that boundary and latches, so a half-record can never poison the record after it. A file longer than committed is read before it is judged: a **partial** tail is this store's own interrupted flush and is cut (reported by `RepairNotice`); a tail ending on a complete line is **another process's history** and the store refuses to append (`ErrConcurrentWriter`) rather than forking the tree or deleting someone else's records; a file that *shrank* is refused the same way. Opening a session never mutates it — the repair happens on the first append — and `Open` will not resume a file reachable by more than one path (`nlink > 1`, since writing through either name silently changes the other). The bound this replaces: an unterminated final line used to make `Open` fail outright, so **one interrupted write bricked the whole session**; measured against 12 real `SIGKILL`s of a writer pushing ~9 MB records, no timing produced a torn tail on macOS/APFS (the kernel commits an append after copying it), which is why the repair is pinned by constructing the byte state directly rather than by racing a kill. `no fsync` above still means what it said: without `Options.StrictFsync`, a power loss can drop the last flushes — what is now impossible is a *partially written record*, and a session file can never again become unreadable.
- **Prompt history:** deliberately a *separate* SQLite/FTS5 subsystem — replay fidelity lives in JSONL, search UX in SQLite; never mixed.

### 3.3 Provider layer

- **Four wire adapters, one shared SSE reader**, hand-rolled over `net/http` (HTTP/2 for free): `data:` frames and `[DONE]` sentinel for OpenAI-family, typed events for Anthropic. Each adapter maps its native stream onto the unified event contract verbatim: `start` → block lifecycle triplets (`text_start/delta/end`, `thinking_*`, `toolcall_*`), `image_end`, terminal `done{reason: stop|length|toolUse}` or `error{reason: aborted|error}`.
- **Partial-JSON tool args:** the final parse at `toolcall_end` is authoritative and strict; when it fails the call is answered with `{}` (never a guessed prefix) and the turn survives — the tool's own required-argument error tells the model to re-issue, so an unparseable blob is never a stream error. Salvaging a prefix is wrong by construction: the relaxed repairer (`internal/ai/sse/partialjson.go`, ~200 LOC port, `ParsePartial`) stops at the first pair it cannot read, so `{"path":"/x" content:"…"}` would become a write of an empty file. `ParsePartial` keeps its streaming/preview role only; the strict parse never routes through it.
- **Abort must resolve a pending read** (ZCode cross-check §5.3, filed): the watchdog above judges liveness from the source side so a stalled consumer can never kill a healthy stream (#283), which is right — but it leaves the *consumer* with no arm that resolves `iterator.next()` when the turn is cancelled. ZCode races three promises and escalates its idle window by `30 s × retryNumber`. Both behaviours belong in one watchdog; see §3.9 item 9.
- **Watchdogs:** goroutine per stream — first-progress timer + idle timer, reset on every event; expiry aborts into `error{aborted}`. **Preconnect:** warm-up dial on model resolve (saves 100–300 ms on transcontinental hops).
- **Stop-reason maps + quirk tables** ported from pi (they encode years of provider annoyance); provider-owned bounded retries (empty completions, malformed envelopes, capability fallbacks such as retrying without rejected strict-tool fields).
- **Backpressure fix:** bounded event queue (1,024 events). On overflow, coalesce consecutive `*_delta` events of the same block — rendering already coalesces visually; persistence only needs final messages. This removes omp's documented unbounded-queue wart without changing semantics.
- **Cancellation:** `context.Context` chains (session → agent → request); tool execution gets a merged derived context (small `errgroup`-based merge helper; Go has no `AbortSignal.any`).

### 3.4 Agent loop

- One goroutine per turn; tools run on a **bounded worker pool** (same-batch concurrency kept from omp, capped ~4–8).
- **Steering** via `chan Steering{steer|followUp|aside}` consumed at step boundaries; `aside` injects at the next agent step boundary without killing the in-flight tool batch; queued messages preserved, never thrown away.
- **Prompt contract (decision pending, ZCode cross-check §3.2).** ZCode's context builder ships three short paragraphs xdev's `SystemPromptBase` has no equivalent for, each aimed at a failure mode xdev actually has: (1) *"Text you write between tool calls may not be shown to the user. Everything the user needs from this turn — answers, summaries, findings, conclusions, deliverables — must be in the final text message of your turn, with no tool calls after it."* (2) *"When the conversation grows long, some or all of the current context is summarized … you don't need to wrap up early or hand off mid-task."* (3) a `# Harness` block teaching how the system behaves (a denied call means the user declined it — adjust, don't retry verbatim; mid-conversation system turns are system-controlled; reference code as `file_path:line_number`). xdev has the machinery all three describe (steering, compaction, permissions, hooks) and tells the model none of it. Filed.
- **Persistence only on `message_end`** (matching omp); streaming text is never persisted mid-flight.
- **Sectioned prompt with declared cache hints (ZCode cross-check §3.1).** The peer's `ContextBuilder` returns `ContextSection[]` where each section declares `{injectionTarget: system|meta_user, cacheHint: stable|dynamic}`, and assembly happens *by hint* — one system message per (target, hint) group — with every system message stamped `cacheControl: ephemeral`. The prompt-cache boundary is therefore a property of the section, computed once at build time, instead of something a human keeps stable by discipline. xdev concatenates `base + context + rules + tools` with character budgets and no boundary concept; this is the structural version of the manual work in [research/omp-prompt-cache-2026-09-15.md](research/omp-prompt-cache-2026-09-15.md). Filed.
- **Extension interception** (`tool_call`/`tool_result`) called synchronously through the ext-protocol bus with **per-call timeout + kill switch**. `tool_call` policy blocks fail closed; a *hung* extension is killable — which in-process code cannot offer and a subprocess can.
- **Subagents:** same session core, restricted tool sets + `outputSchema` (permissive/strict enforcement) + `yield` tool; start in-process (cheap goroutines, shared provider pool), move out-of-process later if isolation matters.

- **Delta from ZCode (2026-09-22, [research/zcode-internals.md](research/zcode-internals.md) §1.4, §4.1):** the batch above runs *after* the stream closes. ZCode's core has a **streaming execution coordinator** (`apps/zcode-cli/packages/core/src/runtime/methods/streaming-tool-coordinator.ts`) that starts a tool as soon as its arguments finish streaming, for a strictly-gated subset — `readOnly && concurrentSafe && !destructive && !needsApproval && !requiresUserInteraction && sideEffectScope === "none"` — then joins the in-flight promises at end-of-stream. A streaming-execution failure falls back to the normal end-of-stream path rather than failing the turn, and a stream that dies mid-flight hands its already-settled results to the recovery path instead of discarding them. The gate is not portable today: xdev classifies tools by **name** (`internal/tool/policy.go`, `planReadOnlyTools` in `internal/agent/planmode.go`) and the `tool.Tool` interface carries no capability vocabulary — so this is blocked on §3.6's manifest item, and is the concrete payoff for it.
- **Plan mode's fail-open list is the second consumer of the same missing vocabulary** (2026-09-12 parity T3 #27/#28): `planReadOnlyTools` is a hardcoded map, so a new read-only tool is denied under plan mode until a human edits it, and the full toolset is still *advertised* to the model while planning. ZCode replaces both halves with a permission policy over the declared capability (`permission/service.ts` `checkPlanMode`: read-only → allow; non-destructive MCP → allow; explicit `allowedInPlanMode` + `sideEffectScope === "session"` → allow; else deny) plus an `EnterPlanMode` description that tells the model before it acts. The deny-side shape fail-closes on unseen tools, which is the property xdev wants.

- **A blank turn is not a finished turn** (2026-09-17, [#331](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/331)): the no-tool-call branch used to end the run on `len(msg.ToolCalls()) == 0` alone, so an assistant turn that streamed only reasoning (thinking block, `stopReason: stop`, no text) — the shape a thinking-mode upstream leaves behind, and the one the gateway's `(context elided)` reasoning echo produces on a tool-loop continuation — ended the session with nothing on screen. `isEmptyAssistant` now gates that branch; a blank turn gets one hidden `EmptyTurnNudgePrompt` user turn (`Attribution: "empty-turn"`) and the loop continues. The bound is **per run** (one bool, same shape as the TTSR interrupt budget), so a model that only ever stalls cannot spend turns on the nudge. `TurnHooks.OnEmptyTurn` names the stall in the transcript; the attribution rides the same three harness-text consumers as the turn-budget wrap-up (replay, rewind, stats).

### 3.5 TUI

- Base: **tcell** (raw, fast, low-alloc) with omp's frame-plan model on top: each frame = mandatory chrome (editor/status/HUD/overlay) + `TerminalFramePlan { history?: {id, rows}, viewport: rows[] }`; transcript blocks in three states — **active** (mutable, viewport-resident), **settled** (finalized, reflows on resize until capacity pressure), **committed** (written into terminal scrollback, caches released).
- **History retirement** offered as batches only under capacity pressure, acknowledged only after the write is accepted — **the terminal's native scrollback is the archive** (no app-side scrollback memory). **Resize** borrows the alt-buffer, recovers the anchor via a DSR (CSI 6n) cursor round-trip, and replays per mode (`rebuild`/`append`/`preserve`). Tool blocks collapse by row budget (0→hidden, 1→label line, 2→folded card, ≥3→full renderer).
  - **Frame plan as shipped (2026-09-14, `internal/tui/rowindex.go`):** xdev keeps its own scrollback (there is no native-scrollback retirement path under tcell), so omp's `ScrollView` contract — render a **prebuilt line buffer** at the offset, never the whole session — is implemented as the row index: one stamped render per block (`blockRend`, a superseded render replaced in place), cumulative row offsets (`start`), and viewport-only materialization. A settled block then costs one struct compare per frame; `draw()` and every scroll key stopped walking the transcript (measured at 85 200 rows: **7.8 ms → 0.50 ms per frame**, `totalLines` 68 µs → O(1)). The **bounded middle trim** rides the same stamp: past the newest `toolRecentResults`=4 results a tool block collapses to a head+tail window with the hidden-row count in the hole, so command output is trimmed before the conversation is, `Ctrl+O` opts a result out, and the full text stays in the session JSONL (the same 400-turn transcript is 85 200 → 12 732 rows). Reasoning carries no tier at all (2026-09-17): it renders in a fixed `thinkBoxRows`=12-row box the wheel scrolls, so the trim only ever bounds tool output.
- **Rendering discipline:** every row exactly `width` visible cells; ANSI is zero-width; one trailing separator row per block; synchronized output; IME-safe cursor tails.
- **Editor:** port pi-tui's feature list — path completion, bracketed paste, multi-line, IME-safe cursor tail. Width/ANSI helpers from `charmbracelet/x/ansi` + `mattn/go-runewidth`. **Completion is directory-scoped, not fuzzy over a repo walk** (2026-09-17, [#314]+the bare-`@` fix): the token names one directory plus a prefix, so a keystroke is one `os.ReadDir` (~20–60 µs) and a bare `@` lists the completion root — omp's own shape, where `#getFileSuggestions("@")` is one readdir of `basePath` and the native fuzzy find only runs once a prefix exists. The FS-scan cache stays grep/glob's, never a keystroke's: the whole-repo fallback cost 5.7 s per bare `@` on a 226k-file tree and ran on the UI thread. **A bare PREFIX is answered by a background index, not a keystroke walk (2026-09-29):** `internal/tui/pathindex.go` walks the cwd once at wiring, off the UI thread, and publishes every file path root-relative, sorted by lowercased base name (`.git`/`.hg`/`.svn` and the vendor/build trees in `indexSkipDir` pruned — they are 95% of this polyrepo's 811k files and stay reachable by drill). `@pa` then binary-searches that slice: 7.2 µs/op, 0 filesystem calls, 178k files = 2.9 s and 44 MB once. The readdir still owns every token that names a directory, so a drill is live and the directory listing is unchanged; the index only answers what a readdir cannot.
- **Visual target: the Grok CLI.** M4's look-and-feel target is xAI's Grok CLI (user requirement, 2026-09-09; primary evidence: the grok-build open-source sync — github.com/xai-org/grok-build `crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager-render/src/theme/{groknight,grokday}.rs` + `xai-grok-pager/src/views/prompt_widget/`, verified 2026-09-10; cross-checked against the official media.x.ai TUI screenshot by pixel sampling). Concretely:
  - **Theme slots** (superset of omp's 66-token system; all RGB-defined, quantized at startup to truecolor/256/16, `NO_COLOR` → monochrome): backgrounds `bg_base` `bg_light` `bg_dark` `bg_highlight` `bg_hover` `bg_terminal` `bg_visual`; accents `accent_user` `accent_assistant` `accent_thinking` `accent_tool` `accent_system` `accent_error` `accent_success` `accent_running`; text `text_primary` `text_secondary`; grays `gray_dim` `gray` `gray_bright`; prompt chrome `prompt_border` `prompt_border_active`; markdown `md_heading_h1..h3` `md_code` `md_code_bg` `md_muted` `link_fg`. 2026-09-10 status: base+md slots implemented in `internal/theme`.
  - **Reference palette (GrokNight, the default) — VERIFIED 2026-09-10 from grok-build source** (`groknight.rs`): bg ramp `#0a0a0a` terminal / `#141414` base / `#242424` highlight+user-band / `#1c1c1c` code bg; text `#e1e1e1` primary / `#c8c8c8` secondary; grays `#585858`/`#6c6c6c`/`#787878`; accents (TokyoNight Night) BLUE `#7aa2f7` CYAN `#7dcfff` GREEN `#9ece6a` MAGENTA `#bb9af7` ORANGE `#ff9e64` PURPLE `#9d7cd8` RED `#f7768e` TEAL `#1abc9c` YELLOW `#e0af68`; inline code `#3A95AB`; link `#7aa6da`; prompt border `#323237`/active `#505058`. **Semantic correction vs the old provisional guess:** `accent_user` is neutral `#c8c8c8` (FG_DARK) — user is NOT magenta; magenta belongs to assistant/thinking/running. GrokDay (`grokday.rs`): `#eeeeee`/`#dedede`/`#f5f5f5`, text `#262626`/`#444444`, MAGENTA `#7D4BC6`, RED `#CD3048`, TEAL `#0A8E70`, BLUE `#2F64D2`, BLUE1 `#0F87A2`, GREEN `#378E23`, borders `#c8c8cd`/`#a5a5af`.
  - **Grok composer anatomy (implemented):** rounded box `╭─╮│╰─╯` (border `prompt_border_active` when focused), `❯ ` prefix (accent_user, bold), placeholder "Build anything" (muted, unfocused states), model info line embedded in the bottom border (chrome_caption, gray_dim), blinking block cursor; box grows with wrapped input (inline-viewport grow pushes into scrollback).
- **Scrollback layout** (`pager.toml`-shaped config): outer padding `outer_vpad=1` `hpad 2/2`, block padding `2/2`, left accent rail per block (`collapsed_accent_char` `❙`), scrollbar (a proportional right-edge groove / thumb sized `⌊2·vp·vp/total⌋` half rows, `scrollModel.Scrollbar`, reserved last column, painted opencode-style: `bg_highlight` track fill with a `gray_bright` thumb that lands between rows as `█ ▀ ▄`) with the `▼/▲` row-count hint riding the composer divider, `expandable_indicator` `›` on foldable entries, `tab_width 4`, `line_under_last_entry` off.
  - **`sticky_headers` pinning user prompts — LANDED 2026-10-02 (`feat/sticky-prompt`, user-requested: "the latest user prompt show the sticky box in top like grok cli").** Port of grok-build `crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager/src/scrollback/sticky.rs` (fetched 2026-10-02): a user prompt is the section header of its turn, so scrolled past it pins to the top of the viewport and **shrinks one row per scrolled row** (floor 3 rows — grok's `min_height` is 4, but its 2 padding rows are xdev's prompt text), a blank gap separates it from the stream, and the NEXT prompt approaching clips it from above (`clip_top`) and drops the gap so the transcript's first row does not move mid-push. `internal/tui/sticky.go` keeps the whole thing as pure 1D coordinate math over row heights — `computeSticky(firstRow, vp, prompts) stickyLayout{block, visible, clipTop, rows, row}` — testable with no screen, and `stickyHeaderRows` renders the pinned prompt from the row index's OWN inline render, so pinning changes WHERE a prompt is, never what it looks like (same banded `❯` rows, ` …` admitting clipped text). The scroll model is untouched: the header owns the rows it hides, the transcript paints `[start+rows, end)`, and one row of scroll still moves the viewport's bottom line by exactly one. Row-0 top bar unchanged. Because a header row is NOT the document row its index names, the frame publishes its geometry (`stickyHdr/stickyVis/stickyBlock/stickyDoc`) beside the scrollbar's and `selCornerAt`/`selDocRow`/`userRowAt`/`thinkBoxAt` consult it: a click on a pinned prompt opens that prompt's menu, a drag across the boundary copies header rows AND the rows below, and no click is aimed at a reasoning box that is not under the pointer. ponytail: no new state, no new file beyond the one, no second scroll model — the header is a prefix of the rows the viewport already had.
  - **Block styling:** edit-diff blocks (indent, `hunk_separator "…"`, optional dual line numbers, bg light/dark variants, collapsed one-liner by default with `+N/−M` summary), tool blocks collapsed by row budget, thinking blocks in the result frame — one row collapsed, a clicked (focused) box grown to a 12-row wheel-scrollable window (`gray_dim` body, `accent_thinking` border, bold while focused), fenced code with syntax highlighting via bundled tmTheme
  - **Interaction contract:** simple mode (arrows + `Shift+Arrow` turn jumps, Space/letter refocuses prompt) and opt-in vim mode (`j/k`, `H/L` turns, `J/K` responses, `h/l` fold, `e` expand, `y` copy, `Enter`/`Ctrl+F` fullscreen viewer, `Ctrl+E` thinking folds); `Tab` prompt↔scrollback focus; blocking cards (permission/ask) own the keyboard with `Tab`/`Shift+Tab` row walk and layered `Esc`-steps-back semantics; `Esc` mid-turn = cancel panel (never kills in-flight tools silently — offers keep-running).
  - **Parity is layout + block styling + interaction + palette — NOT 1:1 behavior.** Grok features that fight tcell's low-alloc model or the <100 MB RSS budget are **config-off-by-default or dropped**: 30 fps accent-wave animation (off; static accent rail is the default rendering), mouse hover highlights (off), background blending/`bg_blend` runtime color generation (off — every color comes from the quantized theme table, never blended at draw time). The one exception shipped since (2026-10-03, `feat/tui-motion`) is a bounded ~200 ms `theme.Lerp` ease on the reasoning box's focus border: a tween that ends in ≤6 ticks, that asks for a repaint only while it is in flight, and that reuses the theme's own two inks — the rule above rejects a *sustained* ambient animation, not a transient state change. If any later ship, they are opt-in and must re-justify against the memory budget in M8's audit.

### 3.6 Tools

- `read`/`write`/`edit`/`bash` **exactly per pi's input schemas** (model familiarity is an asset). omp-style line-numbered snapshots + hashline anchors (`PUT N.=M:`, `MV`, `CUT`, `REM`) are the proven edit UX — pure string ops in Go.
  - **Shipped 2026-09-14 (`edit` speaks omp's dialect).** `edit`'s advertised schema is one required string, `input`: a `[path]` / `[path#tag]` header followed by `PUT`/`CUT`/`REM`/`MV` op lines with `+` body rows — the format omp's own tool documents, so a model that has ever edited in omp writes a call xdev accepts. `PUT <N:` / `PUT >N:` insert before/after a kept line (omp semantics; the earlier prose had them inverted). The structured `{path, ops}` object stays as the internal encoding — same verbs, same range engine — because the harness's own tests and the argument-repair path speak it; it is deliberately not advertised. Every malformed shape observed in live sessions is repaired rather than rejected: `ops` sent as a quoted JSON string (7 calls), `path` pushed inside an op (6), the whole args blob sent as bare patch text, patch text inside `ops`. What still fails teaches: each rejection names the accepted shape, and a `[{"op": …}]` header is refused as "not a file header" instead of being read as a path. Grammar lives in `internal/tool/hashline.go`; the recap prose stays under `MaxToolDescriptionChars` (381/400) because the parameters schema is the uncapped, authoritative channel.
  - **Shipped 2026-09-17 (the two ways a live `edit` call actually fails now teach the fix).** Live xdev sessions produced two recurring rejections, both the model's format rather than a parser bug, and both answered uselessly. (1) `edit: edit: line 2: expected a PUT/CUT/REM/MV op line or a [path] header, got "@@"` — a model that sent a unified diff. (2) `edit: body rows start with "+", got "// …"` — a model that knew the verbs but not the row convention. Three diffs in `internal/tool`: `Execute` strips the grammar's own `edit: ` lead before wrapping it, so a rejection reads `edit: line N: …` and never `edit: edit: …` (`strings.TrimPrefix`, pinned by `TestEditErrorsCarryOnePrefix`); `parseHashline` scans for a hunk header up front (`hashlineDiffRe`, `^@@\s*-?\d`) and names the dialect — "this tool does not take diffs", then the hashline form — instead of letting the diff's `--- a/f` first line become a confusing header error; and the row, header and no-op rejects each name the fix. The `Parameters()` prose now **leads** with the `+` rule (it was at char ~600 of 1100, the first thing a provider that truncates a tool description drops). No behavior change to a well-formed patch.
- `bash`: port `executeBash` semantics; one-shot process spawn (~1 ms in Go, no shell-reuse cache needed) — but port the **env-hardening list** and the **OutputSink byte-for-byte** (fixed head+tail windows; no spill — see §2 row). PTY via `creack/pty` behind the same eligibility predicate (UI + opt-in + env escape hatch).
- Grep/glob/fd: v1 shells out to `rg`/`fd` when present (pi's own answer) with a pure-Go fallback (`fs.WalkDir` + `regexp` + gitignore parsing); v2 ports the pi-walker shared FS-scan cache (TTL, partition by traversal options, empty-result revalidation, invalidate-on-write).
- AST tools: **no tree-sitter embedding** (CGO + memory); shell out to the `ast-grep` binary if present — preserves the CGO-free guarantee.
- **Post-write syntax verification for Go (shipped 2026-09-22, [internal/tool/verify.go](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/blob/main/internal/tool/verify.go))** — `edit`/`write` append a one-line note to the tool result when the file they just wrote no longer parses (`go/parser`, standard library: no dependency, no subprocess, no language server to start), mirrored into `Details.syntaxWarning`. It reports, never vetoes or rolls back, and says "syntax" because that is what a parser can see. **Deliberately the parse tier**: [#263](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/263) owns the type-error tier (LSP `publishDiagnostics` on the write path), and `internal/tool` cannot import `internal/lsp` anyway (`internal/lsp/tool.go` imports `internal/tool` for `Result`). Skipped for non-Go files and for files over 2 MiB — neither is the shape an edit-tool patch makes.
- Approvals: port the tier model (read-only/write/exec) + `bash.patterns` allow/deny/prompt with conservative compound-command combination; approvals stay **separate** from interception (approval decides *whether to run*, interception routes *to a better tool*); real containment is external sandboxing (document container recipes like pi).

- **Capability manifest on the tool contract** (a decision, not yet a design — [ZCode cross-check](research/zcode-internals.md) §4.1, issue [#420](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/420)): each tool declares `{readOnly, destructive, concurrentSafe, sideEffectScope, needsApproval, alwaysAsk?, requiresUserInteraction?, timeoutMs, maxOutputBytes, riskLevel, resultBudget}` next to its schemas, and the plan-mode policy, the scheduler, the approval gate and (if it lands) the streaming-execution gate all read *that* declaration. Today xdev keeps three parallel name-keyed tables (`internal/tool/policy.go` tiers, `planReadOnlyTools` in `internal/agent/planmode.go`, the scheduler's `READ_ONLY_TOOLS`) and a fourth consumer would need a fifth table; the peer's rule is the argument — an intrinsic capability is "read by the executor, not guessed at the call site by tool name". A manifest that only the plan gate consumes would be worse than the maps, which is why it is filed as its own issue rather than as a rider on the plan-mode fix.
### 3.7 Memory budget (hard <100 MB RSS)


| Component | Budget | Technique |
|---|---|---|
| Go runtime + binary + idle GC heap | 8–15 MB | Standard; `GOGC` tuning, `debug.SetMemoryLimit` as hard backstop |
| Provider HTTP/2 connections + TLS | 3–8 MB | Shared `http.Client`, connection reuse, streaming bodies |
| Session entries in memory (windowed) | 10–30 MB | Materialize only the post-boundary tail (compaction/reset boundary); older entries stay on disk, loaded on demand for list/tree/export |
| In-flight assistant stream state | 1–3 MB | Only the current message materialized |
| Tool output sinks | <1 MB | Fixed head+tail windows, middle bytes **discarded** (OutputSink ported exactly; retention is **#115**, still open) |
| Transcript renders (row index) | 2–6 MB | One stamped render per block, replaced in place; viewport-only frames; aged tool output collapses first (§3.5) — measured 30 MB RSS through a streamed 2 500-line answer |
| JSON decode buffers | 2–5 MB | `json.Decoder` per JSONL line; never unmarshal whole files |
| MCP child servers | 0 (agent RSS) | Child processes; agent holds pipes + tool descriptors only |
| **Total worst case** | **~30–70 MB (est.)** | Enforced via memory limit + bounded structures |

`debug.SetMemoryLimit` is the process backstop: a runaway turn degrades into "compact now" instead of an OOM kill.

### 3.8 Go ecosystem mapping

| Need | Choice | Note |
|---|---|---|
| TUI | `tcell/v2` (+ `rivo/tview` if rich widgets wanted) | Low-alloc, mature; pair with omp frame-plan port |
| ANSI/width | `charmbracelet/x/ansi`, `mattn/go-runewidth` | Zero-width/IME correctness |
| CLI | stdlib `flag`; `spf13/cobra` only if subcommand UX needed | Prefer stdlib for lightweight |
| LLM SSE | hand-rolled over `net/http` (HTTP/2 built in) | One shared reader + four adapters; nothing else needed |
| JSON repair (partial tool args) | port the relaxed parser (~200 LOC) | `tidwall/gjson` for fast paths if needed |
| SQLite (history FTS / stats rollup) | `ncruces/go-sqlite3` (WASM, pure Go) or `modernc.org/sqlite` | CGO-free; FTS5 included, for prompt history |
| MCP | `github.com/modelcontextprotocol/go-sdk` | Official Go SDK |
| PTY | `creack/pty` | Thin, proven |
| Git | shell out to `git` (like omp) | No go-git for v1 |
| Grep/glob fallback | stdlib + `sabhiram/go-gitignore`; shell out to `rg`/`fd` when present | v2: FS-scan cache port |
| Diff (edit previews) | `pmezard/go-difflib` or `sergi/go-diff` | Structured diffs |
| Token counting | provider-reported usage primary; `pkoukk/tiktoken-go` only if local estimates needed | Local estimates cost RAM |
| Markdown render (TUI) | `charmbracelet/glamour` (lazy-loaded) or minimal custom renderer | pi renders minimal markdown |
| Testing | stdlib + `stretchr/testify` for table-driven provider tests | Match Go conventions |
| Fuzzing | `go test -fuzz` on session loader + JSON repair | Loader parses untrusted files |
| Memory proof | `runtime.MemStats` smoke harness; `GODEBUG=gctrace=1` in CI | Budget verification per §3.7 |

Feasibility verdict (research Part V): every subsystem omp implements has a viable CGO-free Go counterpart; nothing requires a JS runtime. Top risks and mitigations: TUI fidelity (IME, wide chars, resize) → frame-plan discipline + tests against real terminals (Ghostty/iTerm/alacritty/kitty); provider quirk coverage → pi's quirk tables + cross-provider context handoff tests; edit-tool UX parity → the omp-session acceptance test in §3.2.

### 3.9 Reliability requirements (ported from omp, research IV.9)

1. Append-only JSONL + leaf pointer; no destructive edits.
2. Latched persistence errors; fail-closed indeterminate-write handling; atomic stage-then-rename rewrites.

8. **Streaming tool execution must not widen the surface.** If read-only tools gain the ability to run during the stream (the ZCode delta in §3.4), the gate is the declared capability, never a name list: a tool that has not declared `readOnly`/`concurrentSafe`/`sideEffectScope: none` does not qualify, and the failure path falls back to end-of-stream execution rather than failing the turn.
9. **A stream read must be abortable even when the provider is wedged.** xdev's watchdog judges liveness from the *source* side (correct, #283) but the agent goroutine blocked in `for ev := range ch` has nothing to race when the turn is cancelled; ZCode races the pending read against three promises — `iterator.next()`, an idle-timeout timer, and an abort promise built from the turn signal (`adapters/src/model/stream-idle-timeout.ts`) — and escalates the idle window by `30 s × retryNumber`. Both properties are wanted at once.
3. `session_exit` + `tool_execution_start` forensics; synthetic aborted assistant message on resume.
4. Auto-retry with backoff + provider failover + context promotion before compaction.
5. Stream watchdogs (first-progress + idle); bounded provider-owned retries; capability fallbacks.
6. Memory limit as process backstop, so a runaway turn degrades into "compact now".
7. Supply-chain: pin deps exactly, `govulncheck` in CI, single-binary releases with checksums.

| M5 | Compaction (threshold + overflow + promotion + method ladder), retry/failover + partial-stream turn recovery | 200k-token sessions survive | **Complete (2026-09-11)** — MVP (2026-09-10) + tails (b4f9d80); residuals tracked in #6 (methodOrder knob, windowed materialization, memory-pressure trigger) — typed `ai.HTTPError` + err-class taxonomy (auth/transient/overflow/bad-request) at the wire source; transient pre-content retry ladder (500ms→8s, 25% downward jitter, post-content errors never replayed); threshold compaction (reserve 16384/15% floor, keepRecent 20000, chars/4 estimate floor, provider-usage override) + overflow→force-compact→retry-once; `buildContext` emits `EntryIDs` so compaction anchors `firstKeptEntryId` store-side; steering messages persisted (no rebuild loss). Tails complete (2026-09-11, b4f9d80): model-promotion ladder (overflow promotes to the smallest strictly-larger window, one step per overflow; compaction only at the top), multi-provider failover chain (`Agent.Failovers` from models.yml, window-desc; drained retry ladder fails over to the next target, model_change persisted), partial-stream retain+continue (post-content transient retains the text/thinking partial, persists it + a continuation prompt, resumes once) | [#6](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/6) |
| M6 | RPC mode (wire-compatible-ish) + subagents (yield contract) + MCP client + deferred tool catalog | embedders can drive it | **Complete (2026-09-11)** — RPC mode shipped (9db3a7d): `xdev rpc` JSONL-over-stdio with ready handshake (protocol 1, 1 MiB frames), seven id-correlated commands, event streaming with the materialized done message, bounded concurrent dispatch; hermetic pipe tests + external-driver smoke (prompt→events→exact reply, steer/abort/state round-trips). Subagents shipped (b393086): `SpawnChild` yield contract + `task` tool (restricted tools, outputSchema permissive/strict, artifact handoff), yield-only isolation test-pinned, child sessions isolated by `titleSource:subagent` + a dedicated subtree so resume never adopts one; live-verified delegation. MCP client shipped (dc721c0): official Go SDK, mcp.yml (stdio/HTTP, filters, per-server disable), per-server failure isolation, `<server>_<tool>` namespacing, 120s call cap, results windowed through the 32KB sink; parent-registry only. Residuals (non-blocking, named): host-tool calls + extension-UI dialogs (explicitly v2 frames) and the deferred tool catalog (its demand-driven members are M13's scope) | [#7](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/7) |
| M7 | Ext subprocess protocol + FS-scan cache + AST shell-out | parity with omp daily workflow | **Complete (2026-09-12)** — the M7 riders closed: ext runtime actions now route `steer`/`aside`/`followUp` (aside labeled through the follow-up channel) and `register_provider` validates a models.yml-shaped payload into the M9 provider registry, resolving through buildProvider (httptest-proven). Everything else (protocol handshake, fail-closed per-call interception + retirement, per-event timeout+SIGKILL, declarative renderer specs, extension commands, FS-scan cache, grep/glob fast paths, AST shell-out, editor completion) was already consumed; issue #8 closed. | [#8](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/8) |
| M8 | Memory hardening audit, fuzzing, cross-platform builds, packaging | <100 MB RSS verified under worst-case transcript | **Audited + fixed (2026-09-11, ce8fcae)** — measured: 109 MB / 50k-entry session loaded to 130 MB heap (over budget). Fix: **windowed materialization** — entries before the latest reset/compaction boundary are dropped at load (indexes rebuilt in batches; file untouched) → 62 MB for a 104 MB transcript, pinned by `TestWorstCaseTranscriptStaysUnderBudget`. Fuzzers for the session loader and the JSON-repair parser (87k / 5.8M execs clean) + 20s fuzz in CI; `govulncheck` pinned (0 called vulns); release-artifacts job (6 static platforms + SHA256SUMS). **Complete (2026-09-11)** — every box closed: windowed materialization (62 MB measured for a 104 MB worst-case transcript, budget asserted), two fuzzers (87k/5.8M execs clean, 20s each in CI), govulncheck pinned (0 called vulns; CI toolchain bumped to 1.25.14 over stdlib CVEs), six-platform CGO-free cross builds at 10.1 MB stripped, release-artifacts with SHA256SUMS, a cross-provider handoff contract test (failover to a different transport carries the whole chain + follows the new model), gctrace memory proof in CI, and three reference docs (session format, extension protocol, container/sandbox) that were validated against the code — the extension example exposed a real bug (policy-only extensions were silently discarded) which is now fixed and regression-tested. **Landed 2026-09-13, live-verification wave (`3b5e8e8`→`1a554ba`):**

- **Live collab verified end to end** — host plus a view-only guest and a
  full-control guest joined over real sockets: both links print unwrapped,
  guests mirror the host's turns as they stream (`▸ What is 12*9?` then `108`
  appeared in the replica), view-only guests state their limits and their typed
  lines are dropped, `/collab status` reports room + guest count, `/collab stop`
  tears it down. Fixed: `xdev join <full-link>` opened by announcing itself
  *view-only* and contradicted itself two lines later, because the banner read
  the first welcome frame — which must say `Writable:false` for every guest,
  since the write token rides `FrameHello` after the handshake (URL fragments
  are never sent over HTTP). `Link.Full()`, parsed locally, is the authority.
- **The host `ask` relay is documented rather than half-wired** (#99): both
  ends exist and neither is called — `Host.RequestUI` (`host.go:267`) and
  `Backend.UIResponse` (`host.go:31`, consumed at `414`) have zero callers, while
  the frames and `Guest.UIResponse` work at the protocol level. The missing
  third piece is input arbitration on the guest (its stdin lines *are* prompts),
  which is a decision, not a wire — filed with line numbers instead of shipping
  a relay that swallows guest input.
- **`ai-title` now fires on a default install**: the model cascade reached
  nothing without configured roles (there is no built-in mapping), so every
  session kept its `print <timestamp>` title. The title request now runs on the
  session's own model; verified live with no roles configured
  (`add git remote origin2`).
- **17-subcommand CLI sweep** (empty store and populated): `gallery render
  memory worktree plugin completions stats usage share gc bench update setup say
  config` all return sane exit codes with actionable messages and no panics;
  `render --html`, `share`, `stats --json`, `search --json` checked against real
  sessions.
- **Two crashes only live driving found**, both invisible to the suite:
  `lsp code_actions` asserted a `Position` struct as a JSON map and panicked on
  its first real request; `Manager.clientAt` shared one cap-1 result channel
  among concurrent waiters, so the second waiter dereferenced nil when two lsp
  calls raced the first gopls launch. Each has a test that fails against the old
  code.
- **Process lesson recorded**: a build break reached `main` when a chain used
  `;` before the commit (`cat >>` had *created* a file holding only a
  placeholder test, and removing the placeholder left a zero-byte `.go` file —
  a parse error). The verifier printed the failure and the commit ran anyway.
  Now: `&&` only, verifier last in its own call, and the pushed HEAD proven
  buildable from a clean `git worktree` before installing.

**Fixed 2026-09-13 — "the whole TUI freezes" after a failed command (5b999f0).**
The tree selector could own the keyboard while painting nothing: `handleTreeKey`
returned handled for every key and had no `Ctrl+C` case, and two routes left it
open with no panel drawn (a filter/search matching no row, and a panel with no
room above a tall composer). A modal trap that reproduces the reported symptom
exactly — usage error empties the composer → Esc opens the selector → every key
including the quit chord is swallowed → process alive, terminal dead. Recorded as
symptom-matching, not as an observed trace of the user's own session: the
selector declines to open with zero entries, so their instance also needed a
filter/room route to go invisible, and the watchdog below is what names any
recurrence definitively. Guards: the quit chord is never modal-owned, and
open implies painted (an empty result set renders "no rows match · Ctrl+O
changes filter · …"; a panel with no room closes the selector). Each guard has a
test that fails against the specific line it guards; the fixed behavior is
verified live (empty-result panel paints with its way out, Ctrl+C exits 0).
Related: the UI loop now heartbeats and writes `dumps/tui-stall-<ts>.txt`
(goroutine stacks, mode 0600, collected by `xdev gc`) if one iteration exceeds
5s, so any future stall diagnoses itself instead of being unreproducible
(c3985bc).

**Fixed 2026-09-29 — a dead tty left the UI alive and deaf instead of ending it.**
A terminal read error is terminal, and tcell already says so: its `inputLoop`
posts one `EventError` and then **returns** (`tscreen.go:1041-1062`), so nothing
is ever read from the tty again. `App.Run` could not see that. The poll goroutine
exits only on a nil event, and tcell returns nil solely from `StopQ`
(`screen.go:510`), so it parked on a terminal that would never produce another
event — and had the event been delivered, `handleKey` would have dropped it
anyway, since it only acts on keys, resizes and mouse events. The UI therefore
stayed alive, painting and heartbeating (which is why the stall watchdog stayed
quiet) with a tty in raw mode and the alt screen up, and **no key, resize or
quit chord could do anything**. The only goroutine that could read the terminal
was the one that had just died, so nothing inside the process could recover.
The fix is one branch where the event is polled: an `*tcell.EventError` is
logged and ends the loop, which returns through `runTUI` so `defer scr.Fini()`
puts the terminal back and the user relaunches. Staying alive would be a zombie
on a dead terminal. Guarded both ways by `internal/tui/input_error_test.go`:
`TestTtyReadErrorEndsTheUILoopInsteadOfWedgingIt` fails against the absent
branch, and `TestOnlyTheReadErrorEndsTheUILoop` (a resize plus two keystrokes
must still land in the composer) fails if the check ever widens past the error.

**Fixed 2026-09-30 — a wedged UI loop was an un-exitable session (session 00b1c5a0).**
The stall watchdog above names a stuck loop but leaves the user inside it, and
the quit chord is no escape: `handleKey` runs ON that loop, so a loop blocked
inside `scr.Show()` — tcell's synchronous tty write, which parks whenever the
terminal stops draining it (a frozen or suspended pane) — reads no key at all.
Thirteen dumps from 2026-09-30 name it, the worst blocking 17m04s in
`devTty.Write` (`tui-stall-20260930-202555.txt`); the user's report was
verbatim: Ctrl+C and Ctrl+D did nothing and the process had to be killed. Two
halves, because the chord and the timeout fail for different reasons. The
chord: the poll goroutine that feeds `keyq` now serves `quit` itself when the
watchdog has marked the loop wedged (`app.go`), so the key is honoured by the
one goroutine that still receives it — the loop comes back, sees `quitCh` closed
and returns, and `runTUI`'s defers restore the terminal. Every other key is left
to the loop, and Esc stays cancel-only, so a wedged session cannot turn "stop
this turn" into "exit". The timeout: a loop still wedged ten minutes after its
first dump restores the terminal and exits non-zero (`stall.go`,
`SetStallExitAfter`), because nothing inside the process can — the turn
goroutine, the MCP servers and the tool workers are all in that pid. The clock
is per episode, so a loop that recovers is never taken away, and the 0 value
keeps every other embedder on the old behaviour. Five tests guard it
(`wedged_quit_test.go`): the chord quits a wedged loop; Esc and a rune do not; a
healthy loop keeps the chord to itself; a never-recovering loop exits AND
restores; a recovered one is reported and left alone.
**Residual wiring debt — re-audited 2026-09-13 (full-tree consumer-proof pass; every row below carries a ticket):**

Reconciliation audit method: 10 domain fan-outs re-verified every closed-sweep claim against the tree @ 4ed9920 with the "landed = has a production caller" bar; 34 follow-up issues filed (#79–#112), correction comments posted on the affected closed issues, and three stale closeout/docs claims corrected (hindsight AutoRecall IS live; TTSR `condition` IS consumed; hub processes ARE reaped — the goal-budget "lag" is only the documented crash-window). The high-severity finds:
**Tightened 2026-10-02 — the give-up clock outlived its own use (sessions `b5884a98`, `0bba44f7`; 135 dumps).** The 2026-09-30 fix set the wedged-loop exit at ten minutes on the reasoning that it sits "far past every measured stall". Its own evidence said otherwise: of the 135 dumps `~/.xdev/agent/dumps/` holds, 20 name the loop inside `drawText`/the tcell write path and the worst blocking `syscall.write` is **17m04s** (`tui-stall-20260930-202555.txt`) — every one of which ended with a kill from outside, which is what the timeout was supposed to prevent. Two mechanisms were broken, both invisible from the transcript:

1. **Prompt-injection scanning** (#81): the §5 decision is unimplemented — no scanner exists in the Go code.
2. **Launch flags with no consumer** (#105): after 48459fe only `--external-thinking`, `--service-tier`, `--provider-session-id`, `--prompt-cache-key` remain genuinely unregistered (the 2026-09-12 list claiming `--allow-home/--add-dir/--no-pty/--plugin-dir` among them is stale — all four landed with consumers; `--no-pty` is a documented intentional no-op).
3. **JS eval kernel** (#111): stays a by-design rejection (no JS runtime CGO-free); the py kernel and notebook virtual text shipped — but omp's `eval.py`/`eval.js` disable gates have no equivalent.
4. **Chrome extension for browser-relay** (#112): the Go relay daemon shipped; the browser-side extension did not.
5. **M4 structural frame-plan tail** (#78): all nine boxes re-verified open 2026-09-13 (frame-plan contract, block states, DSR resize, alt-buffer, sticky headers/folds/diff rows/highlighting, bracketed paste, compact toggle, hex verification, terminal matrix).
6. **TTSR injection persistence** (#113): the §2 scope row for TTSR names
   `ttsr_injection` persistence (a session entry recording an injected
   rule-violation notice); no such entry type exists. `ttsr_injection` and
   `Violations` have zero hits in Go, `internal/session/entries.go` enumerates
   xdev's ten entry types without it, and the code states the alternative
   outright at `cmd/xdev/print.go:544`: *"fired state is in-session only, never
   persisted"*. So M11's "Complete" covers the engine (regex + `astCondition` +
   per-kind `interruptMode` gating + the thinking lane), not this record.
   Adding it means reversing that in-session-only decision on purpose (new
   entry type, retention, cross-process read) — hence #113, split out of #35,
   whose closeout comment now states the engine/arming landed and this pair
   did not. (#95 covers a different TTSR slice: scope/globs + notice attrs.)
1. **The clock was a starvation timer measured against a starved process.** `tui-stall-20261002-100308.txt` shows the UI loop at `goroutine 1 [runnable]` in `drawText` and the tool goroutine at `goroutine 2031 [runnable]` in `syscall.forkExec` — *runnable, not blocked*. That is CPU starvation, not a deadlock: load average 8.36 with 674 processes, 7 concurrent `xdev` sessions, and swap 2534M/3072M used. The watchdog is another goroutine on the same starved process, so the ten minutes it needs to elapse are ten minutes of the same scarcity the user was staring at. Session `b5884a98` shows the cost per hang in `toolResult` rows: two `bash` calls and one `grep` on 1–6ms commands, each `duration: 600001` — `DefaultToolTimeout` (`loop.go`) exactly — and a 2-minute bash timeout returning **9 minutes late**.
2. **The escape hatch was itself blocked on the thing it was escaping.** `exitWedgedLoop` called `a.restoreTty()` (`scr.Fini`) inline, and `scr.Fini` writes to the same wedged fd the loop is parked in. A restore that never returns never reaches `exitProcess`, so the last resort was not one. The restore now runs on its own goroutine behind a 2s bound and the exit proceeds regardless.

**Build/portability ceiling (measured 2026-09-13):** the contract is the six CI
targets (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:104-109`: darwin/linux/windows × amd64/arm64,
all 64-bit) plus `linux/386` as a deliberate 32-bit extra — which is exactly
what caught `internal/theme` initializing an `int` sentinel with `1 << 32`, a
compile error wherever `int` is 32 bits (fixed in 6f53965). Sweeping wider
produces failures that are not xdev's: `linux/arm`, `windows/arm` and similar
die inside `internal/memory → modernc.org/sqlite → modernc.org/libc` with
"build constraints exclude all Go files", i.e. **xdev's platform ceiling is
whatever `modernc/libc` supports**, not `go tool dist list`. Recorded so a
future sweep reads those as the known ceiling rather than new breakage.
Net: `SetStallExitAfter` 10m → **90s**, and the restore is bounded. 90s still covers every measured self-recovering case (the 45s/60s frozen writes #20 measured were a paste the user still held) while ending a session the user has already written off, so `--resume` picks the transcript back up instead of the pane being killed from outside. The other `10m` bound is untouched on purpose: `DefaultToolTimeout` is per-tool and `task` legitimately runs longer, and shortening it would abandon real work — the bounded restore plus the shorter exit is what makes the TUI survivable without that risk. Three tests (`stall_restore_test.go`): a hanging restore still exits (fails on the old code in 7s); a restore that returns still runs before the exit; the dump survives the give-up path. *Last updated: 2026-10-02.*
**Residual wiring debt — re-audited 2026-09-13 (full-tree consumer-proof pass; every row below carries a ticket):**

**Landed 2026-09-15 (resume line on TUI exit, user-requested; the one hermes
copy the research doc called cheap UX):** `runTUI` registers its exit defer
*before* the store-close and `scr.Fini` defers, so LIFO order prints after the
alt screen is released — anything written earlier is wiped by Fini. The line is
the bare, copy-pasteable command (`resumeHint`/`resumeCommand`/`resumeProg`,
`cmd/xdev/sessionops.go`): the invoked name (argv0 basename, `.exe` trimmed,
`xdev` when argv0 is unusable) so a shell that installs the binary as `omp` gets
a line that pastes back, then `--resume <session uuid>`. It is printed only when
the target is reachable: `resolveResumeID(cwd, store.ID())` must land on the
store's own path, which is what turns "--no-session", a chat that never
materialized, a `/drop`ped file, and a store resumed from another directory into
silence instead of a command that errors. The defer reads `store` at exit, so
/new, /fork and /resume change what the line names. Print/RPC/ACP modes stay
byte-clean.
**Landed 2026-09-15 (top bar is permanent, user-requested):** the welcome screen's top bar (`❯ branch` left — the working directory is deliberately not shown: the status row carries it — model right, grok top_bar.rs) now also owns row 0 of the transcript view and carries the **last user prompt** collapsed to one line (`· <first line>`, whitespace/tabs sanitized, clipped with `…`), so the request the session is answering never scrolls away. Narrow windows shed the model first, then clip the prompt; the transcript viewport starts below the bar (`transcriptTop`), `viewportLinesLocked` accounts for it, and the selection geometry (doc anchoring, edge auto-scroll, row capture vs grid fallback) converts screen↔viewport rows by the same offset. A press on the bar is a chrome gesture — the bar itself is copyable. Regression tests: `TestTopBarCarriesBranchAndLastPrompt`; the selection/scroll-hint suites pin the shifted layout.

The high-severity finds:

**Landed 2026-09-14 (#244, goal continuation — the goal actually runs):** `/goal create|resume` starts the goal's first turn (the objective becomes the session title and the first `❯` prompt) and an active goal keeps the run going from there: a turn that yields with no tool calls re-enters with the hidden `goal-continuation` prompt instead of ending the run, so the objective is worked on without a user nudge. It is **TUI-only** — omp's `goal.continuationModes` defaults to `interactive`, and a print/RPC/ACP run must not spend turns of its own after its prompt is answered — and the loop ends when the goal is completed, dropped, budget-exhausted, or the run's turn budget is spent (Esc cancels the turn). Verified live: `/goal create "…NO tool calls as your first message, then create … and mark the goal complete"` → assistant plan-only message → `message user attribution=goal-continuation` in the session JSONL → work → `goal_updated completed` → idle. Residual (still omp-divergent, tracked in #244): no `/goal pause`/`budget` verbs, no mid-turn accounting flush, and the continuation mode set is not configurable.

**Known gaps — resolved (2026-09-10, commit c96f3f2):**
1. **`/<command>` slash commands now work** (M10 #11 first slice): built-in registry (`/new` `/clear` `/drop` `/help` `/quit` + `/q`) routed at input-submit before any user block is created; markdown commands discovered from `<cwd>/.xdev/commands/*.md` + `~/.xdev/agent/commands/*.md` (project beats user, non-recursive, hidden/non-md skipped) with frontmatter `name:`/`description:`, quote-aware `$1..$n`/`$@`/`$@[start]`/`$@[start:length]`/`$ARGUMENTS` expansion and the no-placeholder blank-line append fallback; unknown `/foo` still falls through as literal prompt text. Lifecycle wired to the session store: `/new` swaps a fresh session file, `/clear` appends a durable `reset_boundary` and resets in place, `/drop` deletes the file and starts fresh — all refuse while a turn is in flight. Remaining M10 scope (fork/resume/picker, context files, keybindings, /dump) unchanged.
2. **TUI scrollback implemented** (P0 #17): a tail-anchored viewport scroll model (`internal/tui/scroll.go`) — offset counts lines above the live tail, follow semantics pin streaming output to the tail, scrolled position survives appends/shrinks, clamped at both ends. Keys: PgUp/PgDn, Ctrl+B/F, arrows (when not running), Home/End jump to top/live; `▲ n ▼ n` indicator when content hides. Contract-tested (scroll model table) plus an integration test through the real key path. Fold/expand of tool blocks remains M4 tail scope.
   - **omp-parity (2026-09-14, e326f6b):** the tail-anchored model gained a proportional right-edge `│` track / `█` thumb (`scrollModel.Scrollbar`, thumb `⌊vp·vp/total⌋`) painted over a reserved last column, so position feedback is continuous rather than the `▲ n ▼ n` text hint alone (the hint stays on the composer divider); PgUp/PgDn/Ctrl+B/F now step a full viewport−1 (`scrollPage`) instead of half a screen, matching omp's `ScrollView.page`; and finished tool results render framed in a rounded box (`╭─ name · state (dur) ─╮ … ╰─╯`, `toolBoxLines`) instead of `┃`-railed lines under a `↳ ok (dur)` header — errors tint the whole frame, the head+tail row window and elision marker carry inside, and the boxed block drops its leading rail.
   - **Jump-to-latest chip (2026-09-29, `feat/jump-to-latest`):** scrolling up in a live session showed only the `▲ n ▼ n` counter on the composer divider — a readout, not a control, so catching up after a long stream meant mashing Ctrl+F blind. `paint()` now paints a `↓ n new` pill over the transcript's bottom-right corner whenever rows are hidden below the viewport (`drawJumpChip`, `App.jump` publishes the rectangle), and the press branch in `handleMouse` claims it exactly like the scrollbar does: the gesture spends itself on the button and never anchors a selection on the row behind it. The count is the scroll model's own hidden-row total, so the chip appears and disappears with the tail (nothing hidden → no button, and the cells it would have covered stay selectable) and the rect is cleared every frame, so a chip that is not on screen cannot be clicked. Placed on the transcript's own edge, not the terminal's, so it stays with the content when the context dock is open. Mouse-only by design: `End` already jumps to the tail, so a new chord would have added `/hotkeys` churn for no reachable feature. Tests: `internal/tui/jumpchip_test.go` (count, click, no-chip-at-the-tail).
3. **Agent turn budget graceful** (P0 #18): `MaxTurns` const → `DefaultMaxTurns = 200` + `Agent.MaxTurns` field (0 = default) + `-max-turns` flag (0 = default). At the cap the agent persists a synthetic user wrap-up message ("turn budget reached — wrap up…") and runs exactly one final turn whose tool calls are deliberately left dangling (neutralized on rebuild); the run ends nil-error with a normal assistant status report instead of the fatal `exceeded N turns`. Runaway-loop protection intact (cap applies per run; unit tests cover wrap-up, default resolution, and bounded runaway loops).
4. **Tool results print in full** (P0, 2026-09-10): the TUI no longer collapses tool output to a 200-char one-line preview — the tool-result block renders the complete sink-windowed output the model sees (bash: 16KB head+tail per stream), wrapped, inside a rounded box whose footer carries the outcome the user needs (`⟦Wall: 70ms | Exit: 9 | output truncated⟧`, error tinted), and whose hidden rows carry an *actionable* `… N lines hidden (Ctrl+O to expand)` affordance instead of the unreachable `(log)` marker of the original. The call row above names the call as the tool and its primary argument, never the raw JSON the model sent — and (2026-09-30, `fix/tool-call-wrap`) it no longer clips that argument to the row width: a command longer than the row WRAPS onto continuation rows, and `toolDetail` no longer stops the phrase at 400 bytes. See the footer stamp for the full change. Error results moved from the system-block path into the tool-result block so their output prints too. Ctrl+O expands one result at a time to its full (sink-bounded) length; print mode emits `res.Text` after the footer — headless runs no longer lose bash output. Framed output is sanitized omp-parity first (2026-09-15): `sanitizeOutput` expands tabs to the 3-cell omp stop and drops ANSI/control sequences before any width math — a tab measures 0 cells but paints as an advance to the next 8-column stop, so unsanitized tab-indented output landed every right border of the box elsewhere. Regression tests: `TestToolCallRowShowsNamedArgument`, `TestToolResultRendersBox`, `TestToolResultExitCodeSitsInTheFooter`, `TestToolResultRowWindow`, `TestToolResultBoxAlignsTabbedOutput`.

5. **Thinking output renders + `/settings` toggle** (2026-09-11, #20): the wire layer always parsed reasoning (unified `thinking_start/delta/end` events from all four adapters), but the transcript only ever showed a collapsed "Thought for Xs" header — the reasoning text itself was never rendered and there was no way to turn any of it off. Now: `showThinking` settings key (defaults ← global ← project ← `--config`, `*bool` so an explicit false survives the zero-skip merge; `xdev config list|get|set|reset` honors it) drives display — TUI renders the reasoning in the same rounded frame a finished result gets (`theme.AccentThinking` border, `gray_dim` body, state in the top border) showing a fixed `thinkBoxRows = 12`-row window that the mouse wheel scrolls once a left click has focused the box (`Block.ThinkOff`, rows counted up from the newest thought; the wheel is the transcript's until then) and Ctrl+O drops entirely for that box — no tier-based head+tail trim, so reasoning reads the same at every tier (full text stays in the session JSONL), print mode gates its stderr reasoning stream, and `/settings` (bare) lists the resolved config while `/settings showThinking [on|off|flip]` toggles for the session AND persists to the global layer; turning it off drops existing thinking blocks so the transcript matches a session started with it off. Same commit fixed multi-line system notices (`/help` `/model` `/settings` lists) which embedded `\n` collapsed into one unreadable row — now one row per line.
