- [docs/research/2026-09-15-xdev-slow-session-rca.md](research/2026-09-15-xdev-slow-session-rca.md) — measured root-cause analysis of the user-reported "xdev costs more turns and runs longer than omp": UI-loop stalls kill healthy streams (watchdog) and inject continuation turns; pre-#133 cache gap; post-header `ttft`/`duration` semantics hide queue latency; no artifact offload makes per-step context heavier.
- [docs/research/zcode-internals.md](research/zcode-internals.md) — ZCode (`872ad96`) primary-source cross-check: sectioned `ContextBuilder` with declared cache hints; tool capability manifests read by four consumers; streaming execution of read-only tools; plan mode as a permission policy over declared capability; three-tier compaction (microcompact / auto-compact with a breaker / the boundary ladder); a session **residency pool** (idle TTL + high-water LRU + eligibility facts re-read before eviction); permission responders racing a hook chain against the interactive broker; and an explicit ranked adopt/verify/reject list vs xdev.
- [docs/research/deepseek-harness-deepdive.md](research/deepseek-harness-deepdive.md) — DeepSeek Harness (`21638c5631`, 2,612 commits past the 2026-09-14 study) re-read across eight dimensions — session core, context/window, subagents, tool calls, lifecycle, thinking flow, action flows, UI/transport — with `path:line` on both sides. Findings that change a stated assumption: **xdev has no turn/step boundary at all** (dsh's `turn/end` + merge-extensible `TurnEndReason` is the precondition for crash repair, max-tokens-as-turn-reason and a real status read); **dsh's confinement is OS-level** (Seatbelt/Landlock/bwrap/Windows-ACL behind one canonical writable-root derivation) where xdev's is policy-only; **dsh ships no terminal UI at all** (React SPA + Electron, no `ink`/`@opentui` dependency anywhere), so the two front-ends are not comparable products; **dsh's tool registry is type-inferred and schema-validating** where xdev's is hand-authored `json.RawMessage` with no registry-side arg validation; **xdev's `ConcurrentSafe` table is recorded but never consulted by the executor**. Five ports proposed in dependency order (§9.2): turn/step lifecycle → crash repair → durable `request/header` → sandbox + canonical roots → system-prompt registry. Also corrects four stale claims in [research/dsh-internals.md](research/dsh-internals.md) (§0.2).

---
*Last updated: 2026-09-24 (local Laya compatibility for the typesafe tool):* the `typesafe` tool now accepts a self-hosted Laya `/v1/systemone` endpoint through `TYPESAFE_BASE_URL` or `typesafe.baseUrl`; local auth is optional via `LAYA_API_KEY` or `typesafe.apiKey`. Hosted TypeSafe remains the default. Verified with `go test ./internal/typesafe ./internal/config` and `go vet`; the full suite still has two unrelated pre-existing `cmd/xdev` failures (`TestConnectPickerItems`, `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills`).*
*Last updated: the `│/█` bar is painted and ignored the painted one, so the drag a human makes did nothing. Same stale-key bug the unmerged `d41de09` fixed — the painter had moved the bar to `rightEdge()-1` with the context dock (#291) while `selBarAt` still tested `a.width-1`. That fix is rebased onto `origin/main` and re-verified live: the painted column now grabs and drags (the press takes the grip, the drag scrolls the transcript), and the panel's own border answers nothing. `selBarW` (a boolean in disguise) is gone: the painter publishes `selBarX`, the column it actually drew in, and `selBarAt` tests that. Regression: `TestScrollbarDragStillAnswersWithTheDockOpen` — a 140-column app with the panel open grabs the thumb at `rightEdge()-1`, drags it to the top of the track, and asserts the panel's border stays the panel's.
*Last updated: 2026-09-21 (junk reasoning is no longer painted in the thinking box — branch `fix/junk-reasoning`):* the box used to render whatever reasoning arrived, so a leg whose decode fell apart filled the transcript with symbol soup (`… 196 rows hidden` of it) while the turn produced no answer. `internal/tui/junk.go` now judges the body with the SAME measured rule onegw's `translat.JunkGuard` uses — `wordR < 0.92 && badR >= 0.25`, `badR >= 0.40 && symPer100 >= 10`, or `meanTokenLetters < 2.6 && symPer100 >= 10`, over a body of at least 600 bytes — and `thinkBoxLines` replaces a junk body with one dim notice row inside the same frame (`reasoning discarded · the model produced unreadable output`). The thresholds were fitted against the session corpus (45,017 reasoning blocks from 435 sessions: the rule fires on 5 of 25,000 sampled blocks ≥ 400 bytes, all five genuine junk, while English, Mandarin, Vietnamese, Go source, commit-hash analysis and tables stay clean); the deliberate ceiling — junk whose letters still sit in word-like runs while its tokens mix scripts measures like bilingual reasoning — is documented on `junkThinking` and pinned by `TestJunkThinkingCeiling`. Gating is a VIEW, never a truncation: the block keeps the full text for the session JSONL and the copy path, and the wire-level half (invalid UTF-8 → `ErrMalformedStream`, `CleanUTF8`, `sanitizeOutput`) is unchanged. The onegw side of the same report (a junk leg is failed over before its first client byte and the model re-called with the same context) shipped as onegw PR #138.

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (user-requested "show the tool call like opencode — tool call - new - cache - total in the status line, click to see all history" — branch `feat/toolcall-stats`, committed 597528d): the status HUD now accounts prompt-cache tokens and shows a tool-call summary. `AddUsage(in, out, cacheRead, cacheWrite, total)` folds cacheRead/CacheWrite into `Status` (previously dropped); the `cache` segment renders `cache % · N` (cached share of the billed prompt side, `cacheHitPercent`, shared with dsh's rounding rule; then absolute cached input), so `ctx 92k/200k` reads far too low on a cached conversation no more. A tool-call ledger sits underneath: `ToolCallEntry` (id, name, status, in/out/cacheRead/cacheWrite/total/costUSD) + `ToolCallStats` (in-flight, totals, ledger), `ToolCallStart`/`End`/`Result` events threaded through `cmd/xdev/tui.go` `OnEvent`, `Status.Calls()`/`Calls()`/`RunningToolCalls()`/`AddToolCall*` on App, `App.Totals()` for the stats command — unreferenced until the next step wires it into the status line or a click-to-expand dialog. Also fixed the real `drawStatusRow`/`drawHUD` contract bug (was: three separate " | " measurements; wide rows overdraw the path with a segment separator, narrow rows hide segments the row could hold): the path now stops at `a.width - hudWidth(parts) - 2`, one shared `hudWidth` and one quoted `hudSep` constant.

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (context dock, second pass — `feat/sidebar-crush`): the panel stopped being a box and became opencode's sidebar. `drawDock` no longer paints `│`/`─` chrome: it fills the panel's own columns with `theme.BgBase` and keeps a two-cell gutter (`dockCols` 42, new `dockPad` 2, so the interior is the same 38 columns as opencode's), because a border around a column that already owns its background is a line of chrome the interior pays two columns for. **The slot the border freed carries the session's own name** — the box's old `CONTEXT · N sections` heading died with the box, and `DockOps.Session` (a closure, like every other source) supplies the live title, its short id as fallback, and the harness id for a caller that wired nothing; cmd reads it through the `store` variable so `/new`, `/resume` and `/fork` follow, and `DockBump` from `generateTitle`'s completion and from `RenameSession` is what repaints it. Section headings now split into a bold name and the dim count their source appended (`dockSplit`: `TASKS · 1/2 done`), changed-file rows carry their `+N`/`-N` right-aligned at the panel's edge in the diff's own inks, and paths are cut from the left (`dockPath`, `runewidth.TruncatePrefix`) so it is a directory that gets the ellipsis and two `app.go` in different trees stay distinguishable. `dockRow` replaced the `[]string` row model so a row can carry its own counts and heading flag; the dead `dockGap`/`dockAppend`/`head`/`top` fields went with it. Regression cover: `TestDockAnatomyIsOpencode` (title slot, every heading splits, counts sit at the panel's right edge) and the tightened `TestDockPaintsThePanel`, which now asserts no box glyph appears anywhere in the panel's columns and that the old heading is gone.

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (seventh mouse leg: copying a boxed row carried its frame — the copy half of [#315](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/315)). **A selection copies cells, and the cells of a box include its frame.** Drag over a finished tool result and `╭─ bash ───╮` plus the `│ ` rails went to the clipboard with the output, because the highlight and the copy read the same row and a frame painted as a run is a frame copied. The row model gained a third field — `cell.chrome`, a run that is painted and never copied — and the frame builders (`boxTop`/`boxBottom`/`boxRow`, `toolBoxLines`) mark corners, rules, side borders, pad cells and the right-align fill with it; `paint()` now records the column its first content cell occupies (`startX = -1` until a non-chrome run) and skips chrome runs, so what is painted is what is copied, and a row that is chrome end to end (a bare `╰───╯`) copies as the blank line it looks like. A top rule's **label stays content**: `╭─ bash ───╮` copies as `bash`. The composer, the picker panel and the ask card paint their borders cell by cell rather than as runs, so they never see `cell.chrome` — `boxSelectable`/`ruleLabel` invert the frame glyph-wise for the grid-readback rows and move the copy's start column with every dropped cell (the highlight measures from the same column, and stripping a border without moving it would shift every slice two cells left). Tests: `TestBoxSelectableStripsTheFrame` (the readback table, `x0` asserted) and `TestToolBoxCopyDropsTheFrame` (a real drag over a finished `bash` box copies `hello`/`world`/`bash` and no frame glyph). The wheel-over-a-box half of the branch name is deliberately not in this change.

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (user-reported "after scrolling a lot up and down in the transcript, typing in the composer lags before the character shows" — branch `fix/tui-scroll-end`): **the loop painted a frame per input event.** `Run()` drew once after every event in `keyq`, so a wheel flick (200 notches ≈ 296 `Show()` calls, each a synchronous tty write) queued its whole burst of frames ahead of the keystroke typed behind them, and the drain time is what the user felt. Nothing in the per-frame cost explained it: a keystroke measured a flat ~0.49 ms with the viewport at the tail, scrolled to the top, and after either wheel direction (14 536 rows), because the row index (§3.5) already made frames viewport-bound — the cost was purely *queueing* (`keyq` holds 64 events, and one notch applies in 60 µs while its frame paints in 491 µs on the simulation screen, 292 µs of that in `paint`, until a real tty flush is added on top). Fix: `drainKeys()` applies the input already queued before the single `draw()` each iteration, bounded at `keyBurst = 256` so a held key still reaches the screen; events stay ordered, so no chord or paste marker is dropped. Regression gate `TestScrollBurstCostsOneFrameNotOnePerEvent` drives the real `Run()` loop on a screen that charges each flush 1 ms and counts frames: **293–312 frames pre-fix → ≤24**, and it asserts the burst actually scrolled so an ignored wheel event cannot pass cheaply. The interactive alternative was tried and thrown away, not just skipped: tmux `send-keys` paces the burst (~160 ms for 600 notches), so both binaries measured the same and the harness was timing tmux.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (user-reported "the mcp server error in the chat textbox/notification should not show in the chat textbox, it should be a notification for ~2 min"). **stderr under the alt screen is not a notice, it is damage.** `finishMCP` wrote every failed server to `os.Stderr`; with the TUI on the alt screen that paints over the composer, so the user read `xdev: mcp server unavailable — broken: fork/exec …` inside their own draft and again after quitting. The composer divider already had the channel — the slot a copy confirmation uses (`selNotice`/`selNoticeUntil`, drawn on the model's row and dropped by the tick's own redraw once the deadline passes) — and #272 had already moved discovery notices off stderr; MCP was left behind. `tui.App.SetNotice(text, d)` is the missing door for a caller off the UI thread (it takes `a.mu` and pokes; the UI-thread `setNotice` beside it stays lock-free), `attachMCP`/`finishMCP` grew an optional `report func(string)` sink (nil = stderr, so print/rpc/acp are untouched), and the TUI now attaches MCP once the App exists, reporting for `mcpNoticeGrace = 2 * time.Minute` — **superseded**: that per-mode grace is what `fix/toast-autohide` removed, so the report now takes the toast stack's own error grace (the `~2 min` in the request above was never a lifetime the user had asked to keep, only the shortest way to read a stderr line the TUI could not show). The notice is short by necessity — the divider drops a hint wider than the room beside the model name, so the full fork/exec path would render as *nothing*; `mcpUnavailable` keeps the server name and mcpclient still logs the whole error under `-v`. Verified on the real binary in tmux against a server that exits 1 after 4 s: composer clean, `mcp: broken unavailable` on the divider, `stderr.log` empty, notice gone after the window.

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (user-reported "fix session list only 10 items, i want them load all, lazy load when scroll down, and show lenght of session name base on the width of the terminal size" — branch `fix/resume-picker-load-all`): the `/resume` list was never short, it was **cut**. The no-argument picker's rows come from `Recent` (`cmd/xdev/tui.go`), which built `ResumeOption`s in a `make([]tui.ResumeOption, 0, 12)` and `break`-ed at `len(out) >= 12` — so a folder with 241 resumable sessions showed twelve and no scroll reached the rest, because the rows after the twelfth had never been built. The cap is gone: `recentResumeOptions(cwd, currentID)` scans `session.List` once (it always did) and returns every session whose `CWD` matches, minus subagent children and the live session. The drawing side is why the cap looked structural: `drawPicker` sized its window from `p.visible`, seeded to `pickerMaxRows = 12` in `newPicker` and never grown, so even an uncapped list would have painted twelve rows. The window now follows the terminal — `pickerRowCeiling(a.height)` = `max(5, height/2)`, the budget the tree selector uses — and the existing `avail-chrome` clamp still shrinks it on a short screen; `p.visible = rows` still publishes the drawn height, so PgUp/PgDn page by what is on screen instead of a stale 12 (now `move(max(1, visible))`: a key can arrive before the first paint measures anything). Scrolling already worked (`window()` anchors the drawn slice to the selection) — it was scrolling through a list that stopped at twelve. The name column was the third half of the report: `labelW = min(labelW+2, 28)` spent a fixed 28 cells on the `id · date · status` detail regardless of terminal width, so a 120-column terminal ellipsized names it had room to print and a narrow one let the detail eat the row. It is now `min(labelW+2, max(12, inner-6-pickerDetailCols))` (`inner = w-6`; `pickerDetailCols = 28` is what the detail is worth keeping), the 12-cell floor keeping a name legible when a row is too narrow for both — the detail is what drops, not the thing being picked. Verified in tmux 3.7c at 120x40 on the real store: the footer counts `1/241 items`, PgDn and `↓` walk past row 12, and 260 `↓` land on `241/241`. Tests: `TestRecentResumeOptionsUncapped` (`cmd/xdev` — the real defect: 20 sessions in one cwd all come back, the live one and a foreign cwd excluded), `TestResumePickerWindowFollowsTerminalHeight` and `TestPickerNameColumnFollowsTerminalWidth` (`internal/tui`); the two picker tests fail on the pre-fix tree (`drawn rows = 12`) and the cmd test does not compile against it, which is what pins them as regression checks.


*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (a bare prefix finds a nested file — user-reported: "the `@` dropdown does not show all the file names in the current cwd, I have to type the full path"): the fix those two PRs traded against each other is now both. #314 scoped a token to ONE readdir so a keystroke cannot walk, and #329 deleted the whole-repo fallback because a bare `@` cost 5.7 s on a 226k-file tree — which left `@pa` able to see only the cwd's own entries, so `internal/tui/paths.go` was unreachable without typing its directories. `pathindex.go` moves that walk OFF the keystroke path instead of giving it up: one `filepath.WalkDir` of the cwd at wiring, on a goroutine, published as an `atomic.Pointer[[]pathHit]` sorted by lowercased base name. A token naming no directory appends the index's prefix run to the readdir's entries (root-level files are skipped — the readdir already offered them); a token that DOES name a directory is a drill and keeps the readdir alone. Measured on this polyrepo: 178k files indexed in 2.87 s and 44 MB of heap (down from 811k / 227 MB before `indexSkipDir` prunes `node_modules`, `.worktrees`, `dist`, `target`, …), and one keystroke is a binary search at 7.2 µs/op against the real 178k-file index — the linear scan it replaced cost 2.4 ms at 66k, so this is the part that keeps the index off the UI thread's critical path. The dropdown is correct before the walk lands (readdir only) and re-queries itself once it does, via one CAS the 33 ms tick already takes (`takeBuilt`), with no channel and no callback into the UI thread. Live-verified in tmux at 120x40 on a real tree: `@pathind` offers `internal/tui/pathindex.go`, Tab inserts the full cwd-relative path, and a bare `@` still lists the root. Tests: the index's own order/prune/cap, the menu's answer for a nested file, and the two cases that must NOT change (an empty index changes nothing, a directory drill ignores the index).
*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (sixth mouse leg: the selection that copied but never showed — user-reported "when I use mouse to select and copy it do not work in textbox"). **The highlight was painted under the chrome.** `paint()` called `drawSelection()` right after publishing the transcript rows, then repainted the dock, every overlay, `drawComposer` and `drawStatusRow` over it — so a drag that ended on the composer (the first surface an empty transcript offers) copied the text to the clipboard while every cell the gesture covered was restored to normal video by the same frame. The copy worked; the only proof a user gets was missing, which is exactly what reads as "selection does not work". `drawSelection()` now runs last in `paint()`, after `drawStatusRow`, so the selection is a screen overlay over whatever the frame drew. The welcome branch (no transcript) early-`return`ed before ever reaching it, so a selection made on the welcome screen was never painted at all — it now draws there too. Regression cover: `TestSelectionHighlightsTheComposer` drags across the composer with and without a transcript and asserts `AttrReverse` on the covered cells (it fails on the pre-fix tree at both sub-cases); the four earlier selection tests only ever dragged transcript rows, which nothing repaints, which is why the ordering bug survived them.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (user-requested "show the thinking output in a box in the TUI like the tool call, with a fixed window and scrollable" — branch `feat/thinking-box`): reasoning stopped being a header over a wall of dim text. A `KindThinking` block now renders in the same rounded frame a finished tool result gets, with `theme.AccentThinking` as the border and the state in the top border (`⠹ Thinking…` while it streams, `Thought for Xs` once it settles), showing a **fixed `thinkBoxRows = 12`-row window** — the tier-based head+tail trim (`thinkWindow(tier)` and the two `think*` row budgets) is deleted, so reasoning reads the same at every tier instead of collapsing as it ages. The window is **scrollable**: the mouse wheel over the box moves that box's own `Block.ThinkOff` (rows up from the newest thought, clamped at both ends, falling through to the transcript's scroll at either end) instead of the transcript underneath, while Ctrl+O (`ToggleBoxExpand`, renamed from `ToggleToolExpand`) expands the newest boxed block — result or reasoning — to every row. The box's rail is dropped (`rowChrome`: the frame is the line), `ThinkOff` joins the row-index stamp (`blockKey.thinkOff`) so a wheel notch re-renders exactly the box it scrolled, and `rowIndex.blockAt` is the new O(log n) row→block hit-test the wheel needs. Tests: `TestThinkBoxRendersInAFixedWindow`, `TestThinkWindowClampsAtBothEnds`, `TestWheelScrollsTheThinkBoxUnderIt`.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (committed-conflict-artifact sweep — spotted while cherry-picking an unrelated TUI fix, which is exactly the kind of merge that hits it again): a `>>>>>>> eee9219 (feat(tui): reasoning renders in a fixed, wheel-scrollable box)` line was **committed to this file** by 7d0c866 (#311) and carried by every commit since, including the tag the release train points at. Nothing in the tree could see it: the artifact is docs, and `TestNoMergeMarkersInSource` (added after the `@both` leak of 2026-09-12) globs `cmd/xdev/*.go` only. The line is deleted — the hunk it belonged to was already applied, so both sides of the merge are present above and below and nothing is lost — and the gap is closed by `TestNoConflictMarkersInTrackedFiles` (`cmd/xdev/repohygiene_test.go`), which scans every file `git ls-files` reports and fails on any `<<<<<<<`/`|||||||`/`>>>>>>>` line, naming file and line. It exercises the real case in both directions: red on this file at 745 before the deletion, green after. A bare `=======` is deliberately not treated as a marker — it is also a legal setext underline, so flagging it would fail on a valid document, and an unresolved conflict always writes the `<<<<<<<` side anyway.

*Last updated: 2026-09-16 (user-reported "paste the image from the os clipboard to the chat textbox, not working" — the chord and the whole paste path already existed since `d589778`; the macOS reader under it had never once run): `internal/tui/clipboard_image.go`'s embedded AppleScript had **two** dialect errors, either of which kills the paste, and the caller could not tell them apart from an empty clipboard because every failure printed the same reassuring "no image on the clipboard". (1) `(path to temporary folder)` is a **syntax error (-2741)** on current macOS — the script never compiled, so `Ctrl+V` could not have worked on any build. `(path to temporary items folder)` is the spelling that resolves (to `/private/var/folders/…/T/TemporaryItems/`, verified rc=0). (2) Fixing only that hands the next failure: `open for access file f` takes the POSIX-path **string** and dies -61 "File not open with write permission" at the first write; `open for access (POSIX file f) with write permission` is the form that writes. Both phrases and the error codes that pin them are now named in the script's comment, because each is invisible until it is broken. The script also moved to a named const `darwinClipboardScript` so a test can compile it, and `darwinClipboardImage` now captures osascript's stderr: **-1700** (measured: plain text, an empty pasteboard, a Finder file copy, RTF) and the pasteboard family's **-25130 / -25133** (a lazily declared image flavour with no data behind it) keep the plain `noImage` wording, while a script error is reported as itself — the reader's own failure must never masquerade as an empty clipboard again. That distinction is what turned this bug into a dead chord instead of a bug report a year earlier. Two checks in the new `internal/tui/clipboard_image_test.go`: `TestDarwinClipboardScriptCompiles` runs the shipped script through `osacompile` (rejects defect 1 with no clipboard, and it is the cheap one — the negative control fails it, `compilation error: Expected "," but found property. (-2741)`), and `TestDarwinClipboardScriptWritesImage` hands the script a real 3×2 PNG as a `«data PNGf…»` literal — only the clipboard read is substituted, the four file verbs stay exactly as shipped — and compares the bytes it wrote (defect 2 fails it with -61; the `(the clipboard as «class PNGf»)` anchor is asserted to appear exactly once so the substitution cannot silently stop covering the line under test). `TestIsNoImageStderr` pins the error-code split, and deliberately fails a blank stderr: osascript always names its reason, so silence means the assumption needs revisiting. Verified live in a tmux-run real TTY, `screencapture -c` on the clipboard throughout: **pre-fix** binary (`Ctrl+V`) → `read clipboard image: exit status 1: 43:49: syntax error: Expected "," …`; **post-fix** → chip `[Image 1920x1080 #1]` in the composer, and a sent turn answered from pixels (`❯ [Image 1920x1080 #1] what colour dominates this image? one word` → `black`). Text on the clipboard still takes the quiet path: `no image on the clipboard (Cmd+V is the terminal's paste): …`. Also established, so nobody re-litigates it: `«class PNGf»` is answered by conversion for a JPEG- or TIFF-only pasteboard too (declared flavor order does not matter — `the clipboard as «class PNGf»` converted a `public.tiff`-only and a `com.compuserve.gif`-only pasteboard, all rc=0), so no extra flavour fallback is needed; and a stdin-fed osascript variant or a JXA/`NSImage` reader would work equally well but neither is smaller than fixing two phrases. omp reads its clipboard through a native N-API module, not AppleScript (`packages/natives/native/clipboard.js`), which is why this dialect rot went unnoticed against it. Note for the next reader: the `vision: true` flag in `models.yml` gates the chord, and `onegw`'s pinned models do not declare it, so an undeclared-but-actually-vision model gets the "takes no image input" notice instead — the fix's positive path was verified with `XDEV_AGENT_DIR` pointed at a copy of `models.yml` with `vision: true` added.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-16 (user-reported "fix the ci pipeline not runing on the main to bump new version and create new github release"): the 2026-09-15 move of the release train to a `release` branch is reverted — `release.yml`'s push trigger is `branches: [main]` again. The reason holds up on its own: the last release (v0.4.1, 2026-09-16 07:53Z) was cut by a manual push to `release`, while the four commits landed on `main` since then (through 275a5da) never triggered a bump — a release you have to remember to push is a release that does not happen. `main` is the branch every merge lands on, so it is the one trigger that needs no second step. `1365527`'s other half stays: tag pushes (`v*`) and `workflow_dispatch` are unchanged. The `release` branch remains on origin, now inert.

*Last updated: 2026-09-16 (user-requested "revert back the logo X like before" on the README): `assets/brand/xdev-logo.png` is the pre-redesign lockup again (1120x192, the wide `xdev` wordmark with its tagline), restored from `f3e5a7a^`. Scope is that one file: the README markup never moved (`<img ... width="520">`), and `xdev-logo.svg`, `xdev-icon.*`, the favicon, the social preview and `assets/screenshots/welcome.png` keep the 22x7 grid mark, because `TestLogoPixelParity` pins the *SVG* against the terminal art in `internal/tui/welcome.go` — nothing reads the PNG. So the README front page and the in-app wordmark are now deliberately two different renderings; if the brand is ever unified, the PNG follows the grid, not the other way round.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-16 (user-requested "redesign xdev logo, remove background animation on welcome screen", with the new mark supplied as a pixel-grid SVG): the welcome wordmark is now the pixel "xdev" itself — `xdevLogo` in `internal/tui/welcome.go` is 7 rows cut from one 22x7 grid at 2 cells per pixel (`logoWidth()` 44; `TestLogoPixelParity` fails any row that splits a pixel, pinning the seam with the brand SVG, whose rects reduce to the same grid — `assets/brand/xdev-logo.svg` was re-derived from it). Conway's Game of Life is gone from the welcome screen: `lifeGrid`/`newLifeGrid`/`step`/`drawLife`/`lifeArea` and the app's `life`/`lifeTick` deleted, the tick loop now only drives the sheen sweep, which stays; the 18-row random band that used to clear the logo is gone, so `logoArt` needs only `h < 13` to switch to compact. All ten files in `assets/brand/` regenerated from that grid. Deliberate deviation from the pasted SVG: its literal d/e/v origins (130/250/370) sit between grid pixels, so the lockup was re-cut to 120/220/340 and the Menlo text + tagline dropped — do not restore them, it breaks the one-grid invariant. `assets/screenshots/welcome.png` re-shot from the new binary (`scripts/tui-shot.py`).*
*Last updated: 2026-09-16 (a `git describe` stamp was an offer to downgrade, found while verifying the release check on the real binary): `Compare("v0.4.0-2-gf419040", "v0.4.0")` returned -1, because semver ranks *any* hyphenated form below the plain tag and describe's `-<n>-g<sha>` tail is exactly that shape. So `xdev update --check` on a build past the tag printed `update available: v0.4.0-2-gf419040 → v0.4.0`, and plain `xdev update` *acted* on it: the update path verified the manifest, downloaded v0.4.0 and renamed it over a binary that already contained v0.4.0 plus three commits. This session found it by doing that to `~/.local/bin/xdev` — the notice was not a false alarm, the downgrade was real and shipped in the tool's most-used verb. `parseSemver` now trims git's layout (`<tag>-<n>-g<sha>[-dirty][+meta]`, in that order so each suffix has one home) and drops the describe tail before applying semver, matched strictly — digits, then `-g` plus 7+ hex — so real pre-releases (`0.1.0-dev`, `0.2.0-canary.1`) keep semver's rule and still sort below their release. Nine cases in `TestCompare` pin both directions, the dirty/metadata combinations, and a canary carrying a describe tail; the fix is confirmed live, not just in a unit: a binary stamped from this HEAD now says `xdev v0.4.0-3-g3a8f47f-dirty is up to date (channel stable, latest v0.4.0)` and exits 0.
*Last updated: 2026-09-16 (twice-daily version check behind `xdev update` — user: "add version check twice daily"; locked by ask: mechanism **"C: both, one state file"**, action **notify-only**): the shipped `xdev update` (#275, #278) still made the user ask whether a newer release exists. `internal/dist/check.go` adds the state half — one `CheckRecord` (`{checked_at, channel, version, running, error}`) in the data dir, written atomically by whoever runs the check and read by every launch; `MaybeCheck` is the terminal-attached fallback (a detached `xdev update --check` at most once per 12h) and steps aside when a schedule owns the checking, so a machine with the job installed does zero work at launch. `internal/dist/job.go` adds the schedule: `xdev update job install` writes a launchd plist (`com.freepeak.xdev.update-check`, two `StartCalendarInterval`s at 09:00 + 21:00) or a `--user` systemd timer pair (`Persistent=true`, so a laptop that slept through 09:00 still checks on wake), runs it once immediately, and says where its logs are; `job status` is the diagnosis — last check, what it found, which schedule owns it; `job remove` unregisters both ways. Both pass `--timeout 120s`, the new flag on `--check`: an unattended run needs a bound tighter than the 10-minute default a human interactive update waits behind, because launchd starts another in ten minutes and the `running` field only holds one. The environment is the config — `jobEnvPairs` forwards `XDEV_UPDATE_REPO`/`XDEV_UPDATE_API`/`XDEV_CHANNEL`/`XDEV_AGENT_DIR`/`XDEV_PROFILE` and pointedly **not** `GITHUB_TOKEN`: a plist is readable by anything running as the user, and a private-repo token written there outlives the shell that exported it. The notice lands on the two surfaces that can show it — the TUI's welcome slot (joined onto, not replacing, the #272 agent-count line: both are startup facts) and `xdev -p`'s stderr, never its stdout, which is the answer a script parses. `XDEV_UPDATE_CHECK=0` declines even the fallback. `.gitignore`'s `dist/` became `/dist/`: the unanchored form matched `internal/dist/` as well, so the package built, tested and passed review while being impossible to `git add`. 22 tests in `check_test.go`/`job_test.go` pin the record, the throttle, the three ways `MaybeCheck` refuses, and the generated unit files' two schedules; `TestInstalledReleaseNeverCarriesForce` keeps the `--force`/`installRelease` pair this change deleted from coming back.
*Last updated: 2026-09-16 (fifth mouse leg: the two scroll gestures that did not scroll — user-reported "the scroll button in TUI not working when using mouse to drag and moving to scroll up/down"): **the scrollbar became an input device, and the edge drag got a clock.** (1) The right-edge `│/█` bar was painted (app.go reserves its column) but inert: a press there opened a text selection over it. The painter now publishes its geometry every frame — `selBarOn/W/VP/Total/Thumb/Pos`, the picker's hit-table pattern — and a press in the last column grabs the thumb: `selThumbTo` linearly inverts `scrollModel.Scrollbar` (rounded, so the thumb keeps up with the finger), holds the grip taken at press, and a click on the bare track jumps the thumb to the finger. A bar gesture owns no selection at all — it cannot copy, and a highlight left by an earlier drag is dropped, because the bar slides rows out from under it. (2) `selAutoScroll` moved one row **per event**, and tcell strips the SGR motion bit (`input.go:638`, exposed by nothing), so a pointer parked on the transcript's edge reports nothing further: the scroll stalled the instant the hand stopped, which is exactly the gesture. `selEdgeTick` runs off the existing 33 ms UI tick — past `selEdgeDelay` (400 ms, the guard that keeps a fast sweep across an edge row from page-turning) it moves `selEdgeStep` (2) rows per frame and re-anchors the far corner, so the arriving rows join the selection and the copy stays complete. Both halves ride the press **edge**: `handleMouse` now takes the `press bool` its siblings (ask/picker/roster) already had, instead of inferring a press from `!selDown` — that inference let the first motion of a thumb drag reset the grip, and it reset a selection every time a wheel event landed inside a held drag (a wheel report carries no button bits, so it read as "button up"). `internal/tui/scrollmouse_test.go`: a parked held drag keeps scrolling and its copy ends on the pointer's row; a pass-through edge row scrolls for its one event and no more; a thumb press moves nothing until the finger does; no bar, no grab; a wheel inside a drag leaves the gesture alone.

*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (implement the tool-grammar fixes from the speed-bench report §5–6 — branch `fix/tool-grammar-turn-tax`, feeding #284): three unit-proven diffs land in `internal/tool`: `parseHashline` normalizes the colon range spelling (`PUT 15:=24:` → `PUT 15.=24:`; `PUT 3:` keeps its single-line/inline meaning); `read` now prints the `[path#tag]` snapshot header (tag = the recorded freshness hash; notebook windows unchanged), closing the round-trip the `edit` contract always assumed — models fabricated `#1`-style tags because no header existed and took ENOENTs, and `splitSnapshotTag` now refuses a non-hex `#suffix` whose bare name exists as an unknown snapshot tag instead of opening it as a path; read/edit tool descriptions aligned within the 400-char cap. Regression tests pin the colon spelling, the read→edit header round-trip, and the unknown-tag refusal; gofmt clean, internal/tool+agent+tui+session green; patched bench arm ran 0 grammar-error round-trips in 3/3 runs (5/6 runs had ≥1 unpatched).*
*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (user-requested speed comparison "omp feels faster than xdev — test, compare, comprehensive result" → docs/research/2026-09-15-omp-vs-xdev-speed-bench.html): 36 headless head-to-heads (same pinned model onegw/b-ai/qwen3.8-flash, same 6-tool slate, isolated configs, timestamp-decomposed; fixture = 3-bug invoice.js, all 22 fresh runs pass 5/5) confirm the claim on multi-turn edit tasks (median 54.0 s vs 63.9 s evening window; 29.5 vs 47.5 quiet window) but refute the engine theory: harness switching is 2–24 ms/turn (omp) and ≤0.3 s/run (xdev), provider round-trips are 95 %+ of both walls, and xdev boots ~100× faster (0.04 vs 4.06 s fixed cost) winning one-shot tasks 2–4×. The gap is turn count: xdev's tool surface burned 1–4 grammar-error round-trips per run — hashlinePutRe rejects the colon spelling "PUT 15:=24:", read prints no [path#tag] header so the model fabricates one, and splitSnapshotTag's 4-hex rule turns the fabrication into an ENOENT that teaches nothing; correlation is 1:1 (0 errors → 5 turns → omp-parity wall). A unit-proven three-diff fix (colon normalization, read header emission, teach-the-grammar tag refusal; /tmp/bench2/xdev-tool-grammar-fix.patch) removed all error round-trips in 3/3 live runs — pending a land decision. Second-order findings: sub-1K-token prompts never hit the provider prefix cache; the 90 s watchdog stretches upstream stalls (observed 121.7 s) into minutes; pi-minimal-prompt variance on mid-tier models (bash-cat reads, re-verify loops) is the residual turn-count tax.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (user-reported "xdev costs more turns and runs longer than omp" — RCA): measured the two tools' session stores (166 xdev / 52 omp JSONL, same onegw lanes) — steady-state full-step latency is identical (p50 17 s); the gap is episodic: UI-loop stalls (13 dumps, worst 15 m) hold `a.mu` across `s.Show()`, back-pressure the synchronous `OnEvent` path, and the 90 s idle watchdog then kills healthy streams → retain-and-continue injects a synthetic "cut off by a provider error" user turn (27 observed) plus backoff retries; follow-up nudges run 57.5 % of xdev user messages vs 16.7 % omp. Post-header `ttft`/`duration` semantics hide the queue latency; the pre-#133 cache gap and no artifact offload add uncached/context weight. Full analysis + fix directions: [research/2026-09-15-xdev-slow-session-rca.md](research/2026-09-15-xdev-slow-session-rca.md). No code changed by this analysis.
*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (user-requested "add the new keybindings, like F5, to retry the current session to keep resuming running"): the keymap gained function-key chords (`keyName` now names F1–F12, so `keybindings.yml` can bind them) and a new `retry` action bound to **F5** by default. `cmd/xdev/tui.go` refactored runTurn's agent-launch half into a shared `startTurn`: it runs the agent over the CURRENT store, so F5 resumes a session whose stream dropped (the socket-close case) with **no duplicate user prompt** — the same history drives another turn. The retry op re-takes the turn claim exactly as a submit does, so it refuses politely while a turn runs or before the first prompt, and a guest (collab-forward) session defers to the host. Covers omp's `retry` (Alt+R there; F5 is xdev's chord, remappable). Tests: `TestRetryChordResolves` (F5 resolves through the real `chordOf` path) and `TestAppRetryChord` (wired op fires once, adds no block; unwired → one system notice).*
*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (user-requested "implement the markdown table render in the chat session like omp"): assistant prose gained a real GFM table renderer — `internal/tui/markdown.go` recognises a pipe row followed by a `|---|---|` delimiter and paints an omp sharp grid: a rule between every row pair, bold flush-left header, `│ ` padding, corners from `BoxSharp()` (the ascii preset degenerates junctions to `+`). Column widths port omp's fitter (read from `packages/tui/src/components/markdown.ts` in the omp/18.2.0 binary): longest-word floor capped at 30, proportional shrink, greedy remainder hand-out, `3r+1` border overhead off the terminal width. Cells still render inline markdown (bold/code/links, `\|` escapes; alignment colons parse and are ignored — everything is flush-left, as omp ships it), and below `4r+1` cells the raw lines render unchanged, omp's escape hatch. Covered by `markdown_test.go` (exact grid, fit-to-width, narrow fallback, delimiter requirement, cell syntax, CJK column alignment).
*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (user-reported "update my input prompt text box, still have the text bg color black in the user input"): the sent-message band painted its row fill but drew the text runs with foreground-only styles — in tcell v2 a style's zero background is ColorDefault (SGR 49), so every cell holding a prompt rune reset to the terminal's own (near-black) background inside the band, and the user input read as black behind its own glyphs. The shared band-row painter now composes the row's bg onto the rail, the runs and the timestamp whenever ln.bg is set (one guard covers user prompts, code fences and every future band row); TestUserBandPaintsUnderGlyphs pins the band under the prompt's own cells. The user's daily binary also proved stale — a stripped pre-band release build — reinstalled from the pushed commit.*)
*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (user-requested "update the top title of xdev not only showing the last user input prompt but also showing the first user input prompt"): row 0 now carries **both** anchors of a session — its FIRST user prompt and the NEWEST, joined by the bar's own separators (`❯ branch · <first> · <newest>`). `lastPrompt` became `topPrompts`, which scans forward for the opening prompt and backward for the newest, both through the new `promptHead` (first line, `promptScanBytes` head, sanitized and squeezed) and both stopping at the first prompt with text, so the per-frame cost is two collapsed lines instead of one. Width sharing is deliberately asymmetric: the newest prompt — the request on screen — may never be cut below half the room and takes the whole bar when a half share each falls under `topPromptCells`, because two three-word fragments name nothing; the first takes what is left and pays for it with `…`. The bar still keeps its cell of right-edge air, still shows one entry until a second prompt exists, and still leaves the directory and the model to the status row. `TestTopBarCarriesFirstAndLastPrompt` and `TestTopBarNarrowKeepsNewestPrompt` pin the pair, the fallback and the spill; the single-prompt test gained a not-painted-twice assert.
*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (#133 prompt-cache breakpoints, from the goal "research how omp caches the prompt/input context, then check xdev and enhance"): xdev parsed `cache_read`/`cache_creation` but emitted **no** cache control, so those counters could never move — every turn paid full price for a 5–17k token prefix that never changed. Now `ai.StreamRequest.Cache` (`CacheOpts{Key, SideRequest}`, zero value = the old byte-for-byte request) drives two provider-native mechanisms: Anthropic `cache_control` markers (system as blocks + one on the LAST tool + the rest of the 4-marker budget over the tail newest-first and every 15th markable message behind it, skipping `thinking`; the tool-array marker is what survives a per-turn reminder changing the system prompt) and OpenAI `prompt_cache_key` (verbatim within 64 chars, else `pc_<fnv64a/36>`), the key gated on a first-party host because xdev has no per-model compat table and a proxy 400s on the unknown field — omp itself puts both behind `compat.supportsPromptCacheKey`. Identity is `Store.ID()` via `cacheOpts()`, shared by the main chain and the handoff summarize call (`SideRequest` stops its markers at the last completed tool round, so a served-and-dropped request writes only a prefix the next turn reads); a 4xx naming `cache_control` rebuilds keyless, retries once, and latches markers off for the process; `XDEV_CACHE_RETENTION`/`PI_CACHE_RETENTION` = `short|long|none` (`long` adds `ttl:"1h"`, `none` drops both vocabularies). `/usage` now prints the hit ratio — the number that says whether any of it works. Deferred on purpose: Google managed `cachedContent` CRUD, explicit `prompt_cache_breakpoint`, `auto` retention. Shape read out of the omp binary + 10 offline checks in `internal/ai/cache_test.go` → [research/omp-prompt-cache-2026-09-15.md](research/omp-prompt-cache-2026-09-15.md).

*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (user-requested "remove the dir path at top bar"): the top bar's location is the git branch alone (`❯ main`); the working directory lives only on the status row's left. `cwdLabel`/`cwdShort` retired with it — a branchless welcome bar paints just the model. `TestTopBarCarriesBranchAndLastPrompt` asserts the temp-dir name never reaches row 0.

*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (user-reported "The box tool call output in xdev not having the style format like omp"): the tool-result frame now sanitizes output before measuring it — `sanitizeOutput` expands tabs to omp's 3-cell stop and drops ANSI/C0/C1 sequences (omp parity: `we()` + the control-char strip in its tui utils, read from the binary's embedded source) — because `runewidth` counts a tab as 0 cells while the terminal paints it as an advance to the next 8-column stop: every `│` of a tab-indented result landed on its own column. `TestToolResultBoxAlignsTabbedOutput` pins one shared right edge.

*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (user-reported "the chat textbox can not move up/down using arrow keys when it have multiple line"): Up/Down now walk the composer's visual rows — hard-newline rows and soft-wrapped alike — through Editor.moveLine, which reads the same wrapRows geometry the painter uses (arrows traverse exactly what is drawn) and carries a vim/omp sticky desired column so alternating arrows over ragged rows never drift left. At the first/last row — or a one-row draft — moveLine reports false and HandleKey keeps the existing history-recall contract (multi-line drafts are still never clobbered), matching omp/18.1.22 editor.ts cursorUp/cursorDown. Tests: TestArrowsWalkHardNewlineRows, TestArrowsWalkWrappedRowsAndRecallAtEdge.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (fourth mouse leg: click-to-focus on the live-agent roster — omp's own `tui.mouse` behavior): clicking an agent row in `/hub` now selects it **and** runs Enter's action on it, through one shared `rosterActivate`, so a click can never drift from the chord; the wheel moves the list selection and scrolls an open transcript view instead while one is up. The roster painter publishes the row map each frame (the same table pattern as the picker's), clears it on every frame that shows no list, and the hit-test is by row like omp's `SelectList.hitTest(row)` — an agent row spans the panel, so the column carries no meaning. Press-edge tracking moved out of the picker into one `App.mouseBtnDown` computed in `handleKey`, because tcell strips the SGR motion bit: without a single shared edge, the drag reports a terminal sends along with a click would choose row after row. omp gates this behind `tui.mouse` (default off) because its default config never captures the mouse; xdev always captures it, so the feature is on and clicks are otherwise inert. Hover highlight still off per §3.5. Covered by `TestHubRosterMouse` and live-verified that the routing does not swallow the transcript (`/hub` open, drag across the transcript still copies).
*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (third mouse leg: the modal picker answers the mouse — #165's routing half, omp-shaped): a list the user can see is a list the user can click. The picker painter now publishes its row and tab geometry every frame (`picker.hitItem`/`hitY0`, `tabAt`/`tabY` — the same table omp's `SelectList` rebuilds inside `render()`), and `handlePickerMouse` acts on it with omp's semantics: **one wheel notch moves the selection one row**, **one click on a row takes it and chooses it immediately** (`clickItem` calls `onSelect` itself, no confirmation second click), and a click on a view tab switches view. A click anywhere inside the panel is consumed, so it can no longer scroll the transcript or start a text selection underneath the modal. Hover highlight is deliberately NOT routed: §3.5 already decided mouse hover stays off, and omp gates its own hover behind the same opt-in. Live-verified with tmux inside a pty (so the bytes travel the real path and `capture-pane` gives an exact grid): `/model` opened → Tab to the model list → two notches moved `onegw/free → onegw/dev → onegw/fast` → one click closed the panel and the composer divider re-read `onegw/fast`. Ten transcript-selection checks re-ran unchanged after the routing went in.
*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (second mouse leg: one drag now covers more than one screen — closes the ceiling the previous stamp named): a transcript gesture anchors its corners to **document rows** instead of screen rows, and pulling the pointer onto the transcript's top or bottom row scrolls the view one row per event (a terminal's edge auto-scroll; dragging past the last row into the composer counts as pulling downward). Rows the scroll pushes away are kept in a per-gesture cache, so the copy at release is complete — live-verified as **32 rows copied across a 25-row transcript**, WORD01..WORD21 with no gaps. Both highlight and copy now resolve through one `selSpan`, so what is painted is what is copied; that also caught a real bug the unit tests had been hiding, where middle rows started at the anchor's column instead of the line head and every interior line came back truncated. The remaining limit is narrower than the one it replaced: document rows are positions in the rendered-line index, so a transcript that grows or compacts *during* a drag moves them, and the copy can be off by the rows that arrived mid-gesture.
*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (mouse select → clipboard, user-requested "xdev working exactly the same as omp TUI"): a drag is no longer confined to the transcript. Rows the transcript painter recorded still copy rail-free; every other row — welcome screen, composer, info divider, status row, an open overlay — is now read back from the painted grid, so anything on screen can be copied, which is what an omp user gets from the terminal's own selection. The highlight **survives the release** until the next click (a terminal's rule, and the only visible proof the gesture did anything while the app holds the mouse), the composer's info divider carries `Copied N chars` for two seconds (omp's `showStatus` for every copy path), and a click with no drag clears the highlight instead of writing an empty string over the clipboard. Two real defects fell out of the rework: the copy dropped the last highlighted cell (`hi` was an exclusive bound while `drawSelection` painted it inclusively), and a frame that draws no transcript kept the *previous* frame's row capture, so after `/clear` a drag could copy text that was no longer on screen. Shift+drag still hands the whole gesture to the terminal (omp's documented contract: "native text selection becomes Shift+drag"). Ceiling, marked in `internal/tui/selection.go`: the selection is screen-relative, so a drag cannot reach past one viewport and a transcript that streams mid-drag re-renders under the highlight — document-space anchors plus edge auto-scroll is the upgrade path. Verified live over a pty with injected SGR mouse bytes (7/7: highlight, copy, confirmation, persistence, click-no-clobber, click-no-claim, Shift decline) plus `internal/tui/selection_test.go`.
*Last updated: 2026-09-15 (tree-selector revert = omp navigateTree, user-requested "revert/restore the messages in the message tree, exactly like omp" — 6d898de): Enter on a user row now rewinds the leaf to its PARENT and returns the prompt as a composer draft (the first message rewinds to a reset-boundary root), the draft primes only over an empty composer, re-picking the active leaf answers "Already at this point", and a successful switch reports "Navigated to selected point" — previously the leaf landed ON the user message and a resend duplicated it. Shift+Enter/Alt+S summaries follow omp branchWithSummary: the branch_summary is appended as a child of the TARGET (the new branch's context reads it); the old order hung it off the abandoned leaf where buildContext never reached it. Seam: SessionOps.SummarizeAndBranch → NavigateTree(entryID, summarize) → (draft, error); treeRewindTarget carries the target rule; /branch lost its unreachable ambiguity guard.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-14 (#244 goal continuation, user-reported “goal created and nothing ran”): an active goal no longer only decorates the next user-driven turn — `/goal create|resume` kicks the first turn (objective → session title + first prompt) and a yield with no tool calls re-enters the run with the hidden `goal-continuation` prompt. TUI-only (omp's `goal.continuationModes: [interactive]` default), ending on complete/drop/budget_exhausted or the run's turn budget; `agent.GoalContinuation` + `wireAgentMode(..., interactive)` gate it, `GoalState.ContinuationPrompt()` renders it, and the cmd seam plus the agent loop each carry a test that fails without the wiring.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-14 (second leg: #123, #241, #126, #127). Credential store: `credentials.json` is now verified on **every** read (`mode == 0600 && nlink == 1`, refusal names the repair), the data dir tightens to 0700 on save, a named `auth:` style is an exact authority that never falls through to a key it did not choose, and the macOS Keychain is a credential **source** (`apiKey: keychain:<service>[/<account>]`) and never a sink — measured: `/usr/bin/security`'s prompt silently truncates at 128 bytes with exit 0 (fine for an API key, corrupting for a 1–2 KB JWT) while argv is world-readable in `ps`; [decision record](decisions/keychain-credential-source.md). Repository execution: `.xdev/hooks` is withheld until `xdev trust` records a per-hook digest of what was read (#241), and the notice now reaches the user because `buildHookBus` had been logging every warning through a logger that is off by default. Cancellation: a cancelled turn no longer waits on a tool that ignores the context — #126 bounds it (`DefaultCancelGrace`) and the bound is proven by a stub that fails at 5s without it. Background bash gained its model-facing control plane (`action=status|logs|stop|write`, #127) with the persistence half filed as **#243**. Two red CI runs came from a parallel session's in-flight hunks riding along in a file-level `git add`; the standing rule is now post-commit and pre-push: verify `HEAD` in a throwaway worktree before pushing.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-14 (tool-call output format + decode rate): every call renders as `name · naming argument` with a live elapsed while it runs, finished results are a rounded box whose footer reports wall time/exit/truncation and whose hidden rows expand via Ctrl+O instead of pointing at the log, and the HUD status bar gained a `⚡ t/s` decode-rate segment measured from streamed deltas (omp's per-message math), live while a turn runs and sticky after.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-14 (#114 repo-trust boundary + the CI gate reopened): `main` was red on Linux since c3985bc and is green again (#125 tts tests pinned the darwin backend's name and argv while the code resolves the backend per host; #240 the desktop-control timeout stub assumed a PATH-fallback shell, and fixing it exposed a product bug — `runCmd` used `exec.CommandContext`, whose default kill hits only the direct child, so a timed-out `computer` op reported its deadline and then blocked 5 s on the pipe a surviving descendant held, with that descendant being the process synthesising clicks). `internal/computer` now carries the process-group pair the five sibling spawners already use, plus a bounded pipe drain. Then #114: a cloned repository's `.xdev/config.yml` / `.xdev/models.yml` now merge only through an allowlist (`internal/config/reposafe.go`), the profile is the last word on any provider both name, `${VAR}` expansion is profile-only, project `secrets.yml` can no longer shadow a profile entry, and every refused key is named at startup. Audit of the remaining repo-reachable roots filed as **#241** (`.xdev/hooks` executes with no trust decision, verified by running it).*
*Last updated: 2026-09-14 (/goal verb grammar, user-reported): `error: unknown /goal verb "check"` had two causes — the read verb accepted only `view`/`get`/`status`, and no discoverable surface named the grammar at all (`/help` said "show the active goal", the README listed four verbs without a read verb). Read synonyms now all route to the one query (`check`/`show`/`status`/`get`/`list`/`info`/`view` + bare `/goal`, the `/theme list` rule), `/help` + README carry the full verb set, the empty-goal view names `/goal create <objective>` instead of only the model-facing tool, and a new registry test rejects duplicate commands (the edit that produced this report had also replaced `/hub` — invisible to every other test). Verified live in the TUI. See [parity-delta](parity-delta.md).*

*Last updated: 2026-09-13 (fx cross-check → §5.2 + [research/fx-internals.md](research/fx-internals.md), 799 lines): `vercel-labs/fx` @ `ced6180` (Zig, 671k LOC) studied from a full clone across nine domains and written up with `path:line` evidence. It **confirms** goals 1–2 and the <1,000-token prompt bet (fx's own prompt is 5,450 bytes with 17 tools registered / 15 advertised) and **confirms** two non-goals — no OS sandbox anywhere in fx (searched; its `sandbox` key is proven inert at `config_runtime.zig:3141`) and no process-level RSS budget either, which xdev still does better. Ten parity rows and ten issues out — **#114** (P0 repo-safe config), **#115** retained tool results, **#116** measure the serialized body + named byte budgets, **#117** opt-in `auto` reviewer, **#118** startup/frame budgets, **#119** ship-gate + size delta + leak audit, **#120** terminal tape tier, **#121** config provenance + `xdev doctor`, **#122** write-side torn-tail set, **#123** Keychain + read-time permission checks — and all 31 then-open issues (#78–#113) each carry an fx evidence comment. Two §2 claims were wrong against the tree and are corrected above: "artifact spill" (the sink discards the middle — `internal/tool/sink.go:9-13`) and "no-fsync appends" (there is a per-entry flush plus an optional strict fsync; what is missing is the length guard, the rollback and the unfinished-turn discard). Also fixed: `internal/acp/acp.go`'s package doc still claimed LSP-style `Content-Length` framing after `framing.go` moved to ACP's newline-delimited frames — fx confirms the spec (`acp/jsonrpc.zig:275` NDJSON, 8 MiB inbound cap, oversized answered without dropping the connection). Red `main` CI predating and following this commit is attributed in **#125**.*
