*Last updated: 2026-09-21 (feat/trajectory-rich follow-up — tool-call wall time on disk): `toolResultMsg` now stamps `Message.DurationMS` from the same `time.Since(started)` `OnToolEnd` already measured, so `/trajectory` tool rows show latency (and resume keeps it). `tool.Outcome.DurationMS` surfaces bash `durationMs` from Details as a fallback for older sessions. Tests: `TestToolResultMsgKe…[+60b]

*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (fix/goal-target-only — `/goal <target goal>` works, and the TUI's `error: goal not wired` is fixed): two user-reported `/goal` defects land together. (1) **The command no longer requires a verb.** `/goal ship the exporter` names the session's objective and starts the first turn; a bare `/goal` shows the goal and budget; `complete`/`drop` are the two words that clos…[+1172b]
*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (fix/retry-notices — the "stream error — retrying (x2)" freeze): the retain-and-continue recovery (`post-content failure` — the partial is kept, a continuation turn is injected, the ladder re-asks) was the one retry the console showed as a bare collapsed blip under the older "· provider cut off mid-message" block, so a run that kept re-asking read as hung. `agent.ContinuationRetryError` now rides `TurnHooks.OnEvent` for both post-content arms of `oneTurnWithRecovery` (retainable partial and infinite-without-partial) **before** the backoff, carrying the delay it is about to sleep — the same contract `AllTargetsDownError` / `EmptyTurnRetryError` already had. TUI and print now print it verbatim as "· provider cut off mid-message — continuation injected, retrying in 2s" instead of letting `ai.Classify` collapse it into "retrying (xN)"; both backoffs also now go through the announced delay instead of a bare `sleepBackoff`. Test: `TestContinuationRoundIsAnnouncedOnTheStream`.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (feat/edit-verification — the parse tier of post-edit diagnostics): `edit`/`write` now run `go/parser` over a Go file they just wrote and append a one-line note to the tool result when it no longer parses, mirrored into `Details.syntaxWarning` (`internal/tool/verify.go`). The staleness guard proves a patch landed where the model aimed; nothing proved the result still compiled, so the model learned that one turn later (or never) when its build failed. Reports, never vetoes: the bytes are already on disk and the note is information, not a gate. Non-Go files and files over 2 MiB are skipped (neither is the shape an edit patch makes). It is the cheap end of the diagnostics ladder on purpose — [#263](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/263) owns the LSP `publishDiagnostics` tier, and the import direction forbids the other order (`internal/lsp/tool.go` imports `internal/tool`, not the reverse), which the sibling issue is now blocked on noting.)
*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (docs: ZCode cross-check — [research/zcode-internals.md](research/zcode-internals.md) + §5.5): a same-scope, opposite-stack peer (TypeScript/Node, SQLite event ledger, Vercel AI SDK, session residency pool, hard 400-line file ceiling) was read at primary source and compared mechanic by mechanic. Five findings change a stated design assumption and became issues: **a tool's capabilities belong in its declaration** (`readOnly`/`concurrentSafe`/`sideEffectScope`/`needsApproval`/`stopTurnOnSuccess` read by plan mode + scheduler + approval gate, replacing xdev's three parallel name-keyed tables and closing parity T3 #27/#28's fail-open plan list); **compaction needs a breaker** (`MAX_CONSECUTIVE_RAPID_REFILLS = 3` — compaction that does not buy space is a failure mode, and xdev counts nothing); **read-only tools can run during the stream** (gated on the manifest, with an end-of-stream fallback and preserved results on a mid-stream model failure); **a stream read must be abortable while the provider is wedged** (raced against the turn signal, with an attempt-escalated idle window) alongside xdev's source-side liveness judgement — both properties, one watchdog; **the prompt must state its own contract** (mid-turn text may be invisible; compaction is coming, do not wrap up early; how permissions/hooks/system turns behave). §3.2/§3.3/§3.4/§3.6/§3.9 carry the deltas inline; §5.5 records the verified-and-rejected items too (no SQLite session store, no AI SDK, no hard file ceiling).
*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (fix/tool-capabilities-420 — #420 tool capability manifests): one `tool.Caps` declaration (`internal/tool/caps.go`) replaces the parallel name tables. `planReadOnlyTools` is gone; plan mode fails closed on undeclared tools via `AllowedInPlan`. `Classify` still names only the core four so `Decide`'s TierExec default for dynamic tools is unchanged. `ConcurrentOK` is the stream-time / scheduler gate for #421. Table-diff + undeclared fail-closed tests pin the behaviour.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (fix/sidebar-title — the dock's title slot wraps instead of clipping): a user-reported defect — a long session title ended in "…" in the context dock's top slot, so the one string that names the conversation could not be read whole. `drawDock` painted `dockClip(a.dockTitle())`, a hard 38-cell cut with no second row. The slot now wraps: `dockState.titleLines` holds the session's name wrapped to the panel's interior (`dockTitleLines`, reusing the package's `wrap`, sanitizing through the same `sanitizeOutput` the rest of the panel uses), `titleRows()` is the geometry every row index below the slot shifts by (`layout`'s budget, `drawDock`, `dockRowAt`, `selDockRowsForPaint`), and the content budget is charged those rows so the panel never paints a section over its own name. `dockTitleMax` (3) bounds what the name may cost the work it names; past the cap the last row carries the clip's "…" again, so a title that is too long even wrapped still admits the cut rather than silently dropping text. Regression tests: `TestDockTitleWrapsNotClips` (a 66-cell title takes 2 rows, row 0 carries no "…", every row fits `dockInner`, and the first section still starts under the slot on screen) and `TestDockTitleBoundedAndClickMapFollows` (a 1000-cell title stops at the cap with the "…" back, the sections keep their rows, and the click map follows the shifted rows).*
*Last updated: 2026-09-21 (feat/settings-overlay — `Alt+,` opens a settings panel): the TUI's settings surface was a listing (`/settings`) plus two one-off toggles, so a user who wanted to change what the panel showed had to leave the session and run `xdev config set`. `Alt+,` (and `/settings overlay`) now opens a modal panel over the composer: a category tab strip, one row per setting with its live value, `↑↓`/`jk` to move, `Enter` to change the row under the cursor, `Tab`/`←→` to switch category, `r` to re-read from disk, click to select-and-change, `q`/`Esc` to close. Rows are `toggle` (● on / ○ off in the success/error accents) or `select` (cycles its options); a change is persisted to the global layer through the same `config.Set` path `xdev config set` uses — so a bad value fails in the panel instead of quarantining the config on the next start — and the ones the running session can apply live (`showThinking`, `sidebarMode`, `debugMouse`) take effect on the spot, exactly what `/settings showThinking` and `Alt+S` already did. `thinking` goes through the level ladder behind `ThinkingOps.Set`, so a level set in the panel and one set by `/thinking` cannot disagree about the wire. `keyName` now names punctuation, because `A-,` arrives as ESC + a comma and a chord the runtime cannot resolve is a listed binding that does nothing. Tests: `internal/tui/settings_test.go` (toggle applies and persists; the panel paints its rows; a category tab moves the highlight *and* the action together; `r` picks up an external write; the default chord resolves; an unreadable boolean is never painted "off"; a squeezed window keeps the selection inside the painted rows). Driven end to end against the built binary: `Alt+,` paints the panel, `Enter` writes `showThinking: false` to the config file, and the next process reads it back. `internal/tui/settings.go` (new), `internal/tui/app.go`, `internal/tui/commands.go`, `internal/tui/keymap.go`, `cmd/xdev/tui.go`, README §TUI.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-21 (fix/retry-delay-overflow — the crash after a long retry or thinking loop): `RetryPolicy.delay` computed `base * time.Duration(1<<uint(n-1))`, and the counters that feed `n` (`escalation`, `emptyRetries`) are unbounded because `retry.infinite` and `retry.retryAllErrors` are both default-on. At n = 36 with the default 500ms base the multiply overflows int64 into a negative duration, the `MaxDelay` clamp cannot catch it, and the 25% jitter hands that negative to `rand.Int64N` — **panic: invalid argument to Int64N**, which kills the process and takes the TUI with it. `n >= 57` wrapped the same multiply to zero, which `sleepBackoff` reads as "nothing to wait for" — the busy-spin half of the same bug. The exponential now saturates by doubling up to `MaxDelay` (or `BaseDelay` when a policy sets no cap), so every `n` returns a positive backoff at or below the cap; `TestDelayCannotOverflowAtUnboundedRounds` fails on the old code at n = 36 with the exact panic.
*Last updated: 2026-09-21 (fix/handle-mouse-deadlock — the dock FILES click froze the whole TUI): `handleMouse`'s non-wheel branch in `app.go` runs the entire gesture with `a.mu` held, and the diff overlay's click helpers took `a.mu` again underneath it: `closeDiffOverlayOnClick` → `closeDiffOverlay` (and `dockToggleDiff`), which wedged the UI thread — keys, drawing and the quit chord all dead, process alive, exactly the "xdev froze" report. The state patch is now split from the lock: unexported `closeDiffOverlayLocked` does the work for callers that already hold the lock, `closeDiffOverlay` keeps its own lock for the callers that do not. `openDiffOverlay` was already lock-free (documented in place so it stays that way). Pinned by `TestDiffOverlayClickCannotDeadlock` and `TestDiffOverlayCloseIsSafeUnderTheLock`, both with a 5s watchdog: the first fails on the pre-fix code at the click, and the third helper (`dockToggleDiff`'s Esc chord, reached through `handleKey`) is covered by the second. Found while fixing #435; the 2026-09-20 11:13 stall dump had goroutine 1 parked on exactly this lock at `dock.go:859`.
*Last updated: 2026-09-21 (fix/bash-tui-crash — the "xdev crash when running bash" report): a session whose `bash` call took seconds longer than usual read as a crash because the UI loop froze and recovered without leaving a trace. Two defects, each with a test that fails against the old line:

1. **A recovered slow iteration left no evidence.** The UI-loop watchdog (`internal/tui/stall.go`, c3985bc) writes `dumps/tui-stall-<ts>.txt` only for a loop that never comes back — every dump on disk is that shape. The reported freeze always recovered (the user typed "continue config" afterwards), so nothing recorded it, and 14 dumps from the same morning document only the never-returning kind. `Run`'s loop now closes each iteration through `beatDone(phase, start)`: past `slowIteration` (shipped 10s) it writes `xdev: UI loop iteration took 45.2s (event)` on stderr, naming the phase (`event` = key handling, `dirty` = repaint request, `tick` = background frame) so the next report is a diagnosis rather than a symptom. The heartbeat is unchanged, so the dump and the line stay complementary. `TestBeatDoneNamesASlowIteration` pins exactly one line per slow iteration, the named phase, and the beating.

2. **The bash call row could paint a torn rune.** `toolDetail` (`internal/tui/blocks.go`) is the one render path with no `sanitizeOutput` behind it — its return goes straight into `SetContent` — and it truncated the naming argument at a raw byte 400. For any non-ASCII command (a Vietnamese or CJK `command`/`input` argument: `len("ệ")` is 3 bytes) that cut lands inside a rune and emits a lone continuation byte, in a row every consumer below assumes is runes (width math, `wrap`, the terminal). The 400-byte window now cuts on a rune boundary (`runeBoundary`) and the decoded field is passed through `utf8Only` first; invalid bytes are dropped rather than replaced with U+FFFD, because the replacement char is one cell wide where the torn bytes were zero and a row whose width is a lie paints its box border in the wrong column. The unparseable-args fallback is sanitized too — that path had the identical defect, and `TestToolDetailNeverReturnsTornUTF8` drives both plus the second truncation in `toolSummary` at four widths.

Verified: `go build ./...` clean, `gofmt` clean on all five files, `go test ./internal/tui/` passes. Both new tests fail against `git stash`ed HEAD. The one `internal/tool` failure (`TestSecurityScanNoGoModule`) reproduces on a clean tree — a missing `go` on the child `PATH`, unrelated. Recorded honestly: the user's own freeze left no trace and is not reproduced here; this wave ships what will name it, not a manufactured root cause. The prime suspect remains the documented one at `draw()` (a synchronous `Show()` flush blocking on a suspended tty, #283), which is exactly a recovering multi-second iteration.

*Last updated: 2026-09-23 (fix/bashshell-crash follow-up — repeated quit chords no longer crash xdev): the TUI can receive two Ctrl+C bytes in one terminal burst. `Run` drains the already-queued keys before it returns to its select, so the first quit closes `quitCh` and the second reaches `App.Quit` again; the unguarded `close` then panicked with `close of closed channel` and killed the whole process. `Quit` is now idempotent via `sync.Once`, pinned by `TestRepeatedQuitChordDoesNotPanic`. This is a process-exit defect, distinct from the earlier recovered slow bash/UI iteration report in `fix/bash-tui-crash`; no shell command or secret is involved.
*Last updated: 2026-09-24 (fix/tui-clickable-urls — assistant Markdown URLs open from the terminal): Markdown rendered explicit HTTP(S) links without their destination and did not autolink bare URLs, so a visible answer could not be clicked. The renderer now keeps each target on the painted run (including headings, emphasis, wrapped/table layouts, balanced destinations, and bare URLs); `paint` publishes per-frame hit rectangles; the existing primary-button gesture opens one only on press+release with no drag and revalidates the target against the current frame. Modal overlays mask hidden transcript links, Shift-drag and drag selection are unchanged, timestamps cannot steal a hit, and opener children are reaped asynchronously. `go test ./internal/tui/` and `go vet ./internal/tui/` pass; CGO-free Linux/amd64, Darwin/arm64, and Windows/amd64 TUI test binaries compile. The two repository-wide `cmd/xdev` failures are environment-dependent and unrelated (a local credential changes the connect picker; global skills defeat the empty-repo fixture).

*Last updated: 2026-09-24 (fix/opaque-400-retry): an onegw/OpenCode stream-start HTTP 400 whose body only said `Upstream request failed: [invalid_request_error] invalid request` was classified as `ClassBadRequest` but then fell through the recovery switch's default branch; with the shipped `retry.infinite` policy this replayed the same rejected request indefinitely and printed the misleading `all targets down` notice. `oneTurnWithRecovery` now returns `ClassBadRequest` directly, preserving the provider error without retrying it; recognized recoverable 400s (missing field, tool-name length, explicit upstream refusal) remain transient. Regression coverage: `TestBadRequestMissingOutputRetriesInsteadOfEndingTheRun` and `TestOpaqueBadRequestDoesNotUseAllTargetsDownNotice` in `internal/agent/emptyoutput_test.go`; focused `go test ./internal/agent ./internal/ai ./internal/config -count=1` and `go vet` pass. The full suite retains the two pre-existing `cmd/xdev` failures: `TestConnectPickerItems` and `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills`.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-24 (fix/retry-health-check — the pre-turn provider health check bypassed the recovery ladder): a refused connection at `127.0.0.1:8080/v1/models` exhausted the fallback chain and returned immediately with `agent: health check failed and all targets drained`, even though stream transport errors already used `retry.infinite`. Health-check failures now fail over first, then use an independent bounded/infinite health-check retry budget with cancellable backoff, visible round notices, and fallback-cooldown reversion. Tests: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/agent/...`, `gofmt`, `go test ./internal/agent -count=1`, focused repeated tests, and `go test -race ./internal/agent -count=1`; a live local API outage/recovery interaction completed successfully. The full `go test ./...` run retains two unrelated host-environment-dependent `cmd/xdev` failures; both pass with an isolated `HOME`/`XDEV_AGENT_DIR`.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-27 (`perf/agent-turn-cost`): three changes against the measured turn-count tax in `research/2026-09-15-xdev-slow-session-rca.md` and `research/2026-09-15-omp-vs-xdev-speed-bench.html` (per-turn cost is at parity — 8.5s vs 9.1s — so the gap is turns, not speed). (1) §1 Goal 4's prompt was 685 tokens against its own 1,000 budget, and the slack was spent on nothing: one ignored "use read not cat" line (bash:read ran 15:1 vs omp's 3.9:1) and **no delegation guidance at all**, for a harness already carrying `maxBatchParallel=8`, five agent definitions and depth-2 nesting. `SystemPromptBase` gains two scoped sections (context-gathering, delegating) and now measures 854 tokens — still inside Goal 4, with 146 tokens of headroom left so `maxPromptTokens` keeps biting. `TestBasePromptCarriesToolDiscipline` pins each rule, and every needle is verified absent from the pre-change prompt. The task tool's description gains the same constraints, and its schema now advertises the `{context, tasks[]}` batch shape that has been parsed since parity finding T3 #1 but was invisible — `required:["prompt"]` additionally made a batch unsendable on strict providers. (2) MCP tools were un-deferrable, so a connected server's full schema rode every request; `mcpclient.Register` now defers past `DeferThreshold = 12`, leaving small surfaces untouched, and indexes each tool with its server name as a search tag. (3) `part of #296`: a background subagent's result now reaches an idle parent — `Hub.AddSettleListener` is additive alongside the dock's `SetNotify`, and `StartHubNoticeDelivery` reuses the schedule-reminder shape (idle gate, claim the turn, persist to the store, start the turn) because `Run` takes `history` by value and an idle session has no Agent to drain a queue. New: `internal/agent/hubnotice.go`, 2 test files (12 tests). `go build`, `go vet`, `gofmt` (changed files), `go test -count=1 ./...` and `go test -race -count=1 ./...` pass except the two pre-existing `cmd/xdev` failures noted above; cross-compiles for all six linux/darwin/windows × amd64/arm64 targets with `CGO_ENABLED=0`. Ceiling: the notice is TUI-only, and #296's addressing half (`list_agents`, children messaging each other) is still open.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-28 (`fix/confirm-first` — the agent started implementing before confirming the requirement, even when the prompt already said to fix it). Field report: session `df12bbc8` in x-trader, on the prompt *"Find the root causes and fix why the jev decisions held at gates · EDGE_HURDLE_UNREACHABLE"*, answered with 12 turns of `read`/`grep`/`curl` across four files before anyone stated what the request was read to mean — and the model's own first thought was that the repo was xdev, not the repo the session was in. The rule already existed, but only in the global `~/.xdev/agent/AGENTS.md` context file, which is injected under a hard cap and framed as "not authoritative" — a convention that lives only there gets truncated and discounted. The fix moves it where it binds: a `Before you act:` block in `SystemPromptBase` (`internal/agent/prompt.go`), naming the triggers (ambiguous, non-trivial, spans repos, touches files outside the working directory), the mechanism (`ask`, not a prose question), and the boundary (a clear explicit instruction is its own confirmation; one confirmation covers the run). Deliberately a prompt rule, not the `write` approval gate — the default stays YOLO per §1 Goal 4, and the shipped approval modes are untouched. Base prompt 1,907 runes against its own 2,000 ceiling; `TestBasePromptCarriesConfirmFirst` is the guard, with every needle verified absent from the pre-change prompt. `go build`, `go vet`, `gofmt` (changed files), `go test -count=1 ./internal/agent ./internal/tool` and `go test -count=1 ./cmd/xdev -run TestBundledPrompt` pass; `cmd/xdev` still has the one pre-existing `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills` failure, unchanged by this branch.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-28 (`fix/bash-row-pairing` — the TUI showed no output for a bash call that DID run: two `bash` calls in one batch, finishing out of order, swapped results). Field report: session `1f32044b`, where every turn emitted a pair of `bash` calls (the model reads and shells in parallel) and the transcript boxes read as "the command never ran". Root cause: `internal/tui.FinishTool` identified a tool row by TOOL NAME only — the newest running row with that name — while `agent.runTools` executes same-batch calls concurrently (`MaxToolWorkers=6`, `internal/agent/loop.go`), so whichever result returned first closed the second call's row: one command's output painted under the other command's name, and the row still spinning over the swapped output reads as a hang. The provider's call id was already on the hook (`ai.ToolCallBlock.ID`, which `cmd/xdev/acp.go` uses and the TUI dropped). Fix: `Block.CallID` + `AddToolBlock`/`FinishTool` take the call id and match on it (name only as the fallback for idless callers, so replayed history and bang mode keep pairing and a replay cannot steal a live row); bang runs get their own `bang-N` id; the status row's "● bash" indicator becomes a depth counter (`BeginActiveCommand`/`EndActiveCommand`) so the first of two concurrent calls to finish no longer clears it. Pinned by `TestConcurrentSameNamedCallsPairByCallID`, `TestIdlessFinishDoesNotStealALiveRow` and `TestActiveCommandIsACounter` in `internal/tui/tool_row_pairing_test.go`; `go build`, `go vet`, `gofmt` (changed files) and `go test -count=1 ./internal/tui` pass, and `cmd/xdev` still has the two pre-existing failures (`TestConnectPickerItems`, `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills`), unchanged by this branch.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-28 (`perf/mcp-autostart-off-thread` — the autoStart health probe and launch wait were the last blocking network+subprocess step left on the TUI startup path). `attachMCP` deferred the MCP connect to `go finishMCP` but ran the `autoStart` probe inline, before `app.Run()`: a local server that was down cost an HTTP probe plus a 500ms poll loop of up to `healthTimeoutSec` (30s on the leankg recipe in `<dataDir>/mcp.yml`) of an undrawn screen. The probe now runs inside `finishMCP`, so it lands on the same background goroutine as the connect it gates; `print` mode, which passes `wait=true` and needs the tools before the first turn, is unchanged. Covered by `TestAttachMCPDoesNotHealthProbeOnTheCallersGoroutine` (`cmd/xdev/mcpautostart_test.go`), which fails at 8s against the old inline path and passes in 0.00s now.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-28 (perf/loop-cheap-wins — three bounds the loop did not have, ported from a mechanic-by-mechanic read of the deepseek-harness reference at `21638c56`; full gap analysis in [research/2026-09-28-deepseek-harness-gap.md](research/2026-09-28-deepseek-harness-gap.md)):* a read of the TypeScript reference produced 3.9k lines of file:line evidence (session core, loop, tools…[+342b]
A model that has stopped making progress usually looks like one thing: the same tool, the same arguments, several turns running — each costing a full model round trip to re-derive a conclusion it already had. dsh's `guard/repeat-tool-reminder` (`src/index.ts:191-211`) counts that and injects a notice; it never vetoes, because a guard that blocks a call the model genuinely needs is worse tha…[+101b]
Three details carry the value, and all three are cheaper in Go than they were in TypeScript:
1. **Key order is not identity.** dsh hand-writes a deep key-sort (`sortJsonValue`) to canonicalize arguments. Go's `json.Marshal` sorts map keys itself, so the whole function is the standard library. What stdlib does *not* do is preserve numeric fidelity, so the decoder uses `UseNumber`: without it `12345678901234567890` decodes to `1.2345678901234567e+19` and two calls with genuinely differ…[+174b]
2. **Counting happens where the result is built**, so a *denied* call counts too. A model hammering a denied call is the loop most worth breaking, and a counter that only saw successes would miss it.
3. **A steering message ends the chain.** Repetition around a new human instruction is compliance, not a loop; the chain resets at the drain (`loop.go`, the step-boundary seam) rather than at the guard.
The chain advances in **model order, not worker-finish order**: `runTools` fans out to a bounded pool and joins, then the guard runs single-goroutine over the committed results. A batch whose workers finished out of order must still count as the model wrote it — that ordering is the reason the guard lives at the loop boundary instead of inside `runOneTool`.
Thresholds are dsh's `[3, 5, 8]`: the first is a gentle nudge, later ones name the tool, the run length, and the arguments. The quote is capped at 500 chars (`argumentsPreviewChars` upstream) because a `write` body is a plausible repeat argument and quoting it whole would spend the context the notice exists to protect — the cap bounds what the model *reads*, never what is *compared*, so det…[+21b]
Also settled in this wave, from checking the reference directly: **dsh does no latency engineering at all.** Zero `preconnect`/`keepAlive`/`warmup` in `packages/llm`, no `coalesc*` anywhere in the streaming path (the hits are all in `packages/experimental/`), no partial-JSON tool dispatch, and a three-line `BENCHMARK.md` that points at a Python SDK guide. What it optimizes is *turn count* —…[+672b]
**Verified:** `go build ./...` clean, `gofmt` clean, `go vet` clean, `go test` green for `internal/agent`, `internal/session`, `internal/ai/...`, `internal/tui`. `TestLoopNoticesARepeatedToolCall` was confirmed to fail with the loop wiring removed (0 notices over 5 identical calls).

*Last updated: 2026-09-28 (perf/repeat-call-guard — the next cheap win from the same dsh read, now that the latency question is settled):*
*Last updated: 2026-09-28 (docs: DeepSeek Harness deep-dive — [research/deepseek-harness-deepdive.md](research/deepseek-harness-deepdive.md)):* the 2026-09-14 dsh study was re-run at primary source, 2,612 commits later, across eight dimensions with `path:line` on both sides. **Four claims in [research/dsh-internals.md](research/dsh-internals.md) are corrected** (§0.2): xdev has *no* turn/s…[+1625b]
*Last updated: 2026-09-28 (docs: DeepSeek Harness deep-dive — [research/deepseek-harness-deepdive.md](research/deepseek-harness-deepdive.md)):* the 2026-09-14 dsh study was re-run at primary source, 2,612 commits later, across eight dimensions with `path:line` on both sides. **Four claims in [research/dsh-internals.md](research/dsh-internals.md) are corrected** (§0.2): xdev has *no* turn/step boundary, not a partial one; dsh is at `SESSION_FORMAT_VERSION = 4` with a published migration ladder where xdev is flat v3; dsh still specifies no default approval behavior for shell/edit; and **dsh ships no terminal UI at all** — React SPA + Electron, with no `ink`/`@opentui`/`react-reconciler` dependency in any `package.json` and no `*tui*` package. Five findings change a stated design assumption: (1) dsh's typed turn lifecycle is the precondition for crash repair, max-tokens-as-turn-reason and a real status read — xdev infers "interrupted" by sniffing a 32 KiB tail; (2) dsh's confinement is OS-level behind one canonical writable-root derivation, xdev's is policy-only, which is the one place xdev is genuinely *behind* on safety rather than differently shaped; (3) the two front-ends are not comparable products, so the UI comparison bounds to what the surfaces expose, not to the surfaces; (4) dsh's registry validates tool arguments at the boundary, xdev's does not; (5) xdev's `ConcurrentSafe` table is recorded but never consulted by the executor, so a mutate-then-read batch can interleave. Five ports proposed in dependency order (§9.2): turn/step lifecycle → crash repair → durable `request/header` → sandbox + canonical roots → system-prompt registry. xdev's advantages re-affirmed and kept in the PRD: the deferred tool catalog, four-way turn bounding, evidence-gated goal completion, emitted prompt-cache markers, the entry tree + rewind, the hard plan-mode guard, and telemetry-free-by-policy. Rejected with reasons (§9.4): the in-process plugin VM (re-confirmed), dsh's web/desktop surfaces (a new product, not a port), SQLite-as-transcript, and dsh's provider-gated `defer_loading`. Docs-only; nothing was executed — no harness ran, no test suite ran.

*Last updated: 2026-09-28 (`fix/tui-metric-measure` — the HUD's token and throughput numbers reported values they did not measure). Field report: "token in, out, the throughput (t/s) looks like it run showing wrong number". Six defects, all confirmed by driving the real mutators, not by reading them.

1. **The decode window leaked across turns** (the one that made `⚡ t/s` read low and stay low). `deltaFirst/deltaLast` were cleared by `AddUsage` (i.e. a `EventDone` that carries usage) and by `SetRunning(true)`, which is per *run* (`cmd/xdev/tui.go`), not per turn. A turn that died into the recovery ladder emits `EventError`, never `EventDone`, so its window stayed open and became the next turn's denominator: measured 1000 tokens over a 400 ms message dividing a 511 ms window inherited from a dead turn, 1957 t/s instead of ~2500, with the error growing with the dead turn's length. Every provider emits exactly one `EventStart` per message, which is the boundary the hook layer can rely on — `App.BeginMessage` now closes the window there, wired from `tuiHooks.OnEvent`.
2. **Tool-call argument tokens were in the numerator but not the denominator.** `noteDelta` was wired to text and thinking only, and `tuiHooks.OnEvent` had no `EventToolcallDelta` case, so the window stopped at the last text delta while `output_tokens` counted every byte of argument JSON: 30k tokens over a 204 ms text window reported 147,333 t/s, a physically impossible decode rate. `App.NoteToolDelta` extends the window without counting runes (a `PartialJSON` is an accumulated snapshot, not the new bytes).
3. **A tool-only turn measured nothing and kept the previous turn's number.** No text deltas means an empty window, the `out > 1 && window >= 100ms` guard rejects it, and the row kept showing e.g. `⚡ 1982.0 t/s` from a different message. `AddUsage` now clears `Rate` when a message cannot be measured, and the segment hides — the same rule the segment's own comment already claimed ("never measured is never displayed") but did not implement.
4. **Two different formulas for one quantity.** While a run was live the segment showed `liveRate()` — `runes/4` over the window — which measured 117.6 t/s against the provider's 587.9 t/s on the same message, a 5x gap, and the segment swapped between the two mid-turn. The rune heuristic is gone: one formula, one source, the provider's own count over the message's own window.
5. **`Reset` did not reset the session counters.** `/new`, `/clear`, `/resume`, `/fork` and tree navigation all go through it; it cleared `Work` and `CtxUsed` and left `↑ ↓ ⚡ ⌚ $` behind, so a switched-to session drew the previous session's totals beside its freshly replayed transcript, and a new session opened showing a `⚡` it had never measured. `Reset` is now the session boundary for every counter.
6. **Gemini reported usage in a different shape from the other three providers** (`internal/ai/google_genai.go`). `promptTokenCount` *includes* `cachedContentTokenCount` ("this is still the total effective prompt size meaning this includes the number of tokens in the cached content") and the field was never decoded, so `↑` counted a 30k cached prefix as fresh input while `/usage` and the `⇢cache` label always read 0; `candidatesTokenCount` excludes `thoughtsTokenCount` while `totalTokenCount` is prompt+thoughts+candidates, so `↓` omitted the thinking and the rate divided a numerator that had none of it. `googleUsage.toUsage` normalizes onto the contract Anthropic and OpenAI already keep.

**Not changed, deliberately:** `anthropic.go:538` (`TotalTokens = Input + Output + CacheRead + CacheWrite`) looks like a double-count and is not — Anthropic's `input_tokens` is non-cached input, and the docs' own formula is `total_input_tokens = cache_read + cache_creation + input_tokens` (worked example: 100,000 read + 50 input = 100,050). Tests: `internal/tui/metric_measure_test.go` (5 cases, one per defect above) and `internal/ai/google_usage_test.go` (4 normalization cases). The two `cmd/xdev` failures (`TestConnectPickerItems`, `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills`) reproduce on a clean `origin/main` checkout — a credential-picker ordering and an ambient skills directory, both unrelated.


Three user-reported TUI defects land together, all of them "the surface exists but the pointer does not reach it".

**(1) The diff viewer's inside bar painted a colour the terminal had not chosen.** `drawDiffOverlay` filled its panel with `th.Get(theme.BgBase)`, and `Theme.Get` cannot say "the terminal decides": a theme that leaves `bg_base` blank (the documented way to inherit the terminal's own background) is stored as an explicit black, so the overlay painted `#000000` over whatever scheme was running. It now resolves the slot the way `drawDock` always has — `th.Slot(theme.BgBase)`, falling back to `tcell.ColorDefault` (SGR 49) when the theme leaves it to the terminal — so the overlay's inside matches the sidebar's surface, and a theme that DOES name `bg_base` still gets its own. `TestDiffOverlayInsideIsTheTerminalBackground` fails with `#000000` on the old line.

**(2) The sidebar is a window of its own.** A click anywhere in the panel's columns focuses THAT window: the transcript's hit-tests (the user-message menu, the reasoning-box aim, a link) no longer reach across and claim a cell the panel painted, and the press anchors on screen rows so a drag over the panel copies the panel's own text rather than the transcript row it covers. The panel's own clicks work: a FILES row still opens the diff, and the TRAJECTORY button row now runs the action `dockRowAt` reported — `dockAct` had been dead since the diff-overlay click fix (`eccf354`) rewrote the press branch, so the one panel row that names a surface did nothing the eye could see. The wheel and the keyboard are untouched: the panel is chrome, and what it gained is the mouse.

**(3) Click-to-user-message reverts like the history tree.** The ❯ menu's `revert` is now `/tree`'s user-row rewind — the leaf moves to the message's parent, the transcript is replayed, and the prompt comes back as the composer draft — through the same `NavigateTree` seam, so the two ways of reaching a point in the conversation cannot disagree. `fork` was that same rewind under a second name, so it folded in: the menu is three rows (jump / revert / copy) and `revert`'s hint says the prompt comes back. What a revert still cannot do is put files back, and the notice still says so.

Found while driving the built binary for the above: **`reg.MCPNames` is nil whenever no MCP server is configured, and the dock's MCP source called it unconditionally** — so `xdev` panicked with a nil-pointer dereference on the first frame of every run without MCP configured (the common case; reproduced on `origin/main` and on the pushed release binary). The closure now checks the field first.

**Verified:** `go build ./...` and `go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./...` green except `TestConnectPickerItems` and `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills`, both failing identically on `origin/main` (a credential row in the local keychain and the developer's own `~/.ai-tools` skills); `go test -race ./internal/tui/` green. The new behaviour is covered twice: by session tests, and by `internal/tui/live_e2e_test.go` (`-tags e2e`), which drives the same gestures through `handleKey` — the UI loop's own entry point — so a wrapping bug fails there even when the unit test passes. The built binary was also driven through a real pty against a local OpenAI-compatible server: the panel paints, the two turns land, and the click paths behave as the tests claim.

*Last updated: 2026-09-28 (fix/sidebar-mouse-msgmenu — the diff overlay's inside bar, the sidebar as its own mouse window, and click-to-user-message rewind as the tree rewind):*

*Last updated: 2026-10-03 (fix/full-command-and-title — user request: "always display the full command in bash TUI no matter how long it is, same for the sidebar session title").* Two "show it whole" cuts, both decided by the same rule: the string a person opened the transcript to read is never shortened, and it pays in rows.

1. **The call row shows the whole command.** `toolDetail` (`internal/tui/blocks.go`) still had a first-line cut — `strings.Cut(s, "\n")` plus a `" …"` when the argument continued — so a multi-line bash command or a heredoc showed its opening line and a promise. It now returns the naming field entire, for every tool (`command`, `path`, `input`, …), via `collapseLines`: horizontal whitespace collapses within each line, line breaks survive as line breaks. The row already WRAPS this (`blockLines`, `case KindTool` — continuations indented to the phrase column, each its own `selRow`, so a drag copies the command as painted), so a second line costs a row, not its text. The `utf8Only` drop stays first, because a torn rune is still the one thing this render path cannot survive. The 400-byte window is long gone and stays gone. Painted test: `TestToolDetailKeepsEveryLine` asserts the heredoc's four lines all reach the screen (`TestToolDetailReturnsTheWholeCommand` is the 5000-byte case).

2. **The sidebar's name is uncapped.** `dockTitleMax = 3` was the last thing cutting the session name (`fix/sidebar-title`, #434), left from when a long title was a *hard* 38-cell clip: it wrapped, but still stopped at 3 rows with a `…`. `dockTitleLines` now bounds the name by the BAND (`bandH - dockTitleFloor`, `dockTitleFloor = 4` — the rows the sections keep), so a 1000-cell name wraps over ~26 rows of a 40-row panel and every section still has its rows; only a name longer than the panel itself is cut, and that cut is admitted with a `…` rather than silent. Deliberate trade, per the request: **the title wins the panel** — a first prompt long enough to fill the sidebar leaves nothing under it. `dockLayout`'s budget already reported what a cut cost (`+N more`, the hidden-rows footnote), so the sections below still announce what the name took. Tests: `TestDockTitleTakesTheRowsItNeeds` (no cap, sections survive), `TestDockTitleLongerThanTheBandIsAdmitted` (an 8-row terminal still cuts, and says so), `TestDockTitleWrapsNotClips` (the 2026-09-22 case, unchanged and still true).

**Verified:** `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/tui/` clean; `go test ./internal/tui/` green (19s, whole package). `go test ./...` green except `TestConnectPickerItems` and `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills`, which fail identically on a stashed tree — a credential row in the local keychain and the developer's own `~/.ai-tools` skills, not this change.

## 7. Issue triage (2026-09-29)

A full pass over every open issue at `origin/main` @ `3c44ec5` — 186 open issues, each one traced
to a real implementation site (or proven absent) by reading the code, not the PRD. Verdicts:
**4 done** (closed), **40 partial**, **142 open**. Every open issue now carries exactly one
priority label, assigned impact-first rather than by milestone: P0 = live data loss, a
security/privacy hole, or a wedge; P1 = a gap in the core daily driver (agent loop, TUI
correctness, context/compaction, permissions, provider/auth); P2 = parity, UX or packaging
breadth a daily user rarely hits. The `m*`/`parity`/`enhancement` milestone labels are untouched.

### 7.1 Closed as done

| Issue | What proved it |
|---|---|
| [#83](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/83) | `cmd/xdev/sessionops.go:477-529` `branchSummarizer()` resolves the session model into a callable; `internal/config/settings.go:373-376,818-835` ships the `branchSummary.{enabled,reserveTokens}` block; `internal/agent/compact_snap.go:38-47` vision-gates snapcompact onto `snapcompactText`; `internal/agent/compact_ladder.go:176-187` records the remote rung as a logged no-op. |
| [#102](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/102) | `cmd/xdev/main.go:275-276` attaches `config.InstallID()`; `internal/ai/anthropic.go:114-120,157-159` sends `metadata.user_id`; `internal/ai/codex_responses.go:29-31` + `openai_responses.go:53,127` send `installation_id`; `internal/serve/broker.go:486-493` no longer mints a second id. |
| [#315](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/315) | `internal/tui/app.go:3395-3397` paints `drawSelection()` last, and :3196-3199 paints it on the welcome branch too; regression test `internal/tui/selection_test.go:172`. |
| [#374](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/374) | `internal/tui/app.go:3378-3382` publishes `selDockRows`, `internal/tui/dock.go:799-814` maps panel rows to screen coordinates, `internal/tui/selection.go:784-791` routes them into the shared selection path. |

### 7.2 P0 — data loss, security, wedges

- **#161 — no non-approvable floors.** `internal/tool/approval.go:70-79,82` ships yolo as the default, so `rm -rf ~` needs no prompt, `internal/tool/policy.go:265-296` keeps a quoted `$( )` opaque (so deny rules miss it), and `internal/tool/integrity.go:96-121` protects harness state only — no critical-path, no rc/config, no out-of-workspace read floor.
- **#81 — injected content is never scanned.** `internal/agent/prompt.go` reads `AGENTS.md`-class files and web/browser/MCP output into the prompt verbatim; no scanner, no `<untrusted_tool_result>` wrapping, despite §5 recording the stance.

### 7.3 P1 — core daily driver (42, one line each)

**Agent loop / context:** #84, #115, #116, #137, #141, #168, #178, #216, #243, #284, #299, #421, #422, #424, #430.
**Permissions / approvals / secrets:** #94, #129, #145, #160, #180, #218, #295, #298, #426, #427.
**Providers / MCP / auth:** #150, #153, #262, #263.
**TUI / session UX:** #86, #131, #133, #138, #157, #158, #199, #231, #232, #271, #296.

Three P1s are worth naming because a fix is already half-landed and the remainder is the load-bearing half: **#298** (children are still refused by a nil approver — `cmd/xdev/print.go:1844-1888` registers `TaskTool` with a `Policy` and no `Approve`), **#427** (plan mode denies by default via `internal/tool/caps.go:86-97`, but the model is still handed the full mutating toolset and a reviewer-less host still auto-accepts), **#295** (`AGENTS.md` now reaches children, yet `internal/agent/subagent.go:216-227` gives them no redactor, no compaction, no derived plan mode).

### 7.4 P2 — parity and breadth (142)

Everything else: parity sweeps, LSP/plugin/ACP depth, slash-command and chrome breadth, docs and
CI contracts, and the M14/M15 tail. Prioritised inside P2 by the triage evidence, not by milestone.

### 7.5 Partial (40) — keep open, fix the named half

*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (interactive TUI audit — [QA-TUI-INTERACTIVE.md](QA-TUI-INTERACTIVE.md)): the built binary driven through a real pty with injected SGR-1006 mouse bytes and named keys, found eight defects in the mouse + keybinding surface and fixed four of them, each with a test that fails on `fa37024`. **`C-r` was advertised by `/hotkeys` as `history-prev` and dispatched nothing** — `Editor.HandleKey` has no `KeyCtrlR` case and neither action switch in `app.go` listed the action, so `Editor.HistoryPrev()` (editor.go:215) was called by nothing; one case restores it. **A press in the transcript columns fired the sidebar's action** — `selection.go`'s press branch called `dockRowAt(x, y)`, which answers by row and never by x, with no `dockAt` guard while the two lines above it had one; the guard now lives in `dockRowAt` itself, so both callers (and `closeDiffOverlayOnClick`) get it. **The settings overlay's category tabs answered only the keyboard**, and the painter and the click hit-test each computed the strip's layout independently and disagreed by the leading space and the separator width; the painter now publishes the columns (`settingsOverlayState.tabCells`) and the hit-test reads them. **A click on a read-only settings row was a silent no-op**; it now answers on the composer's notice slot with the key that can change it. Still open with evidence in the report: a held drag parked on the bottom edge at the tail (the mechanism works at the top — a one-sided gap, not a break), `NO_COLOR` collapsing every themed colour to reverse video with no warning, `C-c`/`C-d` quitting on an unsent draft with no confirmation, and sub-3-row terminals painting status fragments over the composer. Two harness traps are recorded there because they silently invalidate mouse work: `tmux -f /dev/null` hands the pane `TERM=dumb` (every "the theme is broken" observation from that setup is an artefact), and an SGR wheel byte in release form arrives as `ButtonNone`, so a wheel test built on it tests nothing.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`fix/quit-exit-chord` — the quit chord had no way out while a turn was running). User report: "xdev cannot exit, ctrl+c / ctrl+d do not exit the session", read against session `2750b48c` (299 records, a live prompt-continuation chain). The `quit` action dispatched on `if running { a.onCancel(); return }` — **twice** in `handleKey` (`app.go:2500` and `:2694`) —…[+1178b]

**Verified live, both arms, through a real pty.** A local gateway that answers `/models` and then never answers a `chat/completions` POST keeps `st.Running` true for as long as the pane lives; the built binary was driven in tmux (`xdev tui`, `XDEV_AGENT_DIR` scratch config, prompt sent, then one chord and a 5s wait). On `origin/main` (80f2380) one `C-c` leaves the pane alive on a fresh composer — `exit_marker=0`, no `session_exit` written, exactly session `2750b48c`'s tail; `C-d` is the same. On this branch one `C-c` or one `C-d` prints the exit menu and reaches the shell (`exit_marker=1`, `EXITED_WITH=0`). `Escape` still does not exit on either arm, which is the point: cancel is not exit. A second `C-c` *does* eventually get `origin/main` out — the first press cancels the turn, `st.Running` goes false, and the next press takes the `!running` branch — so the real defect is that the chord's first and only press while a turn is live buys nothing, and a turn that is slow to unwind (recovery ladder, in-flight tool, MCP call still draining) never reaches that second press.

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`feat/sidebar-black-bg` — user-requested: "change the sidebar background color to black too"). **The sidebar was the last surface still painted a themed background.** `drawDiffOverlay` moved its panel's field to the terminal's own background in `#466`, and the panel beside it kept filling with `theme.BgBase` (`#141414`), so the transcript was the terminal's black and the sidebar beside it was a grey band — two windows wearing two different backgrounds. It was not only a mismatch: the panel's own text cells are painted `StyleDefault.Foreground(...)`, so a themed fill left the fill itself grey while every line of text sat on the terminal's black inside it — the same striping `#454` built into the diff popup, which is exactly what `#466` was called to reverse.

The fix is one line, and it is `#466`'s: fill with `tcell.StyleDefault` (SGR 49) instead of `bg_base`, and let the diff inks carry their own background (`ds.added`/`ds.removed` are already built from `StyleDefault`, so the `.Background(bg)` they used to need is what used to put a second colour into them). The sidebar now resolves to whatever the terminal is — black on the themes people run this in — and a light theme no longer strands its dark ink on a surface xdev picked.

Deliberate: the panel is no longer a *surface* of its own, only a column of the same field with its own text. That is the point of the request, and it is what makes the two windows read as one app. A visible separator would have to be a gutter, which `dockPad` already is.

Tests: `internal/tui/diff_overlay_test.go` (`TestSidebarIsTheTerminalBackground` — a blank gutter cell, a title-slot cell and a bottom-row cell must all be `ColorDefault` while the built-in theme names `bg_base`; it fails on stock `main` with `sidebar cell (118,0) ' ' has background #141414`). `internal/tui/sidebar_fullheight_test.go` (`TestSidebarIsFullHeight` — its "the band reaches row 0" assertion read the cell's *background*, which is now the terminal's by design, so the same claim is made on the row's *content*: row 0 in the panel's columns must carry the panel's title). `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/tui/`, `go test ./internal/...` green; `go test ./...` fails only on `cmd/xdev`'s two pre-existing environment-dependent tests (`TestConnectPickerItems` reads the machine's real MCP config, `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills` reads the machine's real skills dir) — both fail identically with this diff stashed.

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`feat/sidebar-mcp-servers` — the sidebar lists every ENABLED MCP server, not a count of the connected ones). User request: "update my sidebar which show all the enabled mcp servers". `#388` gave the dock an MCP section, but its source was `Manager.Servers()` — the sessions that answered `initialize` — rendered through `mcpNameList` into a heading of "3 servers" and one row repeating the same string. Two things were missing. **A name.** The section never said which servers were in it, so a session with a working `leankg` and a silently broken `be-kg` looked identical to a healthy one. **A failure.** The list was built FROM the connect result, so an enabled server that failed was the one server that could not appear — the exact case a human opens the sidebar to find.

The source is now the config. `mcpDockBlock` (`cmd/xdev/print.go`) reads the merged `mcpclient.Config` — enablement is `IsEnabled()`, the same predicate `Connect` and the `disabledServers` override already agree on — and emits one row per enabled server, sorted, `●` where the manager holds a live session and `○` where it does not. `attachMCP` assigns `reg.MCPBlock` (the renamed `MCPNames`) BEFORE the connect starts, because the panel reads it on the first paint: the section shows the enabled list immediately, every row `○`, and the marks fill in when the sessions land. That repaint is the one new seam in the change — `attachMCP` takes a `done func()` that fires once the connect has settled, and the TUI passes `app.DockBump`; the other three modes pass nil. Nothing about the connect itself moved.

Deliberate: no health probe, no click action, no `disabled` rows. A probe would put a network round-trip and a cache behind a panel whose whole budget is "a handful of sources, a row budget, no I/O", and `/mcps` already prints `online`/`unreachable`/`disabled` per server on demand. `disabled` is a config fact the human wrote; listing it would put rows in the panel for servers that can never answer.

Tests: `cmd/xdev/mcpdock_test.go` (every enabled server listed and sorted, a disabled one absent, a nil config and an all-disabled one both empty, and end to end through the real loader that a server which connects to nothing still reaches the registry's block), `internal/tui/dock_test.go` (`TestDockPaintsEveryMCPName` — one heading plus one painted row per name, verified off the simulation screen; it fails on the old `MCP · 3 servers` source). `go build ./...` and `go vet` clean; `go test ./internal/tui/ ./internal/tool/ ./internal/mcpclient/` green, including `-tags e2e -run TestLive`. `cmd/xdev` has two pre-existing failures on this branch's base (`TestConnectPickerItems`, `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills` — both reproduced on a clean `origin/main` worktree, unrelated to this change).

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`feat/toast` — the notice moves out of the prompt and into opencode's top-right corner). User request: "add the notification like opencode in xdev to show the info, error for example the mcp error show in at noti in top right and disappear, not showing below my textbox prompt input". #319/#272 had already stopped `finishMCP` writing to stderr (the alt screen paints that over the composer) and parked the message on the composer's info divider instead — **the wrong row for a failure.** The divider is a shared slot: the model name and the `▲n▼n` viewport hint both want its right end, and `drawComposer` drops a hint wider than the room beside the model name, so a long MCP error was cut short or not painted at all, on the one row the user is typing over.

`internal/tui/toast.go` adds the corner. One queue, `Toast(level, text, d)` (lock + poke, any goroutine) with UI-thread twins `setNotice`/`setError` (no lock, no poke — the caller already holds `a.mu` or is the loop); `SetNotice` was the off-thread name for `Toast(ToastError, …)` — since `fix/toast-autohide` the MCP report calls `Toast` directly and that alias is gone, so no mode can own a lifetime of its own. `drawToasts` paints newest-at-top over the transcript's rows, one row each: a `bg_highlight` plate sized to the text, `accent_error` foreground and a `✗` for errors / `gray` and `•` for info, right-aligned to `rightEdge()` — the **main pane's** edge, so the context dock keeps its columns — starting under the top bar. The copy confirmation (both paths), a failed paste/clipboard chord, a failed link, a refused image and the read-only settings row all moved with it, which is why the divider's `copyHint()` lead-in is gone and `selNotice`/`selNoticeUntil` are deleted: one notice channel, not two. `paint()` calls it last, after the selection and the composer, and `toastsExpiring()` is the tick's new "repaint me" answer — the one repaint with no event behind it.

Deliberate: 5s info / 8s error as the level's own default (MCP no longer keeps an explicit 2-minute `mcpNoticeGrace` — see the fix below: that lifetime is what made the notice read as chrome that never clears); a cap of 3 with the **oldest** dropped (four broken servers is a report, not a wall over the transcript); no hover-freeze and no click-to-dismiss (opencode has both; neither was asked for, and the freeze needs the pointer's live position, which the tick would have to track — the upgrade path if a message is ever missed); nothing is queued to the session file (a toast is feedback for the second it exists, unlike the persisted recovery notices of `fix/turn-ladder-unbounded`); a pane narrower than 24 columns or shorter than the stack paints nothing rather than a truncated speck.

Tests: `internal/tui/toast_test.go` (corner placement on the first transcript row and never on the divider row, newest-first stacking, the 3-row cap, a multi-line error collapsed to one row, expiry, the narrow-pane skip, and the dock edge), and the two rewritten divider assertions — `TestCopyConfirmationIsAToast` and `TestOffThreadToastIsACornerNoticeAndExpires` (`internal/tui/selection_test.go`) — plus `settings_mouse_test.go`'s read-only click, which now reads the toast instead of `copyHint()`. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/tui ./cmd/xdev`, `go test ./internal/tui` clean. **Verified live** in tmux at 100x28 with the real binary: the `be-kg` connect failure paints as `✗ mcp: be-kg unavailable — see `xdev mcps` for configured URLs` on transcript row 1, keeps painting over the `/settings` overlay, leaves the composer and status row untouched, and drops off-screen on its own after its grace.
