*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (issue triage): every open issue traced to code at `origin/main` @ `3c44ec5`; #83/#102/#315/#374 closed as done, 182 issues labelled P0/P1/P2 by impact rather than milestone, and the P0/P1 sets recorded in §7.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-28 (`feat/task-live-view` — the user can now watch what a subagent is doing, the way Claude Code shows it). A foreground `task` used to be one generic tool row: a spinner, an elapsed timer, then a result box. The child ran for minutes in between and the user saw nothing — the whole point of delegating is that the fan-out is faster than the parent, and the transcript read as a hang. Claude Code's rule (adopted in `research/claude-code/cc-design-decisions.md`, pinned by `TestSubagentYieldOnlyIsolation`) is that a child's transcript NEVER enters the PARENT'S CONTEXT — only the yield does. That rule was never the problem: it constrains what the MODEL reads, not what the HUMAN sees, and the human's half was simply missing.

Four changes, no new subsystem:

1. **The seam.** `SubagentSpec.OnEvent func(SubagentEvent)` (`internal/agent/subagent.go`) reports a child's `start`, one event per finished tool call, and its terminal `end`. It rides the child's existing turn hooks — the same additive-wrap pattern `attachChildAdvisor` already used (`task.go`) — and `SpawnChild`'s RETURN VALUE IS UNCHANGED, so the isolation contract and every existing subagent test keep passing. The event carries the child's raw tool-call arguments rather than a pre-flattened phrase: what a `read` is "about" is `tui.toolDetail`'s job, and the agent package has no opinion about it.
2. **The tool passes it through.** `TaskTool.OnEvent` reaches foreground spawns. The hub composes its own additively (the way it does for `OnRun`), so background jobs are unaffected. A batch item with no `name` is now labelled `agent`/`task #N` (`childLabel`) — an unnamed child used to arrive with nothing for a row to say.
3. **The transcript.** `Block.Sub` holds the call's children; they paint as dim `⎿ <label> · <what it last did>` continuation rows under the call row, using the same `toolDetail` precedence a call row uses. They are LINES, not a new `BlockKind`: a child has no lifecycle the transcript manages (no trim tier, no box, no dock section, no `/trajectory` record), and a kind would drag it through all of those. `rowIndex` needed no change — the running-row stamp already ages per second.
4. **The host.** `taskChildSink` (`cmd/xdev/tui.go`) maps a child event to the `task` row it belongs to. Agent events carry no call id and two `task` calls can be in flight in one turn (`MaxToolWorkers`), so a new child binds to the newest running `task` row and keeps that binding for life — the transcript matches by call id, the same rule `FinishTool` uses after #448.

Deliberate: the paint cap is **3 children + `+N more running`**, and the block keeps ALL of them, because a dropped child would take a late settle with it. No per-child elapsed while the call is in flight (the call row right above already counts the wall time). No persistence: the durable record is the child session, which already exists and is already named in the result. No foreground children in the dock `AGENTS` section or `/hub` — that needs its own decision about what cancelling a foreground child means. Design: [decisions/live-subagent-view.md](decisions/live-subagent-view.md).

Tests: `internal/agent/subagent_progress_test.go` (event sequence, batch labels, result unchanged), `internal/tui/task_live_test.go` (7 cases: per-call binding, late-drop, non-`task` untouched), `internal/tui/task_render_test.go` (5 cases on painted output), `cmd/xdev/task_child_sink_test.go` (2 cases on the host's call binding). `go build ./...` and `go vet` clean; `internal/agent` and `internal/tui` pass in full. The two `cmd/xdev` failures (`TestConnectPickerItems`, `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills`) reproduce on a clean `origin/main` — a credential-picker ordering and an ambient skills directory, both unrelated. **Not yet driven in a live TUI** — the render tests use a simulation screen, which is evidence about the paint path, not about a person watching a real spawn.

*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`feat/auto-answer-toggle` — `/auto-answer yes|no` and the sidebar's build). **The ask card's answer policy was config-only, so the human who decided mid-turn to stop answering their own questions had to leave the session to say so.** `ask.autoAnswer` already existed (`#451`), off by default: a card the human skipped waits for them instead of taking the recommended option. `/auto-answer [yes|no]` (bare call toggles; `on|off|true|false` accepted too, matching `/plan` and `/advisor`) persists the key to the same global layer `xdev config set` edits AND folds it into the running session's in-memory settings. The reason the seam reads the policy PER CALL rather than capturing it: `App.NewAskOps` now takes a `func() time.Duration` wait and `askCardSink` a `func() bool` — a captured bool plus a settings file that moved is how a card ends up answering a question the human was just told it would not. A misspelled argument is a usage error, never a toggle. **The build this process is now shows in the context dock's footer**, under the branch: `App.SetVersion` (called once from `cmd/xdev/tui.go` with `version`), one row, and the footer is the one section that is never folded away — the first question about a session that behaves strangely is which build it was. An unset version paints no row at all rather than a bare `xdev`.


Tests: `internal/tui/autoanswer_test.go` (both toggle directions, the whole yes/no/on/off/true/false vocabulary, a refused typo writes nothing, the unwired seam), `cmd/xdev/asksink_test.go` (`TestAskCardSinkReadsAutoAnswerPerCall` — one question, the flip, then the same skip must answer nothing), `internal/tui/dock_test.go` (`TestDockFooterCarriesTheVersion` — the row, its position, and no row for an unset version).

*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`fix/stall-sleep-false-positive` — a sleeping Mac is not a stalled UI loop). Follows #460 and #461 on the same 2026-09-28 report (session `6917d52f`). Those two fixed the dead terminal; this fixes the diagnosis that was telling the user it was happening 16 times a day when it never was. **The stall watchdog measures silence against the wall clock, and a suspended machine produces silence the loop never chose.** Correlating the 165 dumps in `~/.xdev/agent/dumps/` against `pmset -g log` on 2026-09-28: 16 of them had their `last loop beat` inside a sleep interval to the second — 17m43s, 16m48s, 16m47s, 15m50s, 15m47s, 15m05s, 15m03s, 14m59s, 10m29s, 1m06s, 39s, 23s, 12s, 6s — every one written on the first tick after the lid opened, every one reporting a goroutine 1 sitting in the same idle `select` it always sits in. The counts are the tell: a real UI stall is rare, a Mac that sleeps between keystrokes is Tuesday. Nothing had broken, but the report the user reads when something *does* break is now 16/165 noise, and a false alarm in a frozen-UI file costs more than a late true one — the watchdog says so itself in `stall.go`. So the watchdog now distinguishes the two the only way it can: it polls on an injected clock, and a poll that arrives more than `pollSkip` (30s) late is a machine that was asleep, not a loop that hung. Such a poll grants a grace of two stall windows before the beat is judged, which covers the DarkWake that follows (the machine wakes, the user has not touched it, the loop has not yet drawn) without disarming anything: past the grace an actually-stuck loop is reported exactly as before. `pollSkip` is deliberately far above scheduling jitter — a late tick is milliseconds, a sleep is minutes.

Tests: `internal/tui/stall_test.go` (`TestStallWatchdogIgnoresSilenceThatIsOnlyASleep` — a jumpable clock stands in for a suspended machine: 15 minutes of wall clock in one tick, no dump, and still none after the loop beats again; `TestStallWatchdogStillReportsAStallAfterASleep` — the same jump with no beat behind it still produces exactly one dump, so the grace is a pause and not a pardon). The four pre-existing watchdog tests pass unchanged, now passing `time.Now`. `go build ./...`, `go vet`, and `go test ./internal/tui/` clean; `cmd/xdev` has one pre-existing failure on `main` (`TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills` — it reads the real `~/.xdev/agent/skills`, so it fails wherever skills are installed), verified unrelated by running it on stock `origin/main`.

*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`fix/tui-exit-repro` — a resumed session stops quitting on the nudge it already has). The other half of the 2026-09-28 report (session `6917d52f`), and the half #460 and #461 could not have fixed: both restore the terminal on the way out, and this was never a bad way out — it was a turn that produced nothing. **The file's last four records are the proof: a text-only assistant yield (407), a `prompt-continuation` nudge (408), a `model_change` (409), a `session_exit` code 0 (410) — and no assistant message at all between the nudge and the exit.** A nudge persisted as the final message is a session that can only die: resume it, send anything, and the same shape repeats, which is exactly the reported "kill and resume, prompt continue, it keeps exiting".

The cause is one loop answering two questions with the wrong scope. `internal/agent/loop.go` asks *has this conversation used tools?* (a property of the whole conversation) and *did **this run** already nudge and get a second yield?* (a property of one run) in a single pass over `history` — and `history` opens as everything a resume rebuilt. So after a resume the last user message **is** a persisted nudge, `lastWasPromptCont` latched true, the guard refused to continue, `cont` stayed empty, the turn ended at `if len(queued) == 0 && cont == ""` with no model call, and `runTUI` returned 0. Silencing was permanent, not per-nudge, and `maxPromptContinuations = 8` never got a chance to matter because the counter is reset per `Run` and the guard fired before it incremented. The two questions now have the scopes they actually ask: `usedTools` reads all of history, `lastWasPromptCont` reads from `resumeFloor` — `len(history)` at `Run` entry, recorded once. A nudge in the persisted tail is a previous run's unfinished business, not an answer this run received, so it must not count as one. Same-run behaviour is unchanged: a nudge this run sent and answered with a second yield is still not nudged twice.

Tests: `internal/agent/goal_test.go` (`TestPromptContinuationSurvivesAResumedTrailingNudge` — the resumed file ends on a nudge, the model's first turn yields text with no tool call, and the run must reach the provider a second time with a fresh nudge as its last message; fails on stock `main` with `stream requests = 1`, which is the bug in one assertion. `TestPromptContinuationDoesNotDoubleNudgeWithinARun` — three consecutive yields after a tool turn, and exactly one nudge is sent, so the per-run guard is not a no-op. The two pre-existing prompt-continuation tests and both goal-continuation tests pass unchanged. `go build ./...` and `go vet` clean; `internal/agent` fully green (26s). `cmd/xdev` has one pre-existing failure on `main` (`TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills` — it reads the real `~/.xdev/agent/skills`), verified unrelated on stock `origin/main`.

*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`fix/turn-ladder-unbounded` — a turn may not outlive its budget). The wedge behind the 2026-09-28 dead-terminal report that #460/#461 and #467 each closed one layer of: session `a7e17741`, v0.4.130, 26 records, a `toolResult` at 22:49:13 and a user `continue` at 23:42:21 with **nothing between them** — no `session_exit`, no `tui-stall` dump (so the UI loop was beating), no jetsam, no core. **Four recovery loops had no ceiling at all under the shipped defaults.** `retry.infinite` and `retry.retryAllErrors` are both default-ON (`internal/config/retry.go:94-111`) and the layered settings merge is one-way (`retry.go:198-203`), so no config can switch them off; every `|| policy.Infinite` in the ladder then lifted its bound:

- the empty-turn recovery (`emptyRetries++`, rebuild, backoff, `continue`) had **no counter at all**;
- retain-and-continue used a single `continued` bool that `Infinite` deliberately never set, so `maxPostContentContinuations` could not fire under the default;
- the health-check and all-targets-down escalations were unbounded the same way.

Any of them holds the TUI's single turn claim — `cmd/xdev/tui.go`'s `running` atomic, cleared only by the turn goroutine's own defers (`:1765-1767`) — so a turn that never returns refuses every later submit with `a turn is already running — Esc cancels it`, display-only and never persisted. Esc cancels the context but does not clear `running`; Ctrl-C is mapped to `quit` (`keymap.go:74`) and is gated on `running` too. That is the wedged state the report describes, and the reason the session file looks dead while the process is alive: every recovery announcement is an `AddSystemBlock` (`internal/tui/app.go:495-500`), so a turn looping for 53 minutes writes **zero** records — 0 of 132 session files on this machine contain a persisted retry notice.

**The change:** three ceilings, each applying to the infinite ladder as well as the bounded one. `maxEmptyTurnRecoveries = 3` (the nudge budget already asks twice), `maxPostContentContinuations = 3` via `continuationBudget(policy)` (which keeps the bounded ladder's historical one-shot budget, which `TestRetryPostContentSecondFailureSurfaces` pins), and `maxSilentRecoveryRounds = 12` on both escalations. A turn still ends with the error the TUI already renders, and the user can resubmit.

Tests: `internal/agent/turn_budget_test.go` (`TestEmptyTurnRecoveryIsBoundedUnderRetryAllErrors` — an unanswering model ends with `ErrEmptyTurn` after exactly `maxEmptyTurnNudges + maxEmptyTurnRecoveries + 1` requests, not a drained script; `TestPostContentContinuationIsBoundedUnderInfiniteRetry`; `TestSilentRecoveryEscalationIsBoundedUnderInfiniteRetry`; `TestHealthCheckEscalationIsBoundedUnderInfiniteRetry`; and `TestBoundedSilentRecoveryStillSurvivesALongOutage`, the guard the other direction — an outage longer than the bounded ladder but inside `maxSilentRecoveryRounds` is still survived with no user involvement, which is the contract `retry.infinite` exists for). All four bound tests are mutation-checked: `+1000` on each ceiling makes them fail (the post-content one at 1020 requests against a limit of 4, the escalation one at 1027 against 26). `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` and `go test ./internal/agent/` clean; `cmd/xdev`'s `TestConnectPickerItems` and `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills` fail identically on stock `origin/main` (verified stashed), reading this machine's real credentials and skills dir.

Deliberate ceiling: 12 rounds is a round count, not a wall clock — at the 8s backoff cap that is a little under two minutes of surviving an outage, chosen because the session's real recovery cost is a wedged TUI, not a slow one. If a longer outage is ever worth surviving unattended, the upgrade path is a per-turn wall-clock budget on `oneTurnWithRecovery`'s context (there is no `WithTimeout` anywhere on the interactive turn path today), not a larger constant.

**The second half: a recovery round that nobody can read.** Bounding the loops stops the wedge, but the reason the diagnosis took an afternoon is that the loops were *invisible in the file*. Every recovery announcement was an `AddSystemBlock` — display-only — so a turn that spent 53 minutes in one of them left a session file whose last record was a toolResult from before it started, and nothing in it said a retry had ever happened. Each of the three `notice*` funcs (the screen voice, unchanged, every round) now has a `persist*` twin that appends a `CustomEntry`: `recovery_all_targets_down`, `recovery_empty_turn`, `recovery_continuation`, carrying `round`, `delaySeconds`, `remainingRounds` and the underlying `error`. Throttled by `maxNoticeStride = 3` — first round, then every third — so a 12-round loop writes five records rather than twelve. A `CustomEntry` is the load-bearing shape: `session.buildContext` switches only on `MessageEntry`, `CompactionEntry` and `BranchSummaryEntry`, so the notice is readable by `xdev` and by `jq` and **never reaches the model as a turn** (`TestRecoveryNoticeNeverReachesTheModel` pins that; a persisted retry line that reached the provider would be a user message nobody typed). A nil store or a failed append is a no-op — a notice that cannot be written must never break the recovery it describes.

Tests: `internal/agent/recovery_notice_test.go` (`TestRecoveryNoticeIsPersistedThrottled` — rounds exactly 1, 3, 6, 9, 12, so throttled and not dropped; `TestRecoveryNoticeCarriesTheRemainingBudget`; `TestRecoveryNoticeKindsCarryTheirOwnCeiling`; `TestEmptyTurnRecoveryIsPersisted`; `TestRecoveryNoticeNeverReachesTheModel`; and `TestRecoveryNoticeSurvivesReopen`, the one that matters most — it writes to a real file, closes the store, reopens it and asserts the values round-trip through JSON as `float64`, which is what a `jq` one-liner on a wedged session would read. A mutation check — the `persist*` call sites removed — fails all six).

**Verified live, and the live run found two more bugs.** A dead-gateway reproduction (a Python listener on 127.0.0.1:18080 that answers `/models` and then never answers a POST, driven through `xdev print` with a scratch `XDEV_AGENT_DIR`) produced the state a7e17741 held: a run alive at 210s with an ESTABLISHED, idle socket and **zero** records written. Two defects, neither visible to the unit tests:

1. **The header phase of a streaming POST was unbounded.** The 90s stream watchdog only starts *after* `wirePost` returns, and the client `buildProvider` constructs for every configured provider (`cmd/xdev/print.go:797`) carries no `ResponseHeaderTimeout` at all — so a host that accepted the connection and said nothing held the run open indefinitely. The fix is `Transport.ResponseHeaderTimeout` on a **cloned** transport (`withHeaderBudget`), which bounds the header wait and leaves the body to the watchdog, with no cancel to own. Two earlier shapes failed and both failures are in the code comment: `defer cancelHeader()` on a `context.WithTimeout` killed every healthy stream at its first byte (all the `openai_*`/`google_*`/`responses_*` happy-path tests read `context canceled`), and *not* cancelling on success made `go vet`'s `lostcancel` fire. Cloning keeps the check meaningful everywhere else.
2. **A run whose only writes are recovery notices produced no session file at all.** `internal/session`'s store materialises on disk at the first *assistant* message (`store.go`'s `appendLocked`) — and a run that never gets one is exactly the run this notice describes, so every notice went to memory and died with the process. `persistRecoveryNotice` now materialises the store itself when `Path() == "" && AutoPath() != ""`, the same move `schedule.go:317` already makes before its first durable fact.

Both are pinned by tests that failed first: `internal/ai/wire_post_header_test.go` (`TestWirePostHeaderPhaseIsBounded` against a raw listener that accepts and never answers, with the exact client `buildProvider` builds; `TestWirePostHeaderBudgetDoesNotCapTheBody`, the guard for the shape that killed healthy streams), and `TestRecoveryNoticeMaterialisesAnUnstartedSession` (`OpenMem` + `EnableAutoPersist`, so it reproduces the live shape rather than a pre-materialised store). The live run after the fixes, against a refused port so the ladder drains in seconds, wrote what was asked for:

```
custom recovery_all_targets_down {"round":1,  "delaySeconds":0, "remainingRounds":11, "error":"…connection refused"}
custom recovery_all_targets_down {"round":3,  "delaySeconds":2, "remainingRounds":9,  …}
custom recovery_all_targets_down {"round":6,  "delaySeconds":8, "remainingRounds":6,  …}
custom recovery_all_targets_down {"round":9,  "delaySeconds":6, "remainingRounds":3,  …}
custom recovery_all_targets_down {"round":12, "delaySeconds":8, "remainingRounds":0,  …}
model_change / custom session_exit {"code": 1}
```

The session that used to be silent now says what it was waiting for, how long, and how much budget was left — and the run ended at the bound instead of never. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./internal/...` clean.

**Live re-verification of the header bound, and one honest correction.** Rebuilt and re-run against the same silent gateway: run 1 blocked `[select, 2 minutes]` inside `wirePostWithTimeout`'s `budgeted.Do` (the bound's own line) at the FIRST request, run 2 blocked `[select]` with no duration — i.e. already in a later round, so the 90s expiry did fire and the ladder moved on. The bound works; a `SIGQUIT` goroutine dump is what settled it, since both runs look identical from the outside for their first two minutes.

What that measurement exposed is the unit problem now recorded on `maxSilentRecoveryRounds`: **one round is not one attempt.** A round is a full bounded ladder (`MaxRetries+1` calls) and each call waits out `FirstProgressTimeout` when the host accepts and then goes silent, so 12 rounds × 5 calls × 90s is ~90 minutes of wedged TUI before the turn gives up. The bound is honest; the unit is wrong. A round count cannot express a wall clock, and shrinking the constant would only shorten the outage an unattended run can survive — the fix is a per-turn wall-clock budget on `oneTurnWithRecovery`'s context, which is still the stated upgrade path. Not done in this PR: it changes what an outage costs, which is a judgement call about how long a user should be left waiting, and that is the user's call to make.


Previous entry — 2026-09-29 (`fix/tui-signal-restore` — a stopped TUI no longer leaves a dead terminal). User report 2026-09-28, session `6917d52f`: the TUI vanished mid-turn and the terminal came back unusable (no echo, no prompt, Ctrl-C dead). The session file ends on a toolResult with no `session_exit` — the marker every clean exit writes (`cmd/xdev/tui.go:240`) — the process was found in state `T`, and the pane was reaped with a Hangup two minutes later. Root cause, two halves of one gap: the TUI restores the tty exactly once, on `defer scr.Fini()`, and `internal/tui` installed no signal handler at all, so SIGTSTP (Ctrl-Z, which tcell's raw mode makes the TUI's own responsibility) took the default action and suspended the process *while it held raw mode, the alt screen and mouse reporting*; SIGHUP and SIGTERM did the same. What the guard does: `watchTerminalRoutes` (`cmd/xdev/tui_signal_unix.go`) catches those three, runs `scr.Fini()` first, then re-raises the signal after `signal.Stop` so the job still ends the way the shell expects — a stop the user can `fg`, a hangup it can report — instead of vanishing. SIGWINCH is deliberately untouched: tcell owns it (`tty_unix.go`) and a second `Notify` would starve the screen of resize events. Windows keeps the old behaviour behind a build-tagged no-op (no SIGTSTP/SIGHUP; `syscall.Kill` there is not a POSIX raise). Tests: `cmd/xdev/tui_signal_test.go` drives a real child process per signal (SIGTSTP, SIGHUP, SIGTERM, plus the released-guard case) and asserts the restore ran *and* the process died on that signal; the child is a separate process because a re-raised signal would otherwise kill the test binary. `go build ./...` clean for darwin and windows/amd64, `go vet` clean. Ceiling, named: SIGKILL and a panic on a non-main goroutine still skip the restore — a top-level `recover()` in `main()` remains the fix for the second (a `recover()` in `main()` cannot help either — a panic on a non-main goroutine runs no defer — so it is `goGuarded`, landed as `fix/tui-panic-recover`), and nothing can help the first.

Previous entry — 2026-09-29 (`feat/jump-to-latest` — the TUI grew a jump-to-latest button). Scrolling up in a session showed only the `▲ n ▼ n` readout on the composer divider: a counter, not a control, so catching up after a long stream meant mashing Ctrl+F blind. `paint()` now paints a `↓ n new` pill over the transcript's bottom-right corner whenever rows are hidden below the viewport (`drawJumpChip`), and the press branch in `handleMouse` claims it like the scrollbar — the gesture never anchors a selection on the row behind it. The count is the scroll model's own hidden-row total, so the chip appears and disappears with the tail and the rect is cleared every frame (a chip that is not on screen cannot be clicked); it sits on the transcript's own edge, not the terminal's, so it stays with the content when the context dock is open. Mouse-only: `End` already jumps to the tail. Tests: `internal/tui/jumpchip_test.go`.

Previous entry — 2026-09-29 (`fix/sidebar-title-copy` — the sidebar's title slot could not be copied). User report: dragging across the context dock's top slot, which holds the session's name (or its first prompt when the session has no title of its own), put nothing on the clipboard. Root cause: `selDockRowsForPaint` published only the rows the *build* made (`d.lines`), never the title slot's wrapped lines, so `selRowAt` found no row for that screen row and fell through to the transcript row painted behind the panel — whose cells end far left of the panel's column, so `spanRow` clipped the covered range to empty and the copy came out blank. Fix: the title slot's lines go into the same copy table, one entry per painted row, at the same x0 as the built rows. Tests: `internal/tui/sidebar_mouse_test.go` (two new cases — the session-title slot and the wrapped first-prompt slot, each pinned on the exact cell slice the drag covers). `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/tui/`, `go test ./internal/tui/` clean.

Previous entry — 2026-09-29 (`fix/diff-overlay-bg` — the diff popup's inside is the terminal's black, not the theme's grey). User report 2026-09-28: clicking a changed file in the sidebar opened the full-width diff viewer over a black field, while the rest of the app sat on the theme's `bg_base` (#141414). **#454 fixed the wrong half.** It read the panel's background with `th.Slot(theme.BgBase)`, which is the correct resolution for a theme that hands the slot to the terminal (`""` → ColorDefault) — but a theme that NAMES `bg_base` still got a themed fill, and the fill was the only surface on the frame that carried it: every text cell of the diff (title, rows, footer, borders) paints with `tcell.StyleDefault.Foreground(...)` and therefore resolves its own background to `ColorDefault`. The viewer came up as a #141414 rectangle with #000000 bars under every line of text. `drawDiffOverlay` now fills with `tcell.StyleDefault` (SGR 49) — the whole panel resolves the terminal's own background like the transcript, the composer and the rest of the frame, and a theme that names `bg_base` no longer stripes its own popup. The sidebar is deliberately untouched: it has no text-vs-blank mismatch (drawDock builds every style from `body.Foreground(...)`), and the human liked its #141414 surface, so the two now differ by intent, not by accident. **This reverses the second half of #454** (`TestDiffOverlayHonoursANamedBackground` → `TestDiffOverlayIgnoresANamedBackground`), and the reversal is the point: the earlier fix optimised for "inherit whatever the terminal is running" and left the common case — a named palette — painting a surface the text underneath it contradicted.

Tests: `internal/tui/diff_overlay_test.go` — `TestDiffOverlayInsideIsTheTerminalBackground` (unchanged, the theme-leaves-it-to-the-terminal case) and `TestDiffOverlayIgnoresANamedBackground` (a blank interior cell and a diff-text cell must both be `ColorDefault` when the theme names `bg_base`; it fails with the pre-fix `Slot` resolution, which painted the blank cell #141414). `go build ./...` and `go vet ./internal/tui/` clean; `go test ./internal/tui/` and `go test -tags e2e ./internal/tui/ -run TestLive` green.

**Driven on the built binary, both sides of the same gesture.** Two binaries, one session store, one gesture: a resumed session (whose FILES rows carry 5 real unified diffs) in tmux at 120x40, `env -u NO_COLOR` (a `NO_COLOR=1` in the shell profile collapses every themed colour to SGR 7 and would have made this a false negative), the same SGR-1006 press/release injected on the same row, then the pane captured with `-e` and the cell grid folded back into backgrounds. Row 5 of the popup, the `@@ -117,10 +117,8 @@` hunk header: **pre-fix `x3:#141414 x4:#000000 … x74:#141414`** — the grey band with a black bar under the text — and **post-fix `x0:#000000`**, one background for all 118 columns. Over rows 2–13, the pre-fix binary paints `#141414` in 12 of 12 rows; the fixed binary in 0 of 12. `NO_COLOR` was the trap that cost the first run: with it set, `tcell.Colors()` returns 0 and both binaries painted identically, so the bug looked fixed before it was.

Previous entry — 2026-09-29 (`feat/sidebar-full-height` — the sidebar is a second window, full height). User report: "make the sidebar in my xdev like the sidebar in opencode, full height, like 2 windows the main and sidebar, right now it not full 100 and the textbox still 100% width". The panel's band was the transcript's (`dockGrid` = `transcriptTop` → `height-2-composerRows`), so it stopped under the top bar and above the composer, and `drawComposer`/`drawStatusRow`/`drawTopBar` measured against `a.width`, not `rightEdge()` — the prompt box ran under the panel and the metrics right-aligned to the terminal's edge. `dockGrid` now returns `(0, a.height)`, refusing only below the new `dockMinRows = 5`; `drawComposer`, `drawStatusRow` and `drawTopBar` measure against `rightEdge()`, `drawHUD` takes the pane's width, `composerAvail` wraps at the pane, and `selStartLineSelect` takes the pane's row (a triple-click no longer swallows the panel's rows). With the panel shut every one of those is the whole terminal, so the shipped layout is untouched. `Alt+S` still cycles shown/hidden/auto: the column is a window now, its policy is unchanged. Tests: `internal/tui/sidebar_fullheight_test.go` (the band is every row and carries the panel background top to bottom, the box stops at the boundary and the editor wraps there, the status row and the top bar stay in the pane, the closed shape is the shipped one) plus `dock_test.go`'s `TestDockShortTerminalKeepsItsRows` rewritten for the new floor — all four fail against the stashed tree. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/tui/` and `go test ./internal/tui/` clean; the two `cmd/xdev` failures (`TestConnectPickerItems`, `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills`) reproduce on a clean tree — local provider credentials and a non-empty skills directory.

*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`fix/addusage-arity` — the sidebar's own tests could not compile). #464 landed `internal/tui/sidebar_fullheight_test.go` calling `AddUsage(1200, 340, 1540)`, and #465 landed in the same day raised the signature to `AddUsage(in, out, cache, thinking, total int64)`. Merged in the wrong order, the two are mutually unbuildable: **`go vet ./internal/tui/` fails on `origin/main` @ `19f750e`** with `not enough arguments in call to app.AddUsage` at both sites, and because the failure is in the package's test build, every test in `internal/tui` — the whole TUI suite — is unrunnable until it is fixed. The two calls become `AddUsage(1200, 340, 0, 0, 1540)`: no cache or thinking, and the same total the tests already asserted. #465's own tests (`internal/tui/metric_measure_test.go`) already use the 5-arg form, so this is the shape the signature wants. **Why it matters beyond the two lines:** `internal/tui` is where every TUI regression test lives, so a compile error there silences the entire suite — the failure mode is invisible tests, not a red test. A CI gate that only runs `go test ./cmd/xdev/...` would not have caught it either.

**Verified:** `go build ./...` and `go vet ./internal/tui/` clean (both fail on `origin/main` @ `19f750e` before this); `go test ./internal/tui/` green including the eight `TestSidebar*`/`TestTwoWindow*` cases #464 added; `go test -tags e2e ./internal/tui/ -run TestLive` green.

*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`fix/project-rules-weight` — xdev discounted the repository's own `AGENTS.md`). A 42-run head-to-head against the deepseek-harness reference (7 tasks × 2 arms × 3 repeats, both arms in their real production configuration, same model/gateway/credential) put one task's outcome outside every other number in the suite: `t07-rules-only-discovery`, the only task whose rules exist **solely** in `AGENTS.md` and nowhere in the task prompt. xdev scored 9.3/10 there against the reference's 10/10, and the single failing run (`invisible_rule_changelog`) was the single run that never opened the file — compliance tracked *reading* the file, not being handed it. Root cause: the injected block was headed `# Project context`, a label that reads as background material, and nothing in it told the model that a rule the task did not restate was still live. Every other task in the suite restates its rules in the prompt, which is exactly why six rounds of work never surfaced this.

`ProjectContextHeader` (`internal/agent/prompt.go`) now heads the block as **# Project rules and conventions** and states three things the old label did not: the rules are binding for the session, a task prompt that omits one has not cancelled it, and the injected copy may be followed without re-reading the source. It also bounds itself — a direct user instruction for the task still wins — because a repository rule and a system rule are different things and a header that blurs them is a correctness regression traded for a compliance rate. `ProjectContextBlock` is the single framing path, called by both injection sites (`BuildSystemPrompt` and `childSystem`), so the parent and every subagent cannot drift apart and the fix cannot silently apply to one arm only.

Verified: `go build ./...` clean, `gofmt` clean, `go test ./internal/...` green, `go test ./cmd/xdev/ -run Prompt` green including `TestBundledPromptStaysUnderBudget` (PRD §1 Goal 4's 1,000-token ceiling is unaffected — the header is 413 chars (~103 tokens on that test's own chars/4 yardstick) and the block renders only when a context file exists, so the budget test — which runs in an empty temp cwd with no `AGENTS.md` — measures it at zero). The three new tests in `internal/agent/prompt_project_rules_test.go` each fail against the pre-fix prompt: reverting the header to `# Project context` kills all four binding needles, and reverting `childSystem` to the bare heading kills the subagent arm. The two `cmd/xdev` failures (`TestConnectPickerItems`, `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills`) reproduce on unmodified `origin/main` — they read the developer's real home and are unrelated to this change. Measured on the real repo chain (this repo's full global+root+xdev+worktree `AGENTS.md` set) the assembled prompt is ~4,336 tokens against a 1,000-token goal — but that overshoot is the 16 KB of injected rules, not this header: the same prompt measures 993 tokens with no `AGENTS.md` present and the header contributing zero. The header adds 397 net characters to a block that was already unbounded; the unbounded part is the rules content, and is a separate, larger problem than this one. Recorded honestly: this is a prompt-framing fix justified by n=3 on one task; the compliance claim is not yet re-measured by the benchmark, and `bench/tasks/t07-rules-only-discovery` at n≥9 is the experiment that would settle it.

*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`fix/ask-card-look` — the ask card read as one more transcript block):* the card's own frame was the reason. `drawAskCard` painted a vertical on the right rail only, so its top and bottom rules began on a column no interior row framed; it resumed the top rule a cell past the label, leaving a blank cell in the middle of that edge; its body sat flush against the rails with nothing to the right of the longest line; and it wore `prompt_border_active`, the same dim slot the thinking block and a tool result wear. A box with one side missing, a rule with a hole in it and a dim rail is what a half-scrolled thinking or tool block looks like, which is the confusion being removed. Now the card frames both rails, sets its identity into the rule in the house `boxTop` shape (`╭─ ? ask · 1/2 ─╮` — the question mark is the tell; no framed block in the transcript leads with one), insets the body one cell from each rail, and paints the rail in `accent_user`, the colour the user's own turn already wears. Key handling, answer semantics, wrapping, the option window and the mouse hit map are untouched; `TestAskCardFramesItselfLikeACardNotATranscriptBlock` pins the frame, and `TestAskCardWrapsLongOptionText` now measures indentation past the rail because the rail is frame, not content.

*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`fix/ask-opencode-rail` — "update the ask answer box need to be same of the session TUI windows, right now is has the 100% width overlay to the sidebar"):* the card was the last surface still sized off `a.width` rather than `a.rightEdge()`, so with the context panel open its full-width frame ran *under* the panel — the panel's columns cut straight through the middle of the question the human was answering. It is now a surface of the main pane in opencode's shape: width from `rightEdge()`, one `┃` rail down the left edge in the human's accent (`accent_user`), no `╭─╮` box, and the `? ask` identity demoted from a label set into a rule to a muted first line. The box frame is the deeper half of the report: a thinking block and a tool result are exactly that shape, so the framed card read as one more transcript block even with the panel closed (that part landed in [`#473`](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/pull/473); this PR drops the frame it kept). `AskCardBatch`'s narrow-terminal guard now measures the pane too, so a terminal too tight to give the panel its columns skips the card rather than painting it under them. The surface keeps `bg_base` rather than opencode's `background.raised.base` — a second plane would fight the highlight band the card's own selected row already wears. Key handling, answer semantics, wrapping, the option window and the mouse hit map are untouched; `TestAskCardFramesItselfLikeACardNotATranscriptBlock` is replaced by `TestAskCardIsTheMainPaneNotAFullWidthOverlay` (rail in the accent, no corner painted, nothing inside the panel's columns), and `TestAskCardWrapsLongOptionText` measures indentation past `┃` rather than past `│`.

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`fix/ask-opencode-form` — "update the askbox in xdev, make it look exactly the same as the ask box TUI in opencode"):* #480 gave the card opencode's *placement* (a main-pane surface on a ┃ rail). This gives it opencode's *look*, matched against `packages/tui/src/routes/session/form.tsx` line by line. Four things changed, all in `drawAskCard`/`askRowLines`/`askFooter`: **the header** is opencode's — a `◆` mark in the rail's hue, then the title, then `1/2` (or `Review`) — replacing the muted `? ask · 1/2 · multi` line, whose `?`/dots were xdev's own invention; **the rows** are opencode's — `1. [ ] sqlite`, ordinal + checkbox, no `❯` cursor column and no `☐/☑`, because opencode marks the cursor with a *background on the choice's box* and that band now spans the whole choice (starting after the rail, which stays the card's frame). `Type something…` became opencode's `Type your own answer`; **(descriptions)** stay on their own indented lines under the label, which is where opencode's inline-then-column layout puts them once the row does not fit on one line; **(the footer)** is opencode's shape — lowercase key, then the verb, three spaces between hints (`⇆ tab   ↑↓ select   enter confirm   1-9 pick   z type   x chat   esc skip`) — with `enter done` on a multi card, because Space toggles and stays put there. The one deliberate divergence is `esc skip` rather than opencode's `esc dismiss`: xdev's esc leaves the question on the record for the tool's headless policy (#451), while opencode's cancels the request outright. Two rows stay that opencode's form has no equivalent for: the `Chat about this` escape and the `(recommended)` suffix, both kept because the model reads them (#451) and the recommended marker is the starting cursor. Keys are unchanged — the footer now *describes* the router rather than inventing a second vocabulary. `TestAskCardPaintsTheOpencodeLayout` is the new guard: header, ordinal+checkbox rows, both custom rows, the footer's order and the band all asserted on a live frame, plus an assertion that `❯` is back out of the rows. Verified: `go build ./...` clean, `gofmt` clean, `go test ./internal/tui/ ./internal/tool/` green.


*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`fix/context-budget-priority` — an overflowing `AGENTS.md` chain injected the least relevant rules it had). `LoadContextFiles` walks the chain root→cwd, and precedence runs the other way: the CWD's own rules override its ancestors. The pre-fix loop walked in order and `break`ed once `MaxContextBytes` (32 KB) was spent, so when a chain overflowed it kept every broad ancestor rule and dropped the tail — the most specific file, the one carrying the rules that say which ancestor rules do not apply. A repository with a large global `~/.xdev/agent/AGENTS.md` plus a project file therefore had its project rules silently removed, with no marker that anything was missing.

Two passes fix it. Pass one loads and expands every file in the chain. Pass two fits them **most specific first**: a file is kept if it still fits what the more specific files already claimed, and a skipped ancestor does not end the walk — a small root file that fits is worth more than nothing. The closest file is the floor: if it alone overruns the budget it is injected truncated rather than dropped, because dropping it would leave the session holding only the ancestors' rules, the same inversion one level down. One correction pass re-trims after rendering, because the budget covers the whole block and the marker and headings are not known until the marker text is.

The overflow is now **declared**: a `## Rules budget reached` block names the omitted paths and says the list is incomplete, which turns an invisible gap into a `read` the model can close. This matters because #471 heads the block as the repository's *binding* rules — a silently shortened list under that heading is a lie the model cannot detect. The block stays in root→cwd order, since precedence requires the general rule first and the specific one last.

Verified: `go build ./...` clean, `gofmt` clean, `go test ./internal/...` green. Five new tests in `internal/agent/context_budget_priority_test.go`, each mutation-checked against the pre-fix policy rather than restating the new code: reordering the fit pass to root→cwd with `break` (the old behaviour) fails the specific-rules test; suppressing the marker fails two tests; dropping the closest file instead of truncating it fails the content assertion. That last one is worth recording — the first version of that assertion checked only for the *path*, which the budget marker still prints, so it passed against the very mutant it was written to kill. The assertion now checks for the rendered rules, not the filename. `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills` fails identically on unmodified `origin/main` (it reads the developer's real `$HOME`) and is unrelated. Not measured: how often a real chain actually overflows 32 KB — xdev's own chain does (16 KB of `AGENTS.md` across four levels), which is what motivated the check, but the fraction of repositories that hit it is unmeasured.

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`fix/agents-md-budget` — an overflowing `AGENTS.md` chain could leave the CWD's own file never read at all, silently). Found while pricing the pre-existing 32 KiB `MaxContextBytes` cap. #477 fixed the *policy* — an overflowing chain fits the most specific files first, and the loss is declared by a `## Rules budget reached` marker — but its load pass still walked the chain root→cwd and `break`ed as soon as the pool was spent. So a fat ancestor meant the **CWD's own `AGENTS.md` was never even read**: the file carrying the rules that say which ancestor rules do not apply. The fit pass, whose entire job is to prefer the most specific rules, had nothing to prefer. Measured on #477's own code: a 31 KB global file rendered 32,823 bytes with the repository's rule **absent** and the marker **not** rendered — a silent loss, in exactly the window just under the cap where the fit pass still believed the chain fit.

The fix is one change: **the load pass reads every file in the chain and lets the fit pass spend the budget.** A file's cost is only knowable once it is loaded, and precedence runs cwd→root while the chain is read root→cwd, so paying during the read is paying in the wrong order. Ancestors are never rendered truncated — the fit pass keeps an ancestor whole or drops it entirely, because a half-present broad rulebook would quietly contradict the specific file that overrides it. The existing marker already names dropped paths, so an omitted global rulebook becomes a `read` the model can close rather than a gap it cannot see.

Mutation-verified against the pre-merge load loop: restoring the `break` fails `TestLoadContextFilesNeverStarvesTheClosestFile`. With the fix, a 98 KB global rulebook plus a small repo file renders **402 bytes carrying the repository's rules plus a marker naming the dropped global path**; previously 32,871 bytes with the rule absent and nothing said. All five #477 cases still pass, and the rendered block is byte-identical to #477 for any chain that fits the budget.

Measured, and deliberately not fixed here: this repo's assembled prompt is ~7,748 tokens against PRD §1 Goal 4's 1,000 — chain ~3,655, rulebook ~1,677, skills ~1,436, base ~476, tools ~500. Three separate budgets; the chain is one of them and this change makes it *correct*, not smaller. Getting the total under goal is a deliberate PRD decision rather than a follow-up bug, because the alternative is deleting rules a developer wrote — and the marker makes today's behaviour honest instead of silent.

*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`feat/usage-panel` — user-requested: the status row's numbers "read fine one at a time, but I want the report", with a total tool-call count). `/usage` prints the session's own report as a system block — the same numbers the HUD shows, in the long form a one-line row cannot carry: **Token usage** (total with thousands separators, cache HIT RATE, uncached/cached input, output with the reasoning broken out, provider cost, and the live `context used/window` with its percentage) and **Session statistics** (LLM time, tool time, average time-to-first-token, total tool calls with failures and their share of active time, active time). The two figures the row has no room for are the ones the report exists for: a hit rate is only meaningful as a ratio, and the tool-call count had no surface at all. Every number is a `Status` field the HUD already reads — no second source of truth — under the same lock, and `Reset` clears the new counters with the rest so a `/resume` or `/fork` cannot draw the previous session's totals. Four new `Status` counters (`ToolCalls`, `ToolErrors`, `ToolWork`, `LLMWork`) plus TTFT sum/count, fed from the seams that already measure each fact: `AddToolBlock` counts a call, `FinishTool` settles it (a new `ToolOutcome.Elapsed` carries the raw duration beside the row's formatted one — summing formatted strings is how a report ends up minutes off), `ai.EventDone` banks the provider's `DurationMS`/`TTFTMS`, and the replay path re-bases them from the rebuilt history with `SetLLMTime`/`workOf` so a resumed session reports the averages it really had. Hidden rather than zeroed: an unwired cost, an unmeasured LLM/tool split and a cache-less provider say nothing instead of drawing `$0.00`/`0s`/`cache hit 0%`. Verified: `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `go test ./internal/tui/` green (4 new tests pin the three blocks, the hidden lines, the `Reset` clear and the grouping); `go test ./cmd/xdev/` shows only `TestConnectPickerItems` + `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills`, both reproduced on a clean `origin/main` and unrelated. Driven for real in tmux against a local stub provider reporting a cached turn: `/usage` printed `66,345 tok · cache hit 100% · context 66.3k/200k (33%) · LLM time 0s · avg time to first token 600ms`.*


*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`feat/usage-panel` follow-up — user-requested: "I want these metrics also display in the xdev bottom status bar and use the deepseek harness icons too"). **The report's two glance-worthy readings moved onto the status row itself, wearing dsh's icons.** Two new `statusLine.segments` entries join the shipped default (`command │ time │ tokens │ cache │ context │ rate │ toolcalls`): `cache` renders dsh's cache-hit **RATE** (`cache 100%`) — see the dedup note below — and `toolcalls` renders the call count with failures (`✳1 calls`, `✳2 calls (1 failed)`), the one figure the row had no home for. Both are opt-out like every segment (`statusLine.segments` in settings), and both hide rather than draw a zero. **The icons are dsh's, at one cell each**: `IconGaugeOutline` → `⏱` (work timer, decode rate, ttft), `IconDatabaseOutline` → `▤` (token split, cache hit), and a `✳` for the tool wheel, read from `theme.Theme.HUDIcon` — overridable per theme under `symbols.overrides["hud.gauge|database|tool"]`, and the `ascii` symbol preset substitutes single-ASCII letter tags because a terminal that cannot draw the glyph would rather read `hit 96%` than paint a tofu box. The hardcoded `⚡`/`⌚` glyphs are gone; the decode-rate segment now reads `⏱ 65.2 t/s`. **One latent bug fell out of adding segments**: `hudEssentialWidth` gated the "must survive a narrow row" set on a bare `statusKeepRank >= 5`, so re-ranking the vocabulary silently promoted the token split to essential and it stopped dropping on a 60-column row. That threshold is now `hudEssentialRank` (time and rate by name) and the drop order is theme → model → cost → toolcalls → cache → tokens → context → time → rate, so a narrow row keeps the two dsh readings and the split they refine only as long as the width allows. `/usage` also stopped printing an impossible `N% of active time` when the ratio exceeds 100% (tool time and active time are differently-measured spans; a bang-mode call is tool time with no run span to bank it into) — the line is omitted rather than clamped, since a clamped 100% is the same lie. Verified: `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `gofmt` clean; `go test ./internal/tui/ ./internal/theme/` green (four new tests: the two row segments, the unmeasured-hides case, `HUDIcon` defaults/override/ascii, the omitted share); `go test ./cmd/xdev/` unchanged (same two pre-existing `origin/main` failures). Driven for real in tmux against a local stub provider: the live row read `⏱0s │ ▤↑0 ⇢64.6k │ ↓1.8k ˟1.3k │ ▤66.3k · cache 100% │ ctx 66.3k/200k │ ✳1 calls` beside the same numbers in `/usage`.*


*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`feat/usage-panel` — user-reported on the live row: "looks like we have duplicated value: ▤66.3k fix this"). **The cache segment was printing the session total a second time.** dsh's `UsagePill` reads `{total} · Cache hit N%` because on a row that IS the token reading; xdev's row already carries the ↑⇢↓ split, whose three numbers sum to the same total, so the pill re-drew `66.3k` a few cells after the `ctx` segment had drawn the same figure — the duplication the user spotted. The segment now reports the **rate alone** (`cache 100%`) and the leading `▤` is gone with it, because the database icon is already on the segment this one refines and a second one three cells later is the same duplication in another dress. The exact total stays in `/usage`, where a grouped `66,345 tok` has a line to itself and earns the digits. Pinned by `TestCacheSegmentCarriesNoSecondTotal`, which asserts the segment text is the rate alone, that `66.8k` appears nowhere on the row, and that exactly one `▤` is drawn. Driven for real: the row now reads `⏱0s │ ▤↑0 ⇢64.6k │ ↓1.8k ˟1.3k │ cache 100% │ ctx 66.3k/200k │ ✳1 calls`, with `66,345 tok` still in `/usage`.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`feat/queue-mid-turn` — user-requested: "new message/prompt sent in the running session in xdev has to add to queue and sent to session later like deepseek harness, and also have the button + hotkey to send now which the session will acknowledge and process that message too in the middle of session"). **The mid-turn submit queue shipped, the dsh/Claude-Code shape.** Until now a prompt typed while a turn was running was refused outright — `runTurn`'s single `running` claim printed "a turn is already running — Esc cancels it" and the words were gone, so the only way to talk to a working model was to kill the turn. The agent half already existed (`Agent.Steer` + the loop's steering drain, `internal/agent/loop.go`); what was missing was the half a person can see, and that is what this adds:

1. **Enter while working = queue + steer into the SAME run.** `internal/tui/queue.go` owns the pending list; `tui.App.SetQueueHandlers`'s `onQueue` seam resolves to `curAgent.Steer(text)` in `cmd/xdev/tui.go`, so the message is injected as a user message at the run's next step boundary and persisted by the loop. A host that declines (a guest room with no local run) returns false and the row is withdrawn — a row must never promise a delivery that will not happen.
2. **A visible, append-only, oldest-first list above the composer** — ordinal, kind mark (`❯` / `!` shell / `/` command), flattened one-line text, a `[send now]` button, and `+N more queued` when it overflows the five-row window. Order is never rewritten: a queue that re-sorts is a queue nobody trusts. A click on a row body is take-back — the text returns to the composer, newlines intact.
3. **Retirement is the agent's, not the UI's.** New `agent.Agent.SteeringDelivered func([]string)` fires from both steering drains (step boundary and post-final-tool-call) AFTER the message is persisted, and the TUI's `RetireDelivered` drops exactly those rows. A row used to have no way to know it had been sent; matching is by value oldest-first, so the same sentence typed twice retires twice, and a foreign steer (extension, mailbox) is a no-op.
4. **`[send now]` + F6 = interrupt, acknowledge, run.** A new remappable keymap action (`send-now`, listed in `/hotkeys`) and the per-row button both call the host's immediate-delivery seam: abort the live turn, print `· interrupted — delivering now`, wait (bounded, 2 s) for the run to release its turn claim, then persist the message and start a fresh turn. Interrupting rather than injecting is the honest reading of "now" — the in-flight provider request cannot be pre-empted. With an empty queue the chord says so instead of doing nothing silently.
5. **A row that outlives its run is flushed, not stranded.** `flushPendingQueue` is deferred first in the turn goroutine (LIFO: it runs last, after `running.Store(false)`) and turns the oldest undelivered row into the next turn, one row per run — a prompt typed as a run ended was never seen by the model, and the honest outcome is to run it, not to leave a row claiming otherwise.

Attachments are deliberately NOT queued: a queued image has no honest delivery story (the pixels are not in the steer channel), so it keeps the old path and comes back to the composer with its reason. The residual gap against CC's matrix (#157's remaining parts) is the take-back-ALL / Esc-flush gesture and the per-kind command policy (`/status`-class commands running immediately); those are untouched here.

*Last updated: 2026-10-02 (`fix/queue-delivery` — user-reported, three symptoms of one shape: "the new queue message never reach to the session, and when I click send now it failed the session too. and the queue message successfully sent to session need increate the turn number (right now it display wrong alwasy 0 at first message too) and the session history never show this sent queue message too"). **Three of the mid-turn queue's four promises were wired to something other than the session, so all three failed.** (1) **Delivery went to the wrong session.** `curAgent` was one process-wide pointer every turn overwrote, so with two sessions working whichever turn registered last also took every steer: a prompt typed into the foreground tab was injected into the background run, and its row retired off a conversation that never received it. The live agent is now per session — `tab.liveAgent` plus `tabset.setAgent/clearAgent/currentAgent/agentOf/liveAgents` — and `startTurn` registers it BEFORE publishing "running" (the spinner used to go up ~80 lines before the agent existed, and a submit in that window was refused with its row withdrawn). (2) **The queue itself was one shared list.** Every read — paint, take-back, flush, retire — ran over all entries, so a row could be painted over a session that never typed it and retired by another session's drain. Each entry now carries its owning session (`queuedEntry.session`) and every read/write filters on it (`queueForLocked`); `onSendNow` carries `(sid, text)` so a click or F6 after a tab switch names the row's own conversation, and `flushPendingQueue` drains only the session whose run ended. (3) **"Send now" could end as "send never", or end TWICE.** The bounded 2 s wait for the interrupted run to release its claim ended in a notice and nothing else: the row stayed pending, the slot stayed held, and every later submit was refused. The wait now falls through to a background goroutine that keeps waiting (bounded, `sendNowWaitSlow`) and completes the delivery itself, and it does so only while that session is still on screen. The row also stayed IN THE QUEUE until the delivery landed, so the interrupted run's own end-of-run flush found it and started the same message a second time — found by driving the binary: `SENDNOW` persisted twice into one session file, printed two ACK rows and counted two turns. Send-now now TAKES the row (`TakeQueued`) as its first act, before touching the turn, and EVERY path that does not deliver requeues — including the three that gave up early (slot never released, session parked, another turn took it). Taking the row up front without a requeue on every exit is just a silent drop, which is the "send never" bug wearing a quieter hat. `DropQueued` went with it: a drop-after-success is what left the row visible to the flush. (4) **A delivered prompt was invisible and uncounted.** `RetireDelivered` only removed the row, so the transcript never showed a ❯ for a prompt the model was answering, and the turn pill counted `AddTurn` at a run's END — which is why a session's first turn read 0 for as long as the model worked, and why three prompts steered into one run read as one turn. The turn seam is now ACCEPTANCE: `App.AddTurn` at the composer's send path, plus one per row in `RetireDelivered` (which also prints the ❯ row), and the run-end `AddTurn` call in `cmd/xdev/tui.go` is gone. (5) **The final-turn drain announced before the message was durable, and twice.** It fired `SteeringDelivered` at the drain and again after the persist, so one delivered message printed two ❯ rows and counted two turns, and a crash in between lost a prompt the UI had already shown as delivered. The drain no longer announces; the single announcement sits after the persist, which is the only place it is a fact. Tests: per-session agent registration and clear-vs-newer-turn (`cmd/xdev/tabset_test.go`), rows/delivery/turn-count/send-now-session ownership (`internal/tui/queue_test.go`), and announce-exactly-once-and-only-after-the-persist (`internal/agent/loop_steering_test.go`). Re-verified after every merge of `origin/main` — including the focus-fade (#548) and sandbox-the-suite (#550) work that rewrote `internal/tui/app.go` — by driving BOTH the stock and the fixed binary through one script against a local mock provider, twice: stock shows no turn pill while working and ends at `1t·2g` with neither queued prompt in the transcript; this branch shows `1t·0g` in flight and ends at `3t·2g` with both prompts as ❯ rows. Driving the binary is also what caught the send-now double delivery (`d9469d1`): the interrupted run's own end-of-run flush re-read a row that was still queued and started the message a second time, so send-now now TAKES its row before it aborts anything and requeues only if the delivery itself fails (`328ff5b`). Send-now was then driven three times against the merged binary through the same harness, checking the SESSION JSONL rather than the screen — a substring match over a full-screen TUI capture is repaint debris, while the store is the durable record: each run holds exactly one user entry for the send-now message, and the mock provider answered it once.*
