*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`feat/status-pills` follow-up — user-reported on the live row: "the icon is overlap the number"). **The gauge icon was a two-cell glyph, so it painted over the first digit of the time beside it.** `theme.HUDIcon(HUDIconGauge)` returned U+23F1 STOPWATCH, which runewwidth calls one cell but which most terminals paint TWO (East Asian Ambiguous): the status row writes the icon into one cell, the terminal's own second column lands on the first character of the number, and `⏱9s` reads as the clock sitting on the 9. Two fixes, because either alone is half the answer: **the shipped gauge is U+23F2 STOPWATCH** (Unambiguous, one cell in every terminal that has the glyph), and **`HUDIcon` cuts its result to a single character**, so a theme whose `symbols.overrides["hud.gauge"]` holds a two-cell glyph gets the same collision back — one character of icon, or nothing. ▤ and ✳ were already one cell everywhere. Pinned by `TestHUDIconIsAlwaysOneCharacter` (one character for all three defaults, the gauge is not U+23F1, a two-character override is cut) and re-verified on a real pty: the row replays cell by cell as `⏲13s · 1t·1g │ ▤9.4k · 99%`, the cell after the icon is the digit `1`, and every pill click and exit still holds.

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`feat/status-pills` — merge of origin/main, 4 commits behind). **Two conflicts, both main landing a new painter into the same tail of `paint()`.** `internal/tui/app.go`: main added the toast pass (`drawToasts`, #489) directly after `drawStatusRow`. Both are kept in paint order — the pill popup first (anchored to the status row), then the selection highlight, then the toasts last over everything, because a corner toast is the one thing that must never end up under a highlight or a picker frame. `docs/PRD.md`: four new footers, appended in date order.

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`feat/sidebar-toggle` — user-requested: "add the toggle sidebar to show/hide in xdev"). **The dock had a policy but no switch.** `Alt+S` walks the three states (`show` → `hide` → `auto`) and `/settings sidebarMode` is the same three, so a human who wanted the sidebar *gone* — the one thing both doors are bad at, since the cycle's next stop after `hide` is `auto` and the panel is back at 120+ columns — had no single move that hides it. `/sidebar [show|hide|auto]` (alias `/dock`) is that move: bare toggles what is actually painted, so an auto-closed panel is opened rather than "hidden" a second time, and it writes the same `sidebarMode` key through the same seam, so the command, the chord, the settings row and the global config cannot disagree. Verified: `go build ./...` and `go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./internal/tui/` green, including `TestSidebarCommandTogglesWhatIsOnScreen` (the toggle answers the screen, not the policy, persists both directions, and refuses an unknown word); the built binary driven through tmux at 160×40 — `/sidebar` → hide → show → `auto` each repainted the panel and the confirm line, `/sidebar sideways` printed the usage error, nothing reached the model.*
*Last updated 2026-09-30 (`fix/status-pill-click` — user-requested, three symptoms on the live status row: "remove icon in the bottom status bar", "make the tooltip when user click to metrics in status bard background color to black", "just 1 click to show, 1 click again to hide, no need to click and hold"). **Three changes, all in `internal/tui/statuspill.go`; the shipped layout is unchanged (`command │ time │ tokens`), so `defaultStatusSegments` and the keep-ranks did not move.**

**(1) The two pills lost their icons.** `pillLabel` no longer prefixes `theme.HUDIcon(HUDIconGauge)` (⏲) or `theme.HUDIcon(HUDIconDatabase)` (▤), so the row reads `1s · 1t·1g · 1516.3 t/s │ 50.1k · 97%` and gives the two cells back to the working-directory path. dsh draws an icon per pill because it draws a row of buttons in a browser; a terminal row is a fixed-cell grid whose every column is contested, and the icon only said which family a figure belonged to — a distinction the value's own unit already makes. The theme vocabulary is untouched: `theme.HUDIcon` still exists and the opt-in segments that wear a glyph (`rate`, `ttft`, `toolcalls`, `split`) keep theirs, so `symbols.overrides["hud.*"]` is still a live knob for a session that configured one of those.

**(2) The panel's field is the terminal's own background (SGR 49), not `theme.BgBase`.** The "black" the user asked for is the one call `#495` made for the sidebar: `BgBase` (`#141414`) is not black but a grey band, and it was painted under text cells that resolve to the terminal's black anyway — the same striping `#466` built out of the diff popup. On the dark terminal this reads black; on the built-in light theme it stays light rather than stranding dark label/value inks on a black box.

**(3) One click shows, one click hides — no press-and-hold.** The root cause was a one-word mistake, not a gesture: `handleStatusPopupMouse` dismissed the panel on ANY mouse report outside it, and the release of the very gesture that opened it lands on the pill's own cell — the status row, one row BELOW the panel that just went up. So the button-up closed it again and the popup read as a hold-to-peek. The dismiss is now gated on `press`, so only a new click elsewhere closes it (clicking the other pill still switches panels, click-outside still dismisses, Esc still closes).

Tests: `internal/tui/statuspill_test.go` gains `TestPillStaysUpUntilTheNextClick` (the whole gesture driven through `handleKey` — press, paint, release, paint — must leave the panel up, and a second click must take it down; it fails on `origin/main` with "the panel closed on the button-up") and `TestPillPopupIsTheTerminalBackground` (an interior cell and a label cell must both resolve to `ColorDefault` while the built-in theme still names `bg_base`; fails on `origin/main` with "popup cell (116,21) ' ' has background #141414"). `TestPillsReadDshsOwnLabels` now asserts the row wears neither ⏲ nor ▤, and the icon-needle rows in `chrome_test.go` / `usage_test.go` / `sidebar_fullheight_test.go` / `cmd/xdev/tui_metric_wire_test.go` match the bare figures; `TestCacheSegmentCarriesNoSecondTotal` loses its "the database icon is drawn exactly once" claim, which was a claim about the pills, not about the `cache` segment it tests.

`go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/tui/`, `go test ./internal/tui/ ./cmd/xdev/` green (the two `cmd/xdev` failures, `TestConnectPickerItems` and `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills`, fail identically on `origin/main` — they read this machine's `~/.xdev` and `~/.xdev/agent/skills`, not the repo). **Verified live, not only in unit tests:** the built binary on a pty at 160×40 with `env -u NO_COLOR`, a stub gateway streaming a real turn so both pills carry a session, and SGR 1006 reports fed as the bytes a terminal sends. The status row paints `1s · 1t·1g · 1516.3 t/s │ 50.1k · 97%`, one click on the time pill brings up `Session statistics` (`turns / steps 1 / 1`, `LLM time`, `decode speed`, `active time`) and it is still up after the release, a second click takes it down, one click on the token pill shows `Token usage`, and a click on the transcript dismisses. Every cell of the panel's interior resolves to SGR 49, i.e. the terminal's own background.

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`feat/bash-live-output`). **A running `bash` was a spinner and nothing else — its output appeared all at once, after the command finished.** `runShell`'s copier goroutines wrote straight into the head+tail `OutputSink`s and nothing else was told, so the transcript had nothing to paint until `Execute` returned; a three-second build looked like a hang and a ten-second test looked like one twice. **A live-output seam, four pieces, no change to what the model sees.** `tool.OutputObserver` ([internal/tool/liveoutput.go](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/blob/main/internal/tool/liveoutput.go)) rides the CALL CONTEXT (`WithOutputObserver`/`OutputObserverOf`), not a tool field, because one `BashTool` instance serves every concurrent call — so the call id has to travel with the bytes. `switchWriter` grew a `tap` and calls it AFTER the sink write, under its own lock: the sink gets the bytes first, so a slow observer can delay the transcript but never the child whose pipe would back up. `Agent.OnOutput func(callID, name, chunk string)` is the agent-level seam; `runOneTool` wraps it per call so two concurrent `bash` calls' output can be routed — the same mis-pairing `FinishTool` is matched by call id to avoid. Only the TUI wires it (`app.AppendToolOutput`); print mode, RPC and every tool that does not stream (read, grep, MCP) keep the untouched context. **The TUI side is one block, opened by the first chunk.** `App.AppendToolOutput(callID, name, chunk)` finds or opens a `Block{Kind: KindToolDone, Live: true}` **spliced under its own call row** — never appended at the tail, which for a concurrent batch is whatever call started last — bounded at 64 KiB of tail (`liveTailBytes`), and stamped `liveSeq` at most once per 100 ms (`livePaint`, the ~10 fps the user picked) so a tool flooding chunks costs the frames a reader can see, not one frame per chunk. The stamp is on `blockKey` as well as `Block`, because length alone does not move for a progress bar rewriting the same window of bytes. A live box renders its newest `liveRows` (= `toolRecentHead`) rows and **no footer** — a running command has no exit code it has earned, and no Ctrl+O over output that is still arriving. `FinishTool` REPLACES the box with the settled result (one command, one box: two is the transcript lying about what ran). A tool that streams nothing opens no box, and a chunk for a call that never ran or has settled is dropped, so `!bash` / composer shell mode and every silent tool are byte-for-byte unchanged.  Mutation-verified: deleting `live: b.liveSeq` from `blockKey` fails `TestLiveBoxRepaintsOnceTheTailCapFreezesItsLength` (a flooding command's box freezes once the tail cap holds its length steady) and `TestLiveBoxStampCoalescesButAlwaysMoves`; deleting the wire (`Agent.OnOutput`) fails `TestLiveOutputReachesTheScreenWhileTheCallRuns`.  **Driven by hand against the real binary, in a pty, A/B against the base commit** (worktree `live-base-probe` at `6d5f0c0`, both built from source, one scripted `openai-completions` server on `127.0.0.1` driving a real `bash`): the tool call runs `for i in 1..8; do echo tick-$i; sleep 0.4; done` (~3.3s). **With live output each line reaches the terminal as it is produced — 0.2s, 0.6s, 1.0s, 1.5s, 1.9s, 2.3s, 2.7s, 3.1s, ~0.4s apart, matching the command exactly. On the base build all eight appear at 3.5s, in one burst, when the command exits** — which is the spinner-and-nothing defect this change exists to remove, measured rather than argued. The settled result is identical on both (the tail is present either way), so nothing the model sees moved. The 4,000-line flood case (`i=0; while [ $i -lt 4000 ]`) also completes and settles with the tail intact on both builds. Pinned by `TestBashOutputObserverSeesStreamWhileRunning` (a chunk exists BEFORE `Execute` returns), `TestOnOutputCarriesTheCallIdentity` (concurrent calls each stamped with their own id), `TestLiveBoxSitsUnderItsOwnCallRow` / `TestLiveBoxSpliceInvalidatesTheRowIndex` (splice position and the index-aligned row index), `TestLiveBoxStampCoalescesButAlwaysMoves` (coalescing, and a same-length rewrite still repaints), `TestLiveBoxRepaintsOnceTheTailCapFreezesItsLength` (the cap itself manufactures the same-length rewrite, so the box's own bound is where a length-only stamp would freeze), `TestLiveBoxIsBoundedAndKeepsTheTail`, `TestFinishToolReplacesTheLiveBox`, `TestFinishToolKeepsTheResultUnderItsCallRow` (the settled result rewrites the box's slot rather than being appended above a later call's row), and — at the host boundary, on SCREEN rather than in app state — `TestWiredTUIHostPaintsARunningBashCall` and `TestLiveOutputReachesTheScreenWhileTheCallRuns` (a real `bash`, the real `tuiHooks`, the real paint loop, read off a simulation screen).


*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`docs/prd-syntax-highlight`). **A PRD line described a feature that does not exist.** The scrollback block-styling bullet promised "fenced code with syntax highlighting via bundled tmTheme mapping" — `internal/tui/markdown.go` paints every fenced line as one run in the body ink as a single `md_text`-coloured run on the `md_code_bg` band (`md_code` is the INLINE-code ink, not the fenced one), no `.tmTheme` exists anywhere in the repo, and `grep -rn "theme.Syntax" internal/ --include=*.go | grep -v ^internal/theme/` returns nothing, so all 9 `syntax_*` tokens are settable and unread. The line now states what renders and names the theme tokens as unconsumed; the feature itself moves to [#501](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/501) rather than being quietly deleted, since the tokens are the intended surface. No code changed.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`feat/code-highlight`, closing [#501](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/501)). **The 9 `syntax_*` tokens finally have a consumer.** A fenced block was one run in the body ink, so comment, string and keyword painted identically; `internal/tui/highlight.go` is a hand-written per-line scanner that maps each token onto one of the 9 existing slots, with the launch palettes pinning all 9 from the same TokyoNight Night accents the rest of groknight uses. No tree-sitter and no tmTheme: the constraint is `go.mod`'s CGO-free guarantee (§3.6), the same one that keeps `ast_grep` shelling out to the `ast-grep` binary. The honest limits, all pinned by tests: it lexes ONE line at a time, so a block comment spanning lines is coloured in pieces; a mis-token paints the wrong colour and never drops or reorders a byte (`runsString(runs)` is byte-identical to the source line, which is what the selection/copy path reads). `NO_COLOR` is gated in the RENDERER rather than left to tcell — tcell drops colour at emission, so an ink chosen here would appear in every cell dump and screenshot while the terminal showed a single colour. `internal/tui/markdown.go` is the only render call site that changed: the fence line now records the info string instead of toggling a bare boolean.*

*Last updated: 2026-10-02 (`fix/mermaid-render` — the parser refused diagrams agents actually write: edge chains, inline edge labels, bare shaped nodes, commas in labels, subgraphs, sequence activation and control blocks, and `stateDiagram-v2` all fell back to the source). Three common cases render now: **an edge chain** `A --> B --> C` is two edges off three nodes — the old walker consumed the first arrow and then read the second arrow as the target; **an inline edge label** `D -- resume() --> B` and its dotted sibling `T -. isolated .-> B`, where the closing arrow is as far away as the label is long, so the arrow scan is bracket- and quote-aware rather than a token list; **a bare node** `IDX[("Index")]`, which has no edge at all and used to be a whole-block fallback. A comma inside a bracketed label is the label (`A[docs, notes, specs]`), and a `subgraph` is a frame around its members rather than a refusal. **On a sequence diagram** the `+` of `A->>+B` and the `-` of `B-->>-A` are activation markers, not part of a name, and a control block (`loop`, `alt`, `else`, `opt`, `par`, `critical`, `rect`) is a labelled row whose messages still draw. **`stateDiagram-v2` is a flowchart in different punctuation** — a transition is `A --> B: event` not `A -->|event| B`, `[*]` is the marker, `state "Long" as id` names a state — so it is translated to the flow spelling and handed to the same reader, one graph, one layout, one drawer. Unreadable source still falls back whole; the fallback is the safety property and it did not move. **An answer that ends in a ```mermaid fence was a code band** — the reader had the source and no picture, which is the one thing a diagram is for. `internal/tui/mermaid.go` now parses a fence and draws it, and `renderMarkdown` gives the `mermaid` fence tag first refusal on its body: `fenceLang` reads the info string, `fenceBody` gathers the block, `mermaidLines` draws it, and **every non-result is a nil that puts the code band back exactly as it was** — an unsupported type (class/state/ER/gantt/xychart), a source the parser cannot read, a subgraph, or a diagram that cannot fit the terminal. The fallback is the whole promise: a wrong diagram is worse than source, because a reader cannot tell a parser dropped a line.

Two types render, and the split is the one that survives a terminal. **A sequence diagram is a lifeline grid** (`drawSequence`): one boxed head per participant, a dotted lifeline per column, and per message a wire row (a solid run with `▶` for a call, a dotted run with `◀` for a reply) with its label on the row below, so an arrow never shares a row with the text that would cross it. A self-message is a two-row hook; a `Note over A,B` is a `║ … ║` band. A participant that never appears in a message is dropped rather than given a truncated column, and a diagram whose participant columns do not fit becomes a **call log** (`drawSequenceLog`) — every message still its own rows, endpoints spelled out, labels wrapped as prose. Geometry is lost, content is not. **A flowchart is a graph, so it gets a layout** (`drawFlow` → `drawFlowAt`): longest-path ranks, boxes 3 rows tall, TD ranks stacked with a label row, an elbow row and a head row between them, edges drawn as straight elbows with a label on the row above the horizontal run, and a last-resort **outline** listing (node, then its edges) when no layout fits.

The drawing happens on a rune canvas with a parallel role canvas (`grid`), which is what lets the roles reach the paint: box/wire `md_muted`, arrowhead `md_code`, node label bold `md_code`, body `text_secondary`, note italic `gray_dim` — and the rows are cut into runs **by rune, not byte**, because a byte-indexed split slices a multi-byte box glyph in half and the transcript then paints a replacement character where the wire should be. `mmGlyphs` swaps the whole vocabulary for the `ascii` symbol preset. Everything is bounded: ≤ 240 rows, ≤ 80 nodes, ≤ 200 messages, ≤ 32 cells per label.

Config: **`renderMermaid` (default on, `*bool` for `showThinking`'s reason — the zero-skip merge cannot tell unset from off)**, on the repo-safe key list, in `xdev config list`, in `fallbackValue`, one toggle row in the Alt+, settings panel (applied live through `applySettingImmediate`), and `/settings renderMermaid [on|off]` persisting through the new `SettingsOps.SetMermaid` seam. It is display-only: `SetRenderMermaid` drops the render cache and the new `blockKey.mermaid` bit re-stamps every block, so a flip repaints the transcript in both directions and the source text is never touched.

Tests: `internal/tui/mermaid_test.go` — the call-chain fixture (four participants, a storage hop, a note, a reply) drawn in full; arrow direction per message; the narrow collapse keeping all six messages; self-message hook; every flow edge drawing a head; fan-out spreading into three columns; a cycle terminating the ranking; unsupported types and unreadable sources falling back; the edge-chain case drawing every hop; both edge-label spellings and the dotted one reaching the render; a bare shaped node; a comma in a label not inventing a node; a subgraph rendering; activation markers; the three control blocks; `stateDiagram-v2` with its event labels and its `state ... as` naming; every rendered row fitting at w = 10…200; the ascii preset emitting no non-ASCII; three distinct inks reaching the paint; the fence tag deciding in Markdown; the fallback banding its rows; the live two-way toggle; and the slash setting persisting. `internal/config/settings_test.go` gains `TestRenderMermaidDefaultsOnAndHonoursExplicitFalse` (default on, project layer wins, explicit `false` survives the merge, `config set` reaches the key). `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/tui/ ./internal/config/ ./cmd/xdev/`, `go test ./internal/tui/ ./internal/config/` green.

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`fix/welcome-logo`). **Welcome wordmark is Omarchy-style capital XDEV.** Drawn in Delta Corps Priest 1 (the FIGlet face `omarchy-ascii` / `logo.txt` use at github.com/omacom/omarchy) — capital `XDEV` at ~50 cells × 8 rows, monogram capital-X fallback under that fit. Sheen still rides the assistant accent; binary tagline only on the full mark. The lowercase pixel-grid mark is gone: Omarchy's face is uppercase-only and a small `x` lacked the weight. `TestLogoOmarchyFace` pins width, half-block strokes, and monogram = leading X.

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`fix/welcome-logo` E legibility). **The condensed E read as noise beside D.** Re-cut as a left stem with three full horizontal bars (top / mid / bottom) so the letter is unmistakable at logo size; X, D and tapering V unchanged on the 41-cell grid.

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`fix/welcome-logo` artful E). **E stays legible and looks less like a slab.** Three bars on a left stem keep it readable beside D; Omarchy-style ▄/▀ soft ends on the top/bottom bars restore the hand-fitted logo.txt feel without the earlier unreadable condensed E.

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`fix/think-box-one-row` — user-reported: "change thinking box to 1 rows display only", then "when click the box increase the height like current one which focused and scrollable, and click to that box again or click outside to lost focus, the thinking box back to 1 row again"): **a reasoning box was 12 rows before anyone asked for it.** Every thought painted a fixed 12-row window plus a hidden-row notice, so a turn with four thoughts spent 50+ rows of scrollback on frame and the answer arrived below the fold. The box now has one collapsed row — the newest thought, tail-anchored, no notice, since a notice would cost the only row there is — and a click grows it to the window that already existed: bold border, `… N rows hidden (Ctrl+O to expand)`, wheel-scrolled. The focus is the one that was already there (`App.thinkFocus`, set by the press arm in `selection.go`, drawn bold), so no new state: height is read off it, and a second click on the box or a click anywhere else puts it back to one row with `ThinkOff` intact, so a re-opened box is where it was left. `Ctrl+O` (Block.Expanded) is the one height focus does not own. `thinkWindow`/`thinkMaxOff` now take the height (`thinkWindow(n, off, rows)`) so the render and the wheel cannot disagree about where the box stops.

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`fix/stream-end-retry` — user-reported: "xdev not retry with this error: error: agent: stream: openai-completions: stream ended without finish_reason"). **The error was already classified transient; the ladder just never saw it in one shape.** `ai.Classify` reads the adapter's own sentinel through `prematureCloseRe` (`internal/ai/errors.go`), and `recovery_test.go` already pins the pre-content case — a stream that dies before any content is retried and the run resumes. What escaped was the other shape, and it carries the *same* message: a stream cut **after a tool call started, with no text behind it**. `oneTurn` had set `emitted = true` on the call's `EventToolcallStart`, so the ladder took the post-content branch — but there was no partial to retain (the adapters emit `EventToolcallEnd` only on their terminal path, so a call whose stream dies is never persisted) and `canRetain` was false, so `return nil, history, err` surfaced the error raw. The session ended on a failure the ladder was built to survive.

**The change:** the turn now reports what it left behind. `turnError.orphanToolCalls` counts the unpaired calls, and the transient branch treats that one case as replayable rather than un-continuable: `if turnContentEmitted(err) && orphans == 0` gates the retain-and-continue arm, so a cut with no partial falls through to the pre-content ladder (backoff → replay, then the same failover/escalation a cold death gets). The orphan itself is *not* persisted — a whole-turn re-send regenerates the call from the same history, which is exactly what a retained partial could not do, and a re-sent unpaired call is the shape providers reject (the reason tool calls are excluded from partials in the first place).

Deliberately narrow: only a cut that rendered **nothing** is replayed this way. Once text is on screen, replaying would double-emit it, so those turns keep the retain-and-continue path and its `maxPostContentContinuations` ceiling untouched.

Tests: `internal/agent/recovery_test.go` (`TestStreamEndedMidToolCallReplaysTheTurn` — the exact adapter sentinel, delivered after a `toolcall_start` with a half-streamed argument, must replay the turn and answer, with no request carrying the orphaned call; fails on stock `main` with `a cut mid-tool-call must be replayed, not surfaced: agent: stream: openai-completions: stream ended without finish_reason`, which is the bug in one assertion). Existing pins unchanged and green: `TestStreamEndedWithoutFinishReasonRetriesAndResumes` (the pre-content half), `TestRetryPostContentSecondFailureSurfaces` and `TestPostContentContinuationIsBoundedUnderInfiniteRetry` (the retained-partial budget), `TestMalformedStreamRetriesAndResumes` / `TestMalformedStreamAfterContentRetainsAndContinues` (the sibling clean-EOF shapes). Verified: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/agent/`, `gofmt` clean, `go test ./internal/agent/ ./internal/ai/...` green.

Note: this entry covers the mid-tool-call shape only. The pre-content and post-text shapes of this error already retried before this change. A report of "not retrying" that carried no `· stream error — retrying (xN)` line above it and no `recovery:` line in the file is a different failure — a subagent's own `Run` (`internal/agent/subagent.go`), or the `maxPostContentContinuations = 3` budget draining after consecutive mid-message cuts, which surfaces by design — and is not addressed here.

*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (feat/session-tabs): the TUI holds a live session set (`tabset`, up to 6 open stores). `/new` and `/resume` park the previous session instead of closing it — its turn keeps running, and `tuiHooks` only paints while that session is focused (parked output raises an unread badge). Alt+]/Alt+[ cycle the set; Alt+Shift+]/[ jump to unread; the status row's `sessions` segment shows `N tabs ·busy ✦unread`. Esc aborts only the foreground session. This is the opencode session-tabs model (route.navigate + per-session-id RunCoordinator) ported to xdev's single-process TUI: no server, no tab strip paint yet — the badge and the cycle chords are the whole UI. Issue #131 (background-session fleet / `--bg`) stays separate: that is multi-process attach, this is multi-session in one TUI.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (feat/session-bg, issue #131 slice 1): `xdev --bg "prompt"` detaches a print-mode worker (new session via setsid) so the job survives terminal kill. Status + log live under `~/.xdev/agent/bg/<id>/`; `xdev bg list|logs|stop|rm` manage them. Hang caps: --bg defaults `--max-time` to 2h when unset; stream first-progress/idle watchdogs and `--max-turns` still apply. No supervisor daemon and no attach TUI yet — those are the #131 residual.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (`fix/stuck-quit`). **A stuck xdev was un-exitable: Ctrl+C, Ctrl+D, nothing.** The stall watchdog wrote a dump and left the user in a raw-mode alt screen with a live process, because the quit chord is applied by the very loop that had wedged — a loop blocked 17m inside tcell's synchronous tty write reads no key at all (session 00b1c5a0; the worst of 13 dumps that day). Now the poll goroutine serves the `quit` chord whenever the watchdog has condemned the loop, and a loop still wedged ten minutes after its first dump restores the terminal and exits non-zero instead of living forever. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/tui/ ./cmd/xdev/`, `go test ./internal/tui/ -race` green for the new tests (the two `cmd/xdev` failures and the `TestBeatDoneNamesASlowIteration` / `TestOnlyTheReadError` races fail identically on `origin/main`).*
*Last updated: 2026-09-30 (feat/tui-exit-detach): quitting the TUI with a turn in flight **detaches by default** (opencode parity — the TUI is a client, the run outlives it). Setting `tui.exitDetach` (default on; `xdev config set tui.exitDetach false` or Alt+, "Detach on quit") restores kill-on-exit. Detach cancels the in-process turn and spawns a `--bg` print worker that `--resume`s the same session; hang caps are the bg defaults (2h max-time + stream watchdogs). Esc stays cancel-only.*
*Last updated: 2026-10-01 (`fix/bare-thinking-label` — user-reported: "the sidebar now showing the model like the divider too"). **The reasoning readout said "thinking" twice over.** `App.thinkingLabel` prefixed the rung with the word, so the divider painted `onegw/…-free · thinking high` and the SESSION footer a bare `thinking high` row — beside a model name, so a rung like `high` on its own line read as a second name. The seam is now `App.thinkingLevel`: the bare rung, nothing else, unwired still empty. The divider is `model · <level>`, and the footer pairs the model and the level in ONE row (`onegw/…-free · high`) — the two are one request, and a row that pairs them cannot be misread the way a bare `high` under a path and a branch could. `/thinking` keeps its name: the word is the command, the readout is the answer.*
*Last updated: 2026-10-02 (`feat/sticky-prompt` — user-requested: "the latest user prompt show the sticky box in top like grok cli"). **A user prompt now pins to the top of the transcript once you scroll past it, exactly as the Grok CLI does** — verified against grok-build `scrollback/sticky.rs` (fetched 2026-10-02) and driven live in a 100×30 tmux pane: scroll up mid-answer and the request stays pinned above it, shrinking one row per row scrolled down to a 3-row floor with ` …` admitting the hidden text; the next prompt's arrival clips it away with no gap, so the stream never jumps. The port is ~90 lines of 1D coordinate math in `internal/tui/sticky.go` (no screen needed to test it) plus ~20 lines in `paint()`, and it leaves the scroll model, the row-0 top bar and every other surface alone: the header owns exactly the rows it hides, so one row of scroll still moves the bottom line by one row. Verified live in a real 100×30 and a 90×16 pane: 1-line prompt pinned, multi-line prompt at its floor, prompt pushed off by the next one, 60×14 narrow pane, and a resize with a pinned header.*
*Last updated: 2026-10-02 (`feat/sticky-prompt`, second commit — a live click-through found what the unit tests could not). **The header must not move the rows underneath it.** The first cut shifted every row-index hit-test below the header by the header's height, on the assumption the header *inserted* rows; it does not — it re-renders the viewport's first rows in front of them, so the whole transcript moved one header-height up in every mapping that mattered: a click on a reasoning box, on an inline user prompt, or on a stream row under a pinned header opened nothing, and a drag across the boundary copied the header's text twice. Fixed in `57bff5c`: the mapping below the header is the viewport's own, unchanged, and only the header's own rows resolve through the published geometry; a row the header paints over has no screen position of its own and copies as blank, which is what a row scrolled out of view already did. Five tests (the inline row's document row, the cache keys, the push's middle rows, pinned-row click vs inline-row click, one drag copying each row exactly once) plus a live 110×26 pane driving both menus.*
1. **Prompt-injection scanning** (#81): the §5 decision is unimplemented — no scanner exists in the Go code.
2. **Launch flags with no consumer** (#105): after 48459fe only `--external-thinking`, `--service-tier`, `--provider-session-id`, `--prompt-cache-key` remain genuinely unregistered (the 2026-09-12 list claiming `--allow-home/--add-dir/--no-pty/--plugin-dir` among them is stale — all four landed with consumers; `--no-pty` is a documented intentional no-op).
3. **JS eval kernel** (#111): stays a by-design rejection (no JS runtime CGO-free); the py kernel and notebook virtual text shipped — but omp's `eval.py`/`eval.js` disable gates have no equivalent.
4. **Chrome extension for browser-relay** (#112): the Go relay daemon shipped; the browser-side extension did not.
5. **M4 structural frame-plan tail** (#78): all nine boxes re-verified open 2026-09-13 (frame-plan contract, block states, DSR resize, alt-buffer, sticky headers/folds/diff rows/highlighting, bracketed paste, compact toggle, hex verification, terminal matrix).
6. **TTSR injection persistence** (#113): the §2 scope row for TTSR names
   `ttsr_injection` persistence (a session entry recording an injected
   rule-violation notice); no such entry type exists. `ttsr_injection` and
   `Violations` have zero hits in Go, `internal/session/entries.go` enumerates
   xdev's ten entry types without it, and the code states the alternative
   outright at `cmd/xdev/print.go:544`: *"fired state is in-session only, never
   persisted"*. So M11's "Complete" covers the engine (regex + `astCondition` +
   per-kind `interruptMode` gating + the thinking lane), not this record.
   Adding it means reversing that in-session-only decision on purpose (new
   entry type, retention, cross-process read) — hence #113, split out of #35,
   whose closeout comment now states the engine/arming landed and this pair
   did not. (#95 covers a different TTSR slice: scope/globs + notice attrs.)

**Build/portability ceiling (measured 2026-09-13):** the contract is the six CI
targets (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:104-109`: darwin/linux/windows × amd64/arm64,
all 64-bit) plus `linux/386` as a deliberate 32-bit extra — which is exactly
what caught `internal/theme` initializing an `int` sentinel with `1 << 32`, a
compile error wherever `int` is 32 bits (fixed in 6f53965). Sweeping wider
produces failures that are not xdev's: `linux/arm`, `windows/arm` and similar
die inside `internal/memory → modernc.org/sqlite → modernc.org/libc` with
"build constraints exclude all Go files", i.e. **xdev's platform ceiling is
whatever `modernc/libc` supports**, not `go tool dist list`. Recorded so a
future sweep reads those as the known ceiling rather than new breakage.

**Landed 2026-09-15 (resume line on TUI exit, user-requested; the one hermes
copy the research doc called cheap UX):** `runTUI` registers its exit defer
*before* the store-close and `scr.Fini` defers, so LIFO order prints after the
alt screen is released — anything written earlier is wiped by Fini. The line is
the bare, copy-pasteable command (`resumeHint`/`resumeCommand`/`resumeProg`,
`cmd/xdev/sessionops.go`): the invoked name (argv0 basename, `.exe` trimmed,
`xdev` when argv0 is unusable) so a shell that installs the binary as `omp` gets
a line that pastes back, then `--resume <session uuid>`. It is printed only when
the target is reachable: `resolveResumeID(cwd, store.ID())` must land on the
store's own path, which is what turns "--no-session", a chat that never
materialized, a `/drop`ped file, and a store resumed from another directory into
silence instead of a command that errors. The defer reads `store` at exit, so
/new, /fork and /resume change what the line names. Print/RPC/ACP modes stay
byte-clean.
**Landed 2026-09-15 (top bar is permanent, user-requested):** the welcome screen's top bar (`❯ branch` left — the working directory is deliberately not shown: the status row carries it — model right, grok top_bar.rs) now also owns row 0 of the transcript view and carries the **last user prompt** collapsed to one line (`· <first line>`, whitespace/tabs sanitized, clipped with `…`), so the request the session is answering never scrolls away. Narrow windows shed the model first, then clip the prompt; the transcript viewport starts below the bar (`transcriptTop`), `viewportLinesLocked` accounts for it, and the selection geometry (doc anchoring, edge auto-scroll, row capture vs grid fallback) converts screen↔viewport rows by the same offset. A press on the bar is a chrome gesture — the bar itself is copyable. Regression tests: `TestTopBarCarriesBranchAndLastPrompt`; the selection/scroll-hint suites pin the shifted layout.

**Landed 2026-09-14 (#244, goal continuation — the goal actually runs):** `/goal create|resume` starts the goal's first turn (the objective becomes the session title and the first `❯` prompt) and an active goal keeps the run going from there: a turn that yields with no tool calls re-enters with the hidden `goal-continuation` prompt instead of ending the run, so the objective is worked on without a user nudge. It is **TUI-only** — omp's `goal.continuationModes` defaults to `interactive`, and a print/RPC/ACP run must not spend turns of its own after its prompt is answered — and the loop ends when the goal is completed, dropped, budget-exhausted, or the run's turn budget is spent (Esc cancels the turn). Verified live: `/goal create "…NO tool calls as your first message, then create … and mark the goal complete"` → assistant plan-only message → `message user attribution=goal-continuation` in the session JSONL → work → `goal_updated completed` → idle. Residual (still omp-divergent, tracked in #244): no `/goal pause`/`budget` verbs, no mid-turn accounting flush, and the continuation mode set is not configurable.

**Landed 2026-09-13 (parity sweep, 9081444 back through 48459fe):** #79 deferred-catalog runner wired in all four modes · #80 secrets redactor in TUI/RPC (one shared `wireAgentMode` seam so modes cannot drift again) · #90 mailbox push delivery (poller started; a declined message now stays unread instead of being consumed by a poller nobody listened to) · #107 ai-title cascade consuming TITLE_SYSTEM.md + `/rename` (in-place fixed-width slot rewrite) · #106 interactive ask card wired (it shipped with zero callers) · #108 glob-scoped rulebooks fire at edit/write time (`rules.ForPath` had no consumer) · #92 hook payload key names + the exit-2-vs-warning contract · #94 `ask` batch shape · #91 task batch shape + no-yield child nudge · #20-style inert-flag class: print-mode advisor, headless `-plan` read-only promise, `agent_end` nil payload, roster Alt+A + empty-roster open, `/goal` verbs, composer history recall, Esc/Ctrl+C cancel (`withMaxTime` returned a no-op cancel with no `--max-time`), ACP transport (was LSP `Content-Length` framing, now the spec's newline-delimited JSON, plus a real frame size cap). Second wave (a1bf404…9081444): #84 fallback depth armed (a declared `retry.fallbackChains` order wins over the window ranking, credential-pool rotation, TUI notify) · #85 installed plugins contribute commands/skills/agents/hooks · #86 hindsight cadence in the TUI and compaction carries the recalled memories · #82 `compaction.idleAfter`/`async` reach `CompactionConfig` and an abort cancels the in-flight summarize · #88 the notes-tools and autolearn gates have consumers, the lesson cap is a setting, and learn redacts at store time · #89 sharpshooter friction feed runs per TUI turn · #102 install-id is attached on the wire (Claude `metadata.user_id`, Codex `user`) with the broker's duplicate mint collapsed · #104 `auth-broker`/`auth-gateway`/`browser-relay`/`install` work at the top level · #92 `auto_retry_*` and `session_shutdown` have emission points. Wave 3 (b3d5043…6a19854): background subagents are no longer killed when the turn that spawned them ends (jobs detach from the tool context and are reaped at session close) · #83 branch summaries write a real note through @tiny/@smol behind `branchSummary.*` with the marker as the fallback, and snapcompact is gated on the model’s `vision` capability · `/theme list` aliases the listing · the whole "settings key exists, nothing reads it" class now has a guard test each.

**Known gaps — resolved (2026-09-10, commit c96f3f2):**
1. **`/<command>` slash commands now work** (M10 #11 first slice): built-in registry (`/new` `/clear` `/drop` `/help` `/quit` + `/q`) routed at input-submit before any user block is created; markdown commands discovered from `<cwd>/.xdev/commands/*.md` + `~/.xdev/agent/commands/*.md` (project beats user, non-recursive, hidden/non-md skipped) with frontmatter `name:`/`description:`, quote-aware `$1..$n`/`$@`/`$@[start]`/`$@[start:length]`/`$ARGUMENTS` expansion and the no-placeholder blank-line append fallback; unknown `/foo` still falls through as literal prompt text. Lifecycle wired to the session store: `/new` swaps a fresh session file, `/clear` appends a durable `reset_boundary` and resets in place, `/drop` deletes the file and starts fresh — all refuse while a turn is in flight. Remaining M10 scope (fork/resume/picker, context files, keybindings, /dump) unchanged.
2. **TUI scrollback implemented** (P0 #17): a tail-anchored viewport scroll model (`internal/tui/scroll.go`) — offset counts lines above the live tail, follow semantics pin streaming output to the tail, scrolled position survives appends/shrinks, clamped at both ends. Keys: PgUp/PgDn, Ctrl+B/F, arrows (when not running), Home/End jump to top/live; `▲ n ▼ n` indicator when content hides. Contract-tested (scroll model table) plus an integration test through the real key path. Fold/expand of tool blocks remains M4 tail scope.
   - **omp-parity (2026-09-14, e326f6b):** the tail-anchored model gained a proportional right-edge `│` track / `█` thumb (`scrollModel.Scrollbar`, thumb `⌊vp·vp/total⌋`) painted over a reserved last column, so position feedback is continuous rather than the `▲ n ▼ n` text hint alone (the hint stays on the composer divider); PgUp/PgDn/Ctrl+B/F now step a full viewport−1 (`scrollPage`) instead of half a screen, matching omp's `ScrollView.page`; and finished tool results render framed in a rounded box (`╭─ name · state (dur) ─╮ … ╰─╯`, `toolBoxLines`) instead of `┃`-railed lines under a `↳ ok (dur)` header — errors tint the whole frame, the head+tail row window and elision marker carry inside, and the boxed block drops its leading rail.
   - **Jump-to-latest chip (2026-09-29, `feat/jump-to-latest`):** scrolling up in a live session showed only the `▲ n ▼ n` counter on the composer divider — a readout, not a control, so catching up after a long stream meant mashing Ctrl+F blind. `paint()` now paints a `↓ n new` pill over the transcript's bottom-right corner whenever rows are hidden below the viewport (`drawJumpChip`, `App.jump` publishes the rectangle), and the press branch in `handleMouse` claims it exactly like the scrollbar does: the gesture spends itself on the button and never anchors a selection on the row behind it. The count is the scroll model's own hidden-row total, so the chip appears and disappears with the tail (nothing hidden → no button, and the cells it would have covered stay selectable) and the rect is cleared every frame, so a chip that is not on screen cannot be clicked. Placed on the transcript's own edge, not the terminal's, so it stays with the content when the context dock is open. Mouse-only by design: `End` already jumps to the tail, so a new chord would have added `/hotkeys` churn for no reachable feature. Tests: `internal/tui/jumpchip_test.go` (count, click, no-chip-at-the-tail).
3. **Agent turn budget graceful** (P0 #18): `MaxTurns` const → `DefaultMaxTurns = 200` + `Agent.MaxTurns` field (0 = default) + `-max-turns` flag (0 = default). At the cap the agent persists a synthetic user wrap-up message ("turn budget reached — wrap up…") and runs exactly one final turn whose tool calls are deliberately left dangling (neutralized on rebuild); the run ends nil-error with a normal assistant status report instead of the fatal `exceeded N turns`. Runaway-loop protection intact (cap applies per run; unit tests cover wrap-up, default resolution, and bounded runaway loops).
4. **Tool results print in full** (P0, 2026-09-10): the TUI no longer collapses tool output to a 200-char one-line preview — the tool-result block renders the complete sink-windowed output the model sees (bash: 16KB head+tail per stream), wrapped, inside a rounded box whose footer carries the outcome the user needs (`⟦Wall: 70ms | Exit: 9 | output truncated⟧`, error tinted), and whose hidden rows carry an *actionable* `… N lines hidden (Ctrl+O to expand)` affordance instead of the unreachable `(log)` marker of the original. The call row above names the call as the tool and its primary argument, never the raw JSON the model sent — and (2026-09-30, `fix/tool-call-wrap`) it no longer clips that argument to the row width: a command longer than the row WRAPS onto continuation rows, and `toolDetail` no longer stops the phrase at 400 bytes. See the footer stamp for the full change. Error results moved from the system-block path into the tool-result block so their output prints too. Ctrl+O expands one result at a time to its full (sink-bounded) length; print mode emits `res.Text` after the footer — headless runs no longer lose bash output. Framed output is sanitized omp-parity first (2026-09-15): `sanitizeOutput` expands tabs to the 3-cell omp stop and drops ANSI/control sequences before any width math — a tab measures 0 cells but paints as an advance to the next 8-column stop, so unsanitized tab-indented output landed every right border of the box elsewhere. Regression tests: `TestToolCallRowShowsNamedArgument`, `TestToolResultRendersBox`, `TestToolResultExitCodeSitsInTheFooter`, `TestToolResultRowWindow`, `TestToolResultBoxAlignsTabbedOutput`.

5. **Thinking output renders + `/settings` toggle** (2026-09-11, #20): the wire layer always parsed reasoning (unified `thinking_start/delta/end` events from all four adapters), but the transcript only ever showed a collapsed "Thought for Xs" header — the reasoning text itself was never rendered and there was no way to turn any of it off. Now: `showThinking` settings key (defaults ← global ← project ← `--config`, `*bool` so an explicit false survives the zero-skip merge; `xdev config list|get|set|reset` honors it) drives display — TUI renders the reasoning in the same rounded frame a finished result gets (`theme.AccentThinking` border, `gray_dim` body, state in the top border) showing a fixed `thinkBoxRows = 12`-row window that the mouse wheel scrolls once a left click has focused the box (`Block.ThinkOff`, rows counted up from the newest thought; the wheel is the transcript's until then) and Ctrl+O drops entirely for that box — no tier-based head+tail trim, so reasoning reads the same at every tier (full text stays in the session JSONL), print mode gates its stderr reasoning stream, and `/settings` (bare) lists the resolved config while `/settings showThinking [on|off|flip]` toggles for the session AND persists to the global layer; turning it off drops existing thinking blocks so the transcript matches a session started with it off. Same commit fixed multi-line system notices (`/help` `/model` `/settings` lists) which embedded `\n` collapsed into one unreadable row — now one row per line.
   - Replay parity (2026-09-11, 108c63e): the three transcript replay loops (`--continue` breadcrumb, `/resume`, `/branch`) previously rendered text only, so past reasoning silently vanished — a shared `replayTranscript` helper now walks `ThinkingBlock`s too, gated by the same showThinking toggle (regression-tested through a sim-screen App).
   - **Request side (2026-09-17, the other half of #20):** the key above only ever gated *rendering* — the
     request still asked every model to think, and `minimal` (config's documented off switch) reached the
     adapters as a 0-token budget, which Anthropic rejects (`max_tokens<=0`) and the OpenAI adapters read as
     effort "low". Now: the `thinking` settings key (`auto` (default) | `off` | the `EffortLevels` rungs, plus
     the omp `xhigh`/`max` names that clamp to `high`), folded under `--thinking` and over the model role's
     `:effort` in one helper — `thinkingLevel` → `applyThinkingFlag` — applied at every run mode (print, RPC,
     TUI, and **ACP, which had skipped the fold entirely**). `/thinking [off|auto|level]` and the `Shift+Tab`
     chord (`thinking-toggle`, off ⇄ auto, the Alt+T shape) flip it live: the level, not just the budget, is
     re-folded against the role effort on every `/model` switch, so "auto" re-binds instead of freezing. One
     shared `effortBudget` drops a zero budget to nil, which is what makes "off"/"minimal" mean no parameter
     instead of an invalid one. `xdev config set thinking <level>` validates the same vocabulary. Absent from
     `repoSafeSettingsKeys`: a repository may configure how xdev looks, not how many tokens it burns.
     Ceiling: the registry's task-tool child budget is stamped at startup, so a live flip reaches the parent
     turn and vibe workers, not already-built task children.
   - **One row until clicked (2026-09-30, `fix/think-box-one-row`):** every reasoning box painted a
     12-row window, so a turn with four thoughts was 50+ rows of frame before the answer, and the box grew
     the scrollback as it streamed. `thinkBoxLines` now reads the box's height off the focus: collapsed it is
     ONE row — the newest thought, the tail-anchored window at `thinkBoxCollapsed`, with no hidden-row notice,
     because a notice would cost the only row there is — and a click (`App.thinkFocus`, already set by the
     press path in `selection.go`) grows it to the `thinkBoxRows` window the wheel scrolls, notice and all. A
     second click on the box, or a click anywhere else, returns it to one row; the window's `ThinkOff` is kept,
     so a box re-opened is where it was left. `Ctrl+O` is the one height focus does not own. `thinkWindow` /
     `thinkMaxOff` take the height as a parameter so render and wheel cannot disagree about it.


6. **Long sessions: scroll lag, 100 % CPU, unbounded render memory** (P0, 2026-09-14): three independent causes, all on the UI thread, all measured before/after. A fourth cause of the same complaint — the lag when *typing* after a long scroll — was found and fixed on 2026-09-17 (last bullet).
   - **O(transcript) per frame.** `draw()` rebuilt the whole row list every frame (30 fps while a turn runs) and `totalLinesLocked()` re-walked every block on each scroll key → 7.8 ms/frame at 85 200 rows. Fixed structurally by the row index (§3.5): 0.50 ms/frame, scroll keys O(1)–O(blocks).
   - **Quadratic inline markdown.** `inlineRuns` consumed plain text one rune at a time via `rest = string([]rune(rest)[1:])`, re-decoding the remainder per character — one 6 000-rune streamed paragraph cost **98 ms to re-render** (and 1.36 s per frame once a message reached ~20 KB), which is the reported 100 % CPU during a long answer. It now jumps marker-to-marker and copies plain runs whole: **4.7 µs** for the same render.
   - **Render cache that never let go.** The per-state map cache kept *every* intermediate render of a streaming block: +1 200 stale entries and **274 MB** heap for one 1 200-delta message. One render per block, replaced in place: 4 000 deltas now cost 14 MB, and a live 2 500-line answer runs at **30 MB RSS / 0.2 % CPU** where the previous build sat at **357 MB / 12 % and climbing** (goal: hard <100 MB RSS).
   - **A frame per input event.** The UI loop painted after *every* event, so a wheel flick queued a whole burst of frames ahead of the keystroke typed behind it: 200 notches cost **296 `Show()` calls** (a synchronous tty write each), and the character waited for that backlog to drain — which is why the lag scaled with how much you had scrolled, and why nothing showed up in the per-frame cost: measured flat at ~0.49 ms per keystroke whether the viewport sat at the top or the tail (14 536 rows), because the row index was already doing its job. `Run()` now drains the input already queued in `keyq` and paints once (`drainKeys()`, bounded at `keyBurst = 256` so a held key still reaches the screen); every event is still applied, in order, so no chord or paste marker is dropped. Regression gate: `TestScrollBurstCostsOneFrameNotOnePerEvent` drives the real `Run()` loop with each flush charged 1 ms and asserts **293–312 frames pre-fix, ≤24 after** — plus that the burst actually scrolled, so an ignored wheel event cannot pass cheaply. An interactive tmux/pty A/B was not usable as evidence: `send-keys` paces the burst itself (~160 ms for 600 notches), so the harness measured tmux rather than xdev.
   - Regression gates: `TestRowIndexAgreesWithFullRebuildAtEveryOffset` (the fast path paints exactly what the full rebuild painted, at every offset), `TestSettledBlocksSurviveTailMutations`, `TestAgedToolOutputCollapsesFirst`, `TestExpandedResultEscapesTrim`, `TestFrameCostFollowsViewportNotSession`, `TestStreamingDeltaCostStaysFlat`.

**Milestone deltas from the second-wave research (2026-09-09, `docs/research/*-internals.md`):** M1 adds the classified-error taxonomy (Flag bitmask + retriable kinds + overflow regexes) and never-executed partial-tool-call stubs; M2 adds todo-state replay (backward scan preferring `user_todo_edit` entries) and the title-resolution cascade; M3 adds the `todo` tool (9 ops, 5 statuses incl. `blocked`+`reason`), the 7-pass tool-arg repair pipeline, file-freshness rejection, backgrounded bash, and out-of-band steering markers; M5 adds the compaction method ladder `[remote, handoff, shake, soft]` with 80% recovery band + dead-end parking, `TurnRecovery` partial-stream rewrite, and fork-on-compression lineage as the audited alternative; M6 adds the `yield` wrapper contract (retry budgets, 12k inline / 4k preview async-result split), `todoPhases` in `get_state`, and the deferred tool-catalog bridge; M9 adds the role-selector grammar `provider/id:effort@upstream` with alias loop guard, the durable credential-block table + quota rotation, and failover chains; M10 adds `/rename` + ai-title generation, context-file injection scanning, and the 3-tier stable→context→volatile prompt-cache assembly; M11 adds the parent-owned `todo` policy (stripped from subagent tool sets) and mailbox-style cross-session messaging (NICE).

## 5. Key decisions

- **Go single static binary, CGO-free** — cross-compiles everywhere, ~22 MB dev / ~29 MB installed with the SQLite-backed mnemopi backend linked in (measured 2026-09-12; build without it to reclaim ~7 MB), instant startup, no runtime install; it is the only way to meet the hard <100 MB RSS budget a JIT-ed JS runtime cannot (research Part III, IV.0.2).
- **Port the data model, not the code** — pi/omp's session tree, stream contract, context reconstruction, OutputSink, and frame-plan TUI are mature, crash-tested designs; porting their semantics (same entry names) buys interop with existing session tooling without carrying JS-era implementation weight (research IV.0.1).
- **Extensions as subprocesses with per-event timeout + SIGKILL** — omp's in-process TS extensions run with full process trust and a stray throw kills the session; a process boundary makes hung extensions killable and extensions language-agnostic, and omp's RPC/host-tool model already proves cross-language embedding works (research II.5, IV.5, IV.8).
- **Bounded queues with delta coalescing** — fixes omp's documented unbounded-queue wart without changing semantics: on overflow of the 1,024-event queue, coalesce consecutive `*_delta` events of the same block; rendering coalesces visually and persistence only needs final messages (research II.3, IV.4).
- **tcell over bubbletea** — bubbletea's Elm event model adds allocation churn; tcell is raw, fast, low-alloc, and omp's frame-plan model ports cleanly on top (research IV.6).
- **Pure-Go SQLite (`ncruces/go-sqlite3` or `modernc.org/sqlite`)** — keeps the CGO-free guarantee while providing FTS5 for prompt history; replay fidelity stays in JSONL, search UX in SQLite, never mixed (research Part V, II.2).
- **Shell out to `rg`/`fd`/`ast-grep`; no tree-sitter embedding** — embedding tree-sitter means CGO + memory; Go stdlib + subprocesses cover ~90% of the Rust natives; the one natives concept worth re-implementing in-process is the FS-scan shared cache (research IV.7, IV.10).
- **Product name xdev; research codename `adze` superseded** — the repo name (`FreePeak/xdev`) is authoritative: binary `xdev`, module `github.com/FreePeak/xdev`, data dir `~/.xdev/agent/`; session format remains compatible with omp's.
- **Prompt reality check (2026-09-09 primary sources)** — pi's real base prompt measures ~460–510 tokens over 8 built-ins; omp's full 29-tool slate costs ~12–15k tokens (hashline edit mode alone 5.3 KB). xdev ships the pi-minimal core plus an ESSENTIAL gate: the top-level tool schema carries only essential tools; discoverable tools are demoted behind `xd://` devices and search (parity-omp-internals.md §4).
- **Todo state rides the transcript** — todo phases persist as tool-result `details.phases` plus `user_todo_edit` custom entries (omp v18), replayed by backward scan on branch switch/rewind; NOT side files (Claude Code `tasks/`) and NOT a SQLite table (opencode) — the replayable transcript stays the single source of truth (omp-todos-internals.md §6).
- **Injected content is hostile until scanned** — AGENTS.md-class files and web/browser/MCP tool output pass prompt-injection threat scanning and `<untrusted_tool_result>` wrapping (hermes pattern) before entering context (hermes-internals.md).
- **A child inherits the conventions its parent runs under** — `LoadContextFiles` had exactly one caller (`cmd/xdev/print.go` `promptFnWithMemory`), so the always-loaded hierarchy reached the PARENT prompt only: a spawn ran on the bare `SubagentSystemPromptBase` const plus its definition prompt, carrying no worktree rule, no read-only rule, no PR-URL rule. Confirmed live before the fix (a scout asked to quote the rules globbed the filesystem for `AGENTS.md` and found nothing; its child request measured 511 B with no conventions) and pinned by `TestSubagentChildGetsContextFiles`. `SpawnChild` now appends the hierarchy for the child's own cwd (`childSystem`) — appended, never substituted, so a named agent definition cannot silently drop policy. Memory/recall stays out: recall is keyed to a session's own history and a fresh child session has none.
- **A standing convention lives in a context file, never only in recall** — xdev puts text into the prompt through two channels that look interchangeable when you write a rule down, and are not. `LoadContextFiles` (`prompt.go:281`) injects `~/.xdev/agent/AGENTS.md` and then the `AGENTS.md`/`CLAUDE.md` chain root→cwd under a **32 KB** cap, every turn, framed as authoritative; `rules.Discover` (`rules.go:133`) does the same for native `RULES.md` (`AlwaysApply`, native priority) and the `.cursor`/`.agent`/plugin sources. Auto-recall instead ranks notes against **the last user turn only** (`RecallContextTurns = 1`), caches 60 s, and is hard-capped at **1024 tokens = 4096 chars** with *"Heuristic context recalled from earlier sessions — not authoritative"* prepended (`hindsight.go:588-590`). Measured 2026-09-17 against the live bank: one query returned 8 notes / 8756 chars, so `capRecall` kept 2 notes and part of a third and dropped the rest — a worktree convention that lived only in the bank sat at char 4357 of a 4096-char window. Where it did survive, the block's own framing did the rest: a session read the rule, called it *"a recalled instruction from earlier sessions … heavy for a one-line helper"*, and did not follow it. So: cross-repo habits → `~/.xdev/agent/AGENTS.md`, repo invariants → `<repo>/AGENTS.md`, must-apply-even-when-shadowed rules → `<repo>/RULES.md`; recall carries facts that inform a plan and may be missed, not policy ([decision](decisions/standing-conventions-location.md)).


### 5.5 ZCode cross-check (2026-09-22)

ZCode is a TypeScript/Node coding workbench (Electron desktop + web + a terminal agent) read at
commit `872ad96`; the agent half is `apps/zcode-cli/` (15 packages, a nested pnpm workspace) and the
full evidence is in [research/zcode-internals.md](research/zcode-internals.md). It is the closest peer
studied so far in *scope* (session core, plan mode, prompts, tools, providers, compaction, subagent
profiles, and an explicit long-lived-session lifecycle) and the most different in *stack*
(SQLite-backed event ledger, Vercel AI SDK, a session residency pool, a hard 400-line-per-file
ceiling). Maturity caveat carried into the verdict: the tree ships **zero tests** and its only
verification entry points are `pnpm typecheck`/`pnpm lint`, so its designs are read as
stated-and-wired, not as proven.

**Adopt (changes a stated design assumption, not just a feature):**

- **[#420](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/420) A tool's capabilities are a declaration, not a name lookup.** `ToolMetadata` carries
  `readOnly`/`destructive`/`concurrentSafe`/`sideEffectScope`/`needsApproval`/`alwaysAsk`/
  `stopTurnOnSuccess` beside the schemas, and four consumers read the same declaration. xdev's three
  parallel name-keyed tables (plan mode's `planReadOnlyTools`, `internal/tool/policy.go`'s tiers, the
  scheduler's `READ_ONLY_TOOLS`) are what a manifest replaces — and plan mode's fail-open list is the
  first casualty it fixes (T3 #27/#28). §3.6, §3.4.
- **[#422](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/422) Compaction needs a breaker.** `MAX_CONSECUTIVE_RAPID_REFILLS = 3` /
  `RAPID_REFILL_TOOL_TURN_THRESHOLD = 3`: compaction that does not buy space is a failure mode, and
  xdev counts nothing today. §3.2.
- **[#421](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/421) Read-only tools can run during the stream.** Gated on the manifest, with a fallback to
  end-of-stream execution on failure and preserved results on a mid-stream model failure. §3.4.
- **[#424](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/424) A stream read must be abortable while the provider is wedged**, alongside xdev's source-side
  liveness judgement (both properties, one watchdog). §3.3, §3.9.
- **[#423](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/423) The prompt states its own contract.** Three short paragraphs — mid-turn text may be invisible;
  compaction is coming so do not wrap up early; how permissions/hooks/system turns behave — that
  xdev has the machinery for and never mentions. §3.4.

**Verify / design further:**

- **[#425](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/425) Sectioned prompt with declared cache hints** (`{injectionTarget, cacheHint}` → one system message
  per group, each cache-marked) — the structural form of the manual cache discipline in
  [research/omp-prompt-cache-2026-09-15.md](research/omp-prompt-cache-2026-09-15.md). §3.4.
- **[#430](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/430) Microcompact as a pre-request tier** with a provider-visible boundary event; xdev's `shake`/`soft`
  rungs only run at a boundary that is already paying for a summary. §3.2.
- **[#426](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/426) Permission: two racing responders** (hook chain and interactive broker start concurrently, either
  wins, a failed hook forfeits rather than denies, a modified input re-checks its permission) plus
  `allowAlways: false | "session"` and `alwaysAsk` that survives yolo and plan-mode pass-through. §3.6.
- **[#428](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/428) Residency vocabulary** — `SessionResidencyFacts` + a counter every detached promise registers in
  the tick that starts it — not as a pool port (xdev is one session per process) but as the answer to
  "what is this session still doing", which `--bg`/attach (#131) and a correct resume of a busy
  session both need. §3.2, §3.9.
- **[#429](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/429) Subagent profile scoping**: `injectAgentsMd: false` for a read-only explore child, per-profile
  `permissionMode`, the plan tools force-removed from every child, and the child note "do NOT write
  report/summary .md files — return findings directly". §3.4.
- **[#427](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/427) Plan mode by declared capability** — deny by default in plan mode, two tools with a free enter and a gated exit, the advertised toolset filtered while planning, and a reviewer-less proposal ending the run instead of auto-accepting (closes parity T3 #27/#28). §2.

**Reject (with the reason recorded):**

- **SQLite as the session store.** ~2.7 klines of migration/codec machinery for a benefit xdev gets from `BuildContext`, against two stated goals (JSONL interop; the RSS budget).
- **The Vercel AI SDK.** PRD §1 non-goal stands; the portable half is the *seams* (`bindModel` freezes
  baseURL/protocol/options at model-creation time so a config hot-reload cannot silently re-point a
  running session — the same class of bug as `fix/model-live-switch`, 2026-09-20).
- **A hard 400-line file ceiling.** It is why the peer's turn loop spread across 75 files in
  `runtime/methods/`; xdev's soft version is right for files a human reads on a terminal.
- **`SessionModePort` as an injected port** for the collaboration mode — xdev's `PlanMode` struct is
  the right shape for one front end per process mode.
### 5.1 Claude Code cross-check (adopt / verify / reject)

Claude Code v2.1.263 was studied independently of pi/omp — bundle forensics (verbatim system prompts + tool schemas from the installed binary), the official docs, and design analysis. Full evidence in §6. Verdicts that change or confirm the plan:

**Adopt:**
- **Microcompact before compaction** (M5) — clear old tool outputs first, summarize only as the second phase; plus a context-high-water warning in the status line and a `/context` per-category budget display.
- **Hooks exit-code contract** (M11) — CC's `PreToolUse` blocking semantics (exit 2 = block with stderr reason, JSON `updatedInput` mutation, two-stage matcher + `if` permission-syntax pre-filter) is the proven guardrail model; keep execution boring (`sh -c`, stdin JSON).
- **Subagent yield-only isolation as a contract test** (M11) — a subagent transcript NEVER streams into parent context; only the final yield does. Hub design already implies this; pin it with a test.
- **Plan mode + ExitPlanMode approval gate** (M14) — read-only mode, plan presentation, explicit approve flow. omp has no equivalent; cheap given the approval tiers.
- **AskUserQuestion** (M14) — structured mid-task clarification tool (options, multi-select, timeout→recommended) instead of prose questions; xdev's `ask` dialog is the TUI surface. A batch of questions is ONE tabbed card (not N interruptions), every question carries a free-text row, and a "Chat about this" escape closes the card unanswered so the human can answer in prose.
- **Checkpoint/rewind** (M13, promoted from SKIP-adjacent) — pre-prompt file snapshots + triple restore (code / conversation / both); CC's most-loved feature. Design JSONL turn-boundary markers from M0 so this stays cheap.
- **Think-budget keyword ladder** (M5) — `think` < `think hard` < `ultrathink` → provider reasoning budgets; one constant table.
- **CLAUDE.md as alias** (M10) — AGENTS.md is the standard, but loading `CLAUDE.md` as an alias keeps ecosystem compatibility; `@path` imports, hierarchy, lazy subdirectory loads.
- **Edit-tool semantics refinement** (M3) — CC's exact-string Edit with uniqueness + read-before-edit staleness gates complements the hashline UX; also adopt the benign-exit-1 command list and output-to-file overflow pattern for `bash`.
- **Background bash registry** (M3/M13) — `run_in_background`, `/tasks` listing, timeout→background move with explicit notice, output-file-as-result.

**Verify:**
- RESOLVED by primary-source research (2026-09-09): omp v18 ships a full `todo` tool (9 ops, 5 statuses, transcript-persisted) — the "file-based TODO.md" question is settled. Port omp's todo engine as CORE in M3 (see omp-todos-internals.md); TODO.md survives only as export/import surface in M10.
- Computed context-budget line in the system prompt (CC hardcodes ~90% of window) — inject the real usable-token number instead.
- CC's `auto` permission mode (classifier model reviews every action) — pluggable, off-by-default, only if sandbox integrations demand it.

**Reject (with reasons):**
- Full in-process permission-mode UI — xdev stays YOLO-inside-external-sandbox (pi/omp stance); thin mode concept only.
- LSP/AST/embedding-memory as built-ins — CC itself proves the "thin layer over the model" philosophy (grep/glob/file tools only); extended infrastructure stays opt-in (M13/MCP).
- Multi-agent fan-out without caps — CC's own research post cites ~15× token cost for orchestrator-worker research; keep hub depth/width caps (M11) and compaction budgets.

Net: Claude Code **confirms xdev's pi/omp-shaped minimalism** (it lacks LSP/AST/memory-DB too) while contributing five features omp lacks entirely: microcompact, ExitPlanMode gate, AskUserQuestion, checkpoints/rewind, and the hooks blocking contract.

### 5.2 fx cross-check (adopt / verify / reject)

fx ([`vercel-labs/fx`](https://github.com/vercel-labs/fx), Zig, `ced6180` = v0.0.9; 671,361 lines across 587 files) was studied from a full clone on 2026-09-13 across nine domains — budgets, session core, providers/auth, tools, permissions/execution, TUI, extensibility, embedding, engineering discipline. It is xdev's closest live peer (tiny native single binary, no JS runtime, minimal tool slate, inspectable sessions) and the only reference agent that publishes **machine-checked** budgets. Evidence with `path:line` refs: [docs/research/fx-internals.md](research/fx-internals.md).

**Adopt:**
- **A repository is not a configuration authority** (M9, **#114**, P0). fx's project `.fx.json` accepts exactly three repo-safe keys (`max_agent_steps`, `max_tool_result_bytes`, `context`) and ignores profile-owned ones; the docs give the reason — *"This prevents a repository from silently changing your model preferences"*. xdev layers `<repo>/.xdev/config.yml` over the user's config with the **same schema**, and a throwaway `-overlay` probe at `7c19a54` proved a temp repo could resolve `approvalMode: yolo`, get its declared `bash.interceptor` **executed** with the inherited environment, and redirect a provider `baseUrl`. This corrects §1 goal 4, which conflated user-YOLO with repo-YOLO.
  - **Shipped 2026-09-14 (23173dd), stricter than fx's three keys and stricter than a denylist.** The allowlist is keyed per layer and lives in one file, `internal/config/reposafe.go`: `.xdev/config.yml` keeps the interface (`theme`, `colorBlindMode`, `statusLine`, `showThinking`), the caps (`memoryLimit`, `maxTurns`, `compaction`, `branchSummary`, `experimentalContextManagement`, `models`) and `ask`/`tts`; `.xdev/models.yml` keeps what-a-model-is metadata and the transport dialect and loses every destination and credential, including `${VAR}` expansion, which now runs on the profile alone. A key added to the schema is repository-unreachable until someone lists it deliberately. On a provider both layers name, the profile wins outright. `.xdev/secrets.yml` may add entries and no longer shadow a profile one — redaction matches on the value, so a clone that replaced `GITHUB_TOKEN` with a dummy would have unmasked a secret it never had. Nothing is dropped quietly: one startup notice per repository file names every refused key and the profile path that may set it. Proven end to end with the real binary, and the tests sit at the seam the loop calls (`LoadSettings` → `Policy` → `ReviewBash`), each checked to fail with the guard removed — the class of defect this repo has documented three times.
- **Measure the request; name the budgets; report every clip** (M5, **#116**). fx triggers compaction from the **serialized request body** and calibrates against the provider's returned `usage.input_tokens` (`orchestrator.zig:6831-6908, 366-384`), with exact integer ratios as the contract (high-water `usable×4/5`, target `/10`, tail `/20`, soft ceiling `/4`, source `/8`, one anti-recompaction guard), retention cut only at complete execution steps ("Payloads are measured, never shortened"), **11 named byte budgets** over injected context (`context_limits.zig:20-29`) settable to `"off"` behind a 64 MiB emergency ceiling and overridable by repeatable `--context-limit name=bytes`, and the rule that *"truncated or omitted context is reported to the runtime rather than silently treated as complete"*. xdev estimates at 4 bytes/token against anonymous constants.
- **Retain what you truncate, and give the model a door back** (M3, **#115**): 16 KiB spill threshold, 4 KiB preview, content-addressed handle, ≤8 MiB retained, one tool for byte ranges *and* literal search over the retained bytes. xdev discards the middle.
- **`auto` = one bounded second-model review, as an opt-in tier** (M9, **#117**): a fixed small reviewer per provider (not user-selectable), `maxOutputTokens 2048`, ≤16 KiB packet, 64 holds + 64 unavailable attempts per turn; `caution` **only** for concrete injection or malice; a clear verdict authorizes the exact unchanged action; caution/incomplete/unavailable holds that action and returns guidance instead of prompting or aborting; headless with no prompter denies, traced. This answers §5.1's open "CC's `auto` mode — pluggable, off-by-default" line with a shipped design. YOLO stays xdev's default.
- **Containment without a sandbox** (part of #114/#117): auto-run only a fixed read-only command allowlist, executed in a **3-variable scrubbed environment** (`PATH=/usr/bin:/bin`, no `HOME`, git env neutralized) — provably no ambient authority.
- **Write-side durability set** (M2, **#122**): one buffer per turn batch, `file.length() == committed_bytes` asserted before writing (foreign-writer detection), positional write, `sync()`, `setLength` rollback on failure, unfinished trailing turn discarded at open, 0700/0600 with a lock deadline; plus a recover/migrate verb pair and a doctor that prints the exact repair command (**#121**).
- **Credential storage is verified at use time** (M9, **#123**): macOS Keychain by shell-out to `/usr/bin/security` (CGO-free, same philosophy as our `rg`/`fd`/`git` shell-outs), and where a file is the store, `mode == 0600 && nlink == 1` is re-checked before trust with the parent forced 0700 and a hard failure otherwise; an explicitly named credential source is an exact authority whose absence **never** falls through to another billing boundary.
  - **Shipped 2026-09-14 (4ff976e, f8ddc2c), with the Keychain write lane rejected by measurement.** What xdev does now: `credentials.json` is verified **on every read** (`mode == 0600 && nlink == 1`, refusal names the repair), the data dir is tightened to 0700 on write, and an explicitly named credential is an exact authority — `auth: oauth` with no OAuth login, or a `keychain:` reference that cannot be satisfied, errors instead of falling through to a key or an environment variable (the silent account swap). The Keychain is a **source**, reachable as `providers.<p>.apiKey: keychain:<service>[/<account>]`, and never a sink: `/usr/bin/security` takes a secret either as an argument (world-readable in `ps`) or through a prompt that [measured as silently truncating at 128 bytes](decisions/keychain-credential-source.md) with exit 0 — fine for an API key, corrupting for a 1–2 KB JWT. Reads carry arbitrary length (verified byte-exact at 8 KiB, and end to end through `ResolveCredential` at 2 KB against the real tool). Token zeroization is not implemented and is not available: Go strings are immutable, so the practical control is that secrets are never retained beyond the request and never logged whole (`Redact`, tested).
- **Budgets a machine enforces** (M8, **#118/#119**): 2 ms mean startup per command on one authoritative runner with a default new commands inherit, hyperfine at 100 runs/10 warmups against a **seeded worst-case store** (8 sessions × 256 MiB sparse logs) and a baseline reported but never subtracted; frames at 8/12/17 ms p50/p90/p95 with thread/fd/RSS deltas as the leak check; ratio-based gates for shared runners; a base-vs-head size **delta** warning (52,429 B) in PR CI with the hard ceiling reserved for release (7.800 MiB + 0.250 MiB headroom); a ship gate that jq-asserts the Actions **job-name set** so a skipped matrix row fails; and `check-public-surface.sh` — a tracked-tree leak audit for `/Users/<name>/` paths, token-shaped strings and fixture hygiene — inside PR CI. xdev already has the memory proof, the fuzzers and govulncheck; these three close the time/size/integrity blind spots.
- **A tape tier between unit tests and tmux** (M4, **#120**): `FX_DEBUG_RECORD=1` → `.fxtape`; `fx replay <tape> --golden out.txt` needs **no PTY**, so a user's render bug becomes a checked-in golden diff (SHA-256 cell-grid hashes; screenshots explicitly *not* the oracle), under the rule "pick the lowest layer that can observe it".
- **Target-scoped instructions** (M10, evidence for **#108**): fx resolves the `AGENTS.md` chain **per tool-call target** at call time (narrowest scope wins, user request outranks project text), delivered as entity-escaped wrappers with per-file and total byte budgets, and refreshed before continued turns — exactly the consumer `Rulebook.ForPath` never got, and the escape half is the cheap companion to #81's scanner.
- **Ready-gate prose** (docs, folded into #119): *"Do not document intended behavior as if it already exists"*, and never say ready/done/complete until you personally ran the built binary and drove one real interaction, because tests "do not always construct the full runtime, attach a TTY, or spawn background threads" (`AGENTS.md:5-17`). That is this repo's own recurring defect class, named as a rule.
- **Detached commands stay the model's to finish** (M3, **#127**): fx's `shell` is one tool with `run`/`interact`/`stop`; `yield_time_ms:0` returns an execution handle that stays addressable across turns (64 live + 32 tombstones, persisted with the session), `interact` re-observes or sends exact bytes, `stop` terminates the group. xdev starts such jobs (`run_in_background`, #16) and hands back a job id + output path, but exposes no control: `Stop`/`WriteInput`/`Logs` exist only on the hub's *named-service* table (`internal/agent/proc.go:297,380,254`), and `cmd/xdev/pscmd.go:160-166` records that neither registry is persisted. Clone by unifying the registries, not by adding a second control surface.
  - **Shipped 2026-09-14 (0fed6f3), one control surface instead of two.** The verbs went onto `bash` itself (`action=status|logs|stop|write`, `job=<id>`) because the issue's own diagnosis was two registries with one inert — a second control plane would have recreated the split. Each job now carries the two things that make it addressable: its process-group id and a stdin pipe kept open, so a detached REPL, watcher or `y/N` prompt is a conversation instead of a dead end. `stop` waits for the reaper so the state it reports is final (the #126 guarantee, applied to a job); an unknown id is refused at the seam by every verb; a timeout handoff honestly reports `stdin: not writable`; and eviction prefers finished jobs, warning in the start response when a live handle must go. Verified live by driving the built binary as the model across four turns (`write hello-world\n` → `logs` → `ECHO:hello-world` → `stop` → `signal 15`). The persisted-handle half (fx's 64+32 records travelling with the session) is **#243**, needing a session entry type, a probe-and-re-register on resume, and host validation.

**Verify:**
- **Alt-screen chat vs inline bands** (#78). fx never owns the screen in chat: row bands with *typed* owner violations (`PreservedShellMutation`, `WriteOutsideBand`), scrolled-off lines pushed into the terminal's **real scrollback**, `?1049h` only for overlays, and a diff emitter that feeds accepted bytes back through its own VT emulator to detect and repair a partially-applied scroll.
