The bar was a `│` hairline track in `gray_dim` with a `█` thumb in `gray`, positioned in whole rows, and it read as a second border on the transcript rather than a control. opencode's `ScrollBar` (opentui 0.5.12, `packages/tui/src/routes/session/index.tsx`: a `scrollbox` whose `verticalScrollbarOptions.trackOptions` is a track *background* plus a foreground, and `@opentui/core`'s `SliderRenderable`, which works in two virtual cells per terminal row) does three different things, and all three are now xdev's: (1) **the track is a filled groove, not a glyph** — the reserved last column is `bg_highlight` with no character, so a bar reads as a rail inset into the page; (2) **geometry is in half rows** — `scrollModel.Scrollbar` now returns a span on a `2·vp`-tall track with the thumb `⌊2·vp·vp/total⌋` half rows, and `sbGlyph` paints `█` / `▀` / `▄` per row (the same three states opentui's `renderVertical` draws), so the thumb can land between two rows instead of snapping a whole row at a time — on a 20-row viewport that doubles the number of distinct positions a sweep of the scrollback can show; (3) the thumb is `gray_bright` on the groove, so it is the only bright thing in the column. The mouse follows the paint: `selBarPos`/`selBarEnd` publish the half-row span, `selBarAt` returns a grip in half rows (a press is a whole row, so it lands on that row's upper half) and `selThumbTo` inverts the same half-row placement, so a one-row pull moves the thumb a row and a track click still lands the thumb's top under the finger. Colours come from the slots xdev already has (`bg_highlight`, `gray_bright`), so no theme-contract change and a custom theme keeps controlling the bar through the documented fallback chain. Not ported: opencode's default-hidden `session.scrollbar` setting, its 1-column content gutter and its `session.toggle.scrollbar` keybind — the bar stays always-on whenever the transcript overflows. Verified: `go test ./internal/tui` green, and driven live in a 100×30 pty (`!seq 1 400`, PageUp, and an SGR-1006 drag of the thumb) with the binary rebuilt from the branch.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`fix/hud-token-split` — user-reported "the output token number in the status bar is too much compared with the input token, I think there is something wrong"). The `↑ ↓` counters claimed numbers the provider never billed, in two independent ways. **↑ showed the fresh-input residue**: every provider normalizes onto `input + output + cacheRead = totalTokens` (`internal/ai/openai_completions.go:518` subtracts the cache from `prompt_tokens` on purpose, and `agent.contextTokens` prices compaction against that split), so a real onegw turn billing a 65,054-token prompt of which 64,575 was a cache read painted `↑479` — 99.3 % of the prompt invisible, and 62.1M cache-read tokens against a 767k counter over 683 turns of one session. **↓ counted thinking as output**: `completion_tokens` is inclusive of `completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` on every wire xdev speaks, so that same turn's `↓1770` was 506 tokens of visible text plus 1264 tokens of thinking that never reaches the transcript; reasoning is 68–91 % of `↓` across recent sessions. The `ctx` segment needed no change — it already read `TotalTokens`, the one of the three that was right. This is the same class [#453](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/453) fixed for the `⚡` rate segment (Gemini's `candidatesTokenCount` excluding `thoughtsTokenCount`, which also normalized the provider side); the two counters beside that rate were left. `Status` gains `TokensCache` + `TokensThink`, `AddUsage` takes both (`in, out, cache, thinking, total`), and the segment renders the split it holds — `↑479 ⇢64.6k │ ↓1.8k ˟1.3k`. Both new glyphs hide when the provider reports neither, so a provider with no cache and no reasoning keeps the plain two-glyph row; the `total <= 0` fallback counts the cache too; `Reset` clears both with the rest of the per-session counters. `⇢` was already the cache glyph in the trajectory line (`cmd/xdev/tui.go:3186`), so the row and the per-turn ledger now read alike. Verified off the **painted row** (the #453 post-mortem was explicit that App-side tests pass with the hook still unwired): `TestHUDContextCountsCachedInput` now drives the wire's own numbers, `TestTokenSplitMatchesTheWire` pins the real onegw turn, `TestTokenSplitHidesWhatTheProviderDoesNotReport` keeps the plain row, `TestResetClearsSessionCounters` puts the new fields on the session boundary.
*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (fix/ask-wait-for-human — an unattended `ask` was answered for the user). The old policy was a 60s timer: when nobody could answer (print/rpc one-shot, a TUI card the human skipped or had no room for), the `ask` tool took the model's OWN `recommended` option and moved on. A question the user never saw silently became a decision — the exact failure the requirement-first rule exists to prevent, and one that a busy human cannot notice. The wait is now the point: **the default is no timer at all**.
*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`fix/tcell-input-loss` — a dead tty left the TUI alive and deaf: "the whole TUI froze, I had to kill it", with nothing in the process able to recover). Read while diagnosing the stopped process in session `6917d52f` (a `T`-state run that #460's signal guard did not cover); the read-error path is a *separate* mechanism and is closed on its own evidence. tcell's `inputLoop` posts one `EventError` and then **returns** — that tty is never read again — and `App.Run` could not see it: the poll goroutine exits only on a nil event, tcell yields nil solely from `StopQ`, and `handleKey` drops anything that is not a key/resize/mouse. So the UI kept painting and heartbeating (which is why the stall watchdog stayed quiet and wrote no dump) with a tty in raw mode and the alt screen up, and no key, resize or quit chord did anything. One branch where the event is polled logs the error and ends the loop, returning through `runTUI` so `defer scr.Fini()` restores the terminal. `internal/tui/input_error_test.go` guards both directions: the wedge test fails against the absent branch, and a resize plus two keystrokes must still reach the composer if the check ever widens. Not claimed as the cause of either wedged session — no trace ties them to a tty read error.
*Last updated: 2026-09-29 (`fix/tty-job-control-signals` — **the stopped TUI, reproduced and fixed**). This is the mechanism behind session `6917d52f`: the process sat in state `T` mid-turn with ECHO/ICANON/ISIG all cleared, and #460's guard never saw it. **Job control is not the SUSP character.** ISIG off means the kernel does not *generate* ^Z, ^C and ^\ from the tty line discipline — but a process group that is not the tty's foreground group still gets **SIGTTOU** the moment it calls `tcsetattr`, and **SIGTTIN** on a background read. tcell calls `tcsetattr` inside `Init()`, so `xdev &` (or any stray `&`) is stopped *during Init, before `watchTerminalRoutes` is armed* — the guard was not wrong, it was late. The default disposition is to STOP, and a stop runs no defer, so `scr.Fini()` never fires and the terminal stays raw with the alt screen up. Reproduced on macOS 25.6.0 with a private tmux server: stock `origin/main` goes `suspended (tty output)` at launch and `suspended (tty input)` when a pane hands the tty away mid-run; the fixed binary stays `R`. The fix is two more signals in the existing guard — `SIGTTOU` and `SIGTTIN` join SIGTSTP/SIGHUP/SIGTERM, restored-then-re-raised exactly as those are. `TestGuardRestoresThenDiesOnSignal` gains `tty-output` and `tty-input` cases, both passing. Ceiling: this restores the terminal and then dies on the signal, exactly as #460 does; it deliberately does NOT try to keep a backgrounded TUI alive, because a TUI that loses the terminal cannot be driven.

- `ask.autoAnswer` (new, **default false**) is the opt-in for the old behavior. `ask.timeout` now only bounds that path; it no longer implies it, so raising the wait no longer quietly buys auto-answering.
- `headlessAskSink` gained one `wait` method shared by `Ask`/`AskBatch` (`internal/tool/ask.go`): auto-answer off blocks on `ctx.Done()` and nothing else, on and it times out and returns the recommendation. `NewAskTool`/`NewHeadlessAskSink` take the flag; `print.go` and `tui.go` pass `settings.AskAutoAnswerOn()`.
- The TUI card is the answer path, so it owns the wait: `runTUI` hands the seam a **zero** timeout when auto-answer is off (`AskCardBatch` already skips its timer on `timeout <= 0`), and `askCardSink` gained `autoAnswer` so a skipped card no longer auto-selects the recommendation — skipping is "not this one", not permission to decide. The two "window too narrow / no room above the composer" notices now say *answer in chat* instead of *using the recommended path*, which is what they mean under the default.
- `AskCard` is otherwise unchanged, and the `AskChatNote` escape hatch still ends the turn. `internal/config`: `ask.autoAnswer` layers (plain-bool, only `true` contributes — same rule as `advisor`) and shows in `config list`/`config get`.

Verified: `go build ./...` clean, `gofmt` clean, `go test ./internal/... ./cmd/...` — the only failures (`TestConnectPickerItems`, `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills`) reproduce on a clean `origin/main` checkout and are unrelated (a credential-picker ordering and an ambient `~/.agent/skills` block). `tool.DefaultAskTimeout` is unchanged at 60s: it is the auto-answer wait now, not the wait.

*Last updated: 2026-09-16 (user: "increase the timeout waiting for answer from 30s to 60s"): the ask tool's headless wait when `ask.timeout` is unset is now **60s** (`tool.DefaultAskTimeout`, `internal/tool/ask.go`). The constant is the only thing that moved — the TUI card, the batch card and the recommend-fallback paths all read it through `AskTool.timeout()`/`headlessAskSink`, so a question nobody answers now waits a full minute before the recommended option proceeds. The failure mode the 30s value was chosen against — a one-shot run stalling CI — is bounded by the same constant, and the schema `_note` the model reads names the new default.

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (user-reported retry failing on `upstream_error` 400 `ask: tool parameter root must be an object type (root schema is an anyOf/oneOf union with a non-object branch)`, xai/grok-4.6 through onegw): the failure was never retriable — the request itself was invalid, so `ai.Classify` correctly says `ClassBadRequest` and the ladder fails fast (a retry would replay the same schema forever). Two gaps fed it. (1) The `ask` tool's parameters root carried `anyOf:[{required:[…]},{required:[…]}]` with **untyped** branches; typing them `object` is the same constraint and is what the live validator accepts. (2) The chat/completions wire never ran the schema dispatcher at all — `openai_responses.go` called `NormalizeToolsForAPI`, `openai_completions.go` sent `t.Parameters` verbatim — so no client-side sanitizer could fire on the transport that actually 400'd. `buildRequest` now normalizes like Responses does, and `SanitizeSchemaForOpenAIResponses` gained `retypeRootObjectUnions`: a **root** `anyOf`/`oneOf` whose branches are all object-compatible (no `type`, or `type:"object"`) gains `type:"object"`; the union is never dropped, mixed-type branches (object beside `string`) and nested unions are left verbatim. Pinned by `TestSanitizeSchemaForOpenAIResponsesRetypesRootUnions` (retype, nested untouched, mixed untouched), `TestOpenAICompletionsRetypesAskRootUnion` (the wire body really carries typed branches, with the required-alternatives intact), and `TestAskParametersRootUnionIsObjectTyped` (the tool's own schema).

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (the gateway is a connectable row, and a fresh install defaults to it): `xdev-server` (`${XDEV_SERVER_URL}/v1`, `$XDEV_SERVER_KEY`, openai-completions) is now a catalog row and the `defaultModel` `xdev setup` prints. The catalog is **generated** — `scripts/gen_connect.go` owns `connect_catalog.go`, so a hand-added row dies at the next regen; the curated row therefore lives in `internal/config/connect_extra.go` and the five catalog reads (`ConnectNames`, `HasConnect`, `ConnectOptions`, `ConnectProviderConfig`, `ConnectModelRefs`) go through `lookupConnect`, which prefers it. `connectFirst` ranks it with anthropic/openai/google so the empty-state hint shows it rather than burying it behind ~200 alphabetical rows. `TestExtraConnectCatalog` fails if a regen drops the row or the row stops passing `RegisterProvider`; `TestSetupCreatesDataDirAndStarterConfig` pins the new `defaultModel`. The base URL is a `${XDEV_SERVER_URL}` template — the catalog validator admits a templated base (`${NEON_AI_GATEWAY_BASE_URL}/v1` is the precedent) and `loadModelsFile` expands `${VAR}` through `expandEnvYAML` before parsing, so one row serves every gateway host with no address in the repo. Verified against a live gateway: `connect --list` shows the row `ready` from the environment, `connect xdev-server --set-default` writes the block, and `xdev print -model xdev-server/free "…"` answers "pong" through `/v1/chat/completions`. **Ceiling: `XDEV_SERVER_URL` must be exported (or placed in the agent `.env`) before the row's `baseUrl` resolves; an unset variable expands to the empty string and the provider fails at request time, not at load.**

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (request-side thinking toggle — the other half of #20, tracked as part 3 of [#140](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/140)): `Shift+Tab` / `/thinking off` now asks for **no reasoning** instead of merely hiding it. The `thinking` settings key (auto|off|minimal|low|medium|high|xhigh|max) folds under `--thinking` and over the role's `:effort` in every run mode — including ACP, which had silently ignored `--thinking` since it shipped — and `effortBudget` now drops a zero-token budget to nil so `minimal`/`off` omit the parameter instead of sending `max_tokens: 0` (Anthropic rejects it; OpenAI read it as "low"). `/thinking [level]` pins a rung and persists it; the chord toggles off ⇄ auto, re-folding against the role effort after each `/model` switch. Display (`showThinking`) and request are now two separate switches.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (user-reported "xdev not retry with this error": `HTTP 400 [invalid_request_error] `input[185]` missing required field `output`` through onegw/Console Go): a tool that ran and printed nothing built a toolResult with empty text, and the openai-responses encoder marshalled `function_call_output.output` with `omitempty`, so the item serialized **without a required field** and the upstream rejected the whole turn. Two defects made it terminal rather than a blip: `ai.Classify` calls a plain 400 `ClassBadRequest`, which the recovery ladder has no case for (fast fail), and the malformed item was **persisted**, so every later turn rebuilt the same rejected request — a retry alone could never have escaped it. The wrong thing was the tool result, so it is fixed where results are made: `ai.Message.EnsureToolOutput` substitutes the `"(no output)"` placeholder (the text the TUI, the compaction renderer and the bash sink already use) for a blank result, applied at `toolResultMsg` — the single choke point every tool result passes through — and again in `session.buildContext`, so sessions already on disk heal on resume instead of 400ing forever. `openaiRespItem.Output` is a `*string` so the field is present even for a caller that skipped the guard, and `isEmptyNoop` recognizes the placeholder so pruning still elides a silent tool. For the reported symptom, a 400 naming a missing required field is a shape a rebuilt request satisfies, so `Classify` now calls it transient and the ladder retries it (marked `ponytail:` with its ceiling and upgrade path); a 400 naming nothing retryable is still terminal.

*Last updated: 2026-09-19 (fix/thinking-empty-turn, issue #389): the empty-turn nudge now fires on reasoning-only turns. `isEmptyAssistant` in `internal/agent/compact_elide.go` checks for thinking blocks in addition to empty text/tool-call state — a turn with only thinking blocks (and no text) is empty, so the nudge re-asks the model or surfaces ErrEmptyTurn instead of looping until the turn budget expires. Regression test `TestIsEmptyAssistantReasoningOnly` added in `internal/agent/compact_elide_test.go`.#389: a thinking-only turn (thinking blocks, `stopReason: stop`, no text) is now correctly detected as empty by `isEmptyAssistant`. Previously `isEmptyAssistant` only checked `m.Text() == "" && len(m.ToolCalls()) == 0` — a turn carrying only thinking blocks had blocks and so was "non-empty", bypassing both the

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (user-reported "only allow mouse wheel scroll when user left click to the thinking box, otherwise the mouse wheel always scroll the chat session as default" — branch `fix/tui-think-box-focus`): the reasoning box's wheel was **pointer-driven**, and that was the defect. `scrollThinkBox` hit-tested the screen row under the wheel and scrolled whatever `KindThinking` block it found, so reading a transcript that had accumulated boxes meant scrolling every box the cursor crossed — the box took the notch and the conversation stayed put. The wheel now belongs to the transcript until a **left click focuses a box**: `App.thinkFocus` (block index, `-1` = none, set in the `Button1` press arm of `handleMouse` via the new `thinkBoxAt(y)` hit-test that both the click and the notch share) is the only thing `scrollThinkBox` consults, and a notch anywhere but the focused box — another box, another block, chrome, or nothing focused at all — returns unconsumed so the caller's `a.scroll(3, …)` runs as the default. The notch itself never moves the focus, which is the whole point: a box sliding under a stationary pointer can no longer steal it, and `thinkBoxAt` is asked again per notch so a focused box that has left the viewport simply stops consuming instead of scrolling off screen. The focused box says so on screen — `thinkBoxLines(i, …)` draws its rule `Bold(true)` when `i == a.thinkFocus`, and `focused: i == a.thinkFocus` joins `blockKey` so the focus change re-renders exactly the two boxes it moved between. Focus is cleared wherever its box stops existing: `New` starts at `-1`, `Reset` clears it with the blocks, and `SetShowThinking(false)` clears it with the dropped boxes. Tests: `TestWheelOnlyScrollsAFocusedThinkBox` (notch over an unfocused box scrolls the transcript and leaves `ThinkOff` at 0 → click focuses it and the frame goes bold → the notch now moves its window → a click off it hands the wheel back) replaces the old pointer-routing `TestWheelScrollsTheThinkBoxUnderIt`; `TestThinkBoxRendersInAFixedWindow`, `TestThinkWindowClampsAtBothEnds`, `TestBlockAtFindsTheOwningBlock` and `TestDrawnThinkingBoxDrawsOneEdge` are unchanged.

*Last updated: 2026-09-18 (TODO-tracker engine wired: stop-time reminders, mid-run nudges, failed-todo reminder; PRD row 126 closed): the context dock was written to disk and never read back, so every resume painted an empty panel beside a transcript full of work. Two of the five sections had a reader that only looked at live process state. **FILES** is derived from the finished result blocks (`App.dockChanges`), and `replayTranscript` handled `RoleUser`/`RoleAssistant` only — `RoleToolResult` was replayed nowhere, so no done-block carrying a diff existed after a reopen. **TASKS** is `TodoTool.Snapshot()`, and while `sessionTodoSink.AppendTodo` has written the omp-canonical `user_todo_edit` entry since the tool landed, nothing ever read one back. Both are restored at the two seams that already existed rather than per entrypoint. `replayTranscript` gained the missing `ai.RoleToolResult` arm: it appends the call row and the result row through the same `AddToolBlock`/`FinishTool` pair a live turn uses, carrying `tool.OutcomeOf(m.Details)` (exit code, truncated, unified diff) and the recorded `Message.DurationMS` — only when it is > 0, because tens of thousands of real toolResult lines predate that field and a `0s` footer would be a lie. `WireTodoSink` now *hydrates* as well as attaches: `replayPhases` walks the active chain leaf→root newest-first (the same walk as `notesChain`, stopping at a `*session.ResetBoundaryEntry` so `/clear` hides pre-boundary lists), takes the first `user_todo_edit` custom entry, and falls back to a non-error `todo` toolResult's `details.phases` — omp's replay order (`docs/research/omp-todos-internals.md` §5.1). That one function is the choke point for every caller (startup, `/new`, `/drop`, `swapStoreTo`, print, rpc, acp), so `/resume` and `/new` are both right: a fresh store hydrates to nil and `adopt` replaces whatever list the previous session left in the tool. No new dependency, no second store, no per-frame read — a windowed store that pruned past the last boundary simply finds nothing, which is a real state. AGENTS and PLAN legitimately stay empty: the roster lives in this process's hub and the pending plan in its `PlanMode`; neither is session state, and a store invented for them would be a second source of truth. Covered by `TestReplayTranscriptRestoresToolResults` (a resumed result block carries its diff, duration and outcome; a result with no recorded duration reports none) and the five `TestWireTodoSink*` cases (restore from a JSON round-tripped payload, newest edit wins, reset boundary hides earlier lists, toolResult fallback, empty session clears the previous list).

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (user: "what is the current default model config of xdev ... some session changed that"; branch `sticky-model`, issue [#338](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/338)). **A typo in `xdev config set` was a config wipe with a success message.** `config.Set` had lost the schema check `f1e43c1` added — the `wip:` snapshot that followed deleted the guard body and left `settingsKeyOK` in `settingskeys.go` as dead code — so `config set showThinkng true` printed "set ..." and exited 0, and the NEXT start rejected the file (per-layer loads decode `KnownFields(true)`), renamed it `.broken-<stamp>-config.yml` and came up on defaults with every setting gone: 16 such files in one afternoon, and a `defaultModel` that kept sliding from `onegw/xdev` to `onegw/free`. Both writers also used `os.WriteFile` (truncate + write), so a second session reading mid-write saw half a document and quarantined the same file — reproduced with parallel writers. `Set` and `DeleteKey` now check the dotted key against the `Settings` schema before touching anything, decode the bytes they are about to write the way the loader will, and replace the file in one rename (mode preserved). Separately, `/model` was not sticky: `setRef` moved the live holder and the session entry but never persisted, so a new process re-resolved `settings.defaultModel` and snapped back. `persistDefaultModel` writes the resolved ref (and the in-memory settings) on every switch, so a new session starts on the model the user last chose. Ceiling: `settingsKeyOK` accepts any name under a map field whose values are scalars or free-form (`toolsApproval.*`, `hooks.*`) and walks struct-valued maps (`debug.adapters`) like structs — a name it cannot classify is refused, never guessed at.

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (docs: where a standing convention belongs — the prompt has two channels that look interchangeable to someone writing a rule down, and are not). Measured against the live Hindsight bank: one recall query returned 8 notes / 8756 chars against a **1024-token (4096-char)** injection cap, so `capRecall` kept two notes and part of a third — a worktree convention that lived only in the bank landed at char 4357 of a 4096-char window. Where it did arrive, the block's own *"not authoritative"* framing did the rest: a session read the rule, called it *"a recalled instruction from earlier sessions … heavy for a one-line helper"*, and skipped it. New [decision](decisions/standing-conventions-location.md) records the rule — cross-repo habits to `~/.xdev/agent/AGENTS.md`, repo invariants to `<repo>/AGENTS.md`, must-apply-even-when-shadowed to `<repo>/RULES.md`; recall carries facts that inform a plan, not policy — with the budget/selection/framing table, the four measurements, and three follow-ups (C1: `xdev setup` scaffolds or reports the four always-loaded surfaces it currently ignores, `internal/dist/setup.go:62`; C2: README documents them — done here, a new *"What is loaded into the prompt, and when"* table in §Configuration; C3: `xdev` gets the repo-level `AGENTS.md` this convention asks for). PRD §5 carries the decision summary and §2's context-files row is unchanged — this is placement policy, not a new feature.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-17 (the edit tool's two live failure modes now teach the fix — branch `fix/edit-failures`): `Execute` no longer double-prefixes a grammar rejection (`edit: edit: line 2: …` → `edit: line 2: …`; `strings.TrimPrefix`, `TestEditErrorsCarryOnePrefix`); `parseHashline` detects a unified diff (a `@@ -1,3 +1,3 @@` hunk header, scanned before the parse loop so `--- a/f` cannot be read as a file header) and answers with the hashline form instead of a parser complaint; the missing-`+`-row and bad-header errors name the convention they wanted; and `Parameters()`'s description leads with the `+` transport rule, which previously sat past the point a truncating provider cuts. `internal/tool` + `internal/agent` green (the `cmd/xdev` `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills` failure is pre-existing — it reproduces on a clean tree with this change stashed, and is about the global skills directory, not this diff).
*Last updated: 2026-09-18 (branch `feat/cursor-provider`): **Cursor connects with no key pasted.** The `/connect` catalog is generated from models.dev, which knows Cursor only as a per-token API, so a subscription user had to find a key and export it. `internal/config/connect_extra.go` now carries a curated `cursor` row (`https://api.cursor.com/v1`, `openai-completions`, `CURSOR_API_KEY`, doc link) in the `extraConnectCatalog` map `gen_connect.go` cannot overwrite, with **no pinned models** — `ConnectProviderConfig` always sets `Discovery = openai-models`, so the live endpoint supplies them at connect time. `isPlan("cursor")` is true: it is a flat monthly subscription, and the listing says so. The credential chain gains one rung between `credentials.json` and the environment: `cursorTokenSource()` (`internal/config/cursor.go`) reads the session JWT Cursor's own IDE stores — the macOS Keychain item `cursor-access-token`, else `~/Library/Application Support/Cursor/User/globalStorage/state.vscdb` key `cursorAuth/accessToken`, opened `mode=ro` — so the IDE *is* the credential and `CURSOR_API_KEY` is never the only way in. Both stores are read-only and silent on absence: a machine without Cursor falls through to the env var rather than erroring. Tests: `TestCursorConnectCatalog`, `TestCursorCredentialChainOrder`, `TestCursorReadyFromLocalToken`, `TestCursorIsCursorProvider`, `TestCursorLocalTokenSource` (the last skips off darwin). Unverified: the Keychain read, the `state.vscdb` read, and the live `/models` call need a machine with Cursor installed and a subscription login.

*Last updated: 2026-09-18 (the release train was stopped by two committed defects, and `xdev update` was installing old code under a newer tag): `cmd/xdev/print.go` assigned `settings.RetryConfig().Infinite` — a `*bool` — to `ag.Retry.Infinite` (a `bool`), so all six `release-artifacts` builds failed and **no v0.4.43 was ever published**; `/releases/latest` therefore kept resolving to **v0.4.42**, a tag parked on the abandoned `release` branch at `7bc7bf3` (94 commits behind main), and both `xdev update` and `scripts/install.sh` installed that stale build. Reading through the existing nil-safe `settings.InfiniteRetry()` restores the build. Separately, `3b2111d` (#352) was committed from a stale working tree and silently reverted #347's `internal/agent/loop.go` work — `ErrEmptyTurn`, the `maxEmptyTurnNudges` budget and #345's `maxPostContentContinuations` — while #347's `loop_test.go` survived, so `go vet ./...` and `go test ./internal/agent/` no longer compiled on main; that blob is restored (keeping #352's health-check probe), which also exposed that #345's own test was red: `policy.Infinite` sets `resumeLeft = -1` but the guard demanded `resumeLeft > 0`, so the infinite ladder could never re-enter retain-and-continue.
*Last updated: 2026-09-18 (web_search zero-config default now DuckDuckGo — `internal/websearch/websearch.go` `runtimeDefaultProviders = ["duckduckgo"]`, chain() skips keyed providers without a key instead of running them and emitting two skip notes; test `TestRuntimeDefaultIsKeyless`): an unconfigured install runs `web_search` end-to-end through the no-JS DuckDuckGo endpoint, so a user without TAVILY_API_KEY or BRAVE_API_KEY no longer sees `tavily: skipped: no API key` / `brave: skipped: no API key` in the answer — issue #110.*
 2026-09-18 (user-reported "the onegw provider failed every run: upstream HTTP 404" + "also fix the click debug log still showing up in textbox chat"): the health-check probe asked for `baseURL + "/v1/models"`, but the user's `onegw` provider has `baseUrl: http://127.0.0.1:8080/v1` with `discovery.injectV1: false` — so the probe hit `/v1/v1/models`, which onegw answers 404, and `healthCheckProvider` then refused every turn. Two fixes: the probe URL now mirrors `internal/config/discovery.go`'s rule (`{base}/models`, or `{base}/v1/models` only when the base carries no version segment) via the new `modelsProbeURL` helper, and `healthCheckOneGet` treats a non-transport response as "the host answered" — a 404 on a catalog path is not a dead host, and the turn is where an auth or path fault belongs. Separately, `internal/tui/selection.go` printed `DEBUG handleClick: …` to stdout on every click, which surfaced in the chat textbox; the print and its now-unused `fmt` import are gone. The same file also had a dropped `a.thinkFocus = a.thinkBoxAt(y)` (the comment still describes the behavior, the line was missing), so clicking a reasoning box no longer focused it for wheel scrolling — restored. Double/triple-click word and line selection were also red on main (#350 landed them with their own tests failing): `handleClick` now returns whether it consumed the press, so the caller no longer re-anchors the gesture at the raw click point and collapses the selection back into a no-motion click, and `selStartWordSelect` pins the word columns to screen coordinates (they were text-relative, so the span resolved against `x0` and copied nothing). `TestDoubleClickSelectsWord`, `TestTripleClickSelectsLine` and `TestWheelOnlyScrollsAFocusedThinkBox` all pass. Pushed as `03e552d` directly to `origin/main` per the user's instruction.*Last updated: 2026-09-18 (hardening pass on web_search: `max_results` validated at the tool boundary in `internal/tool/websearch.go`, `sanitizeResults` nil-guards its `limit` arg in `internal/websearch/websearch.go`, test `TestWebSearchToolRejectsBadMaxResults`; PR #355): negative and over-ceiling `max_results` are now rejected before the engine sees them, and a negative limit can no longer underflow `sanitizeResults` instead of capping rows.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-18 (hardening pass on web_search: `max_results` validated at the tool boundary in `internal/tool/websearch.go`, `sanitizeResults` nil-guards its `limit` arg in `internal/websearch/websearch.go`, test `TestWebSearchToolRejectsBadMaxResults`; PR #355): negative and over-ceiling `max_results` are now rejected before the engine sees them, and a negative limit can no longer underflow `sanitizeResults` instead of capping rows.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-18 (user: "update xdev always use last thinking mode, if not work with model switch to auto" — branch `feat/thinking-sticky`): the pinned `thinking` level is now **sticky across model switches**. Previously a `/model` switch re-folded the same level against the new model unchanged, so e.g. `thinking: high` rode out verbatim to a model that cannot reason. Now `thinkingForModel` (cmd/xdev/launchflags.go) downgrades an actual reasoning budget (`low|medium|high`, plus the `xhigh/max` names that clamp to `high`) to `auto` whenever the catalog marks that model `reasoning: false` — via the new `modelReasoning` helper (cmd/xdev/print.go), unknown = "no opinion", not unsupported. `off`/`minimal` are already no-reasoning requests every model accepts, so they ride through untouched. The user's choice stays in `live.level` (and the persisted `thinking` key); only the folded budget (`live.effort`) follows the fallback, so switching back to a reasoning model restores the level without re-pinning. A `/model` switch to a non-reasoning model says so once in a system block. Same fallback applied at startup in all four run modes (print, RPC, TUI, ACP) so a pinned level never reaches the wire of a model that cannot take it. Test `TestThinkingForModel`.


*Last updated: 2026-09-18 (user-reported `browser` failure — "no Chrome DevTools endpoint at http://127.0.0.1:9222 … connection refused"; the error told the model to start Chrome, which it has no way to do, so the tool stayed dead for the rest of every session once the user's Chrome exited; branch `feat/browser-autostart`). **Attach-only was the defect.** M13 shipped "attach to a Chrome you started"; a headless endpoint does not outlive a long session, and the tool's own message asked for an action the caller cannot take. The tool is now **attach-first, launch-second**: when `ListTargets` finds nothing on a loopback endpoint, the tool starts a browser of its own — private `--user-data-dir` under the data dir (`<DataDir>/browser/profile`), `--remote-debugging-port=<port of the configured endpoint>`, `--remote-debugging-address=127.0.0.1`, `about:blank`, stdout/stderr discarded, never waited on — re-lists targets, then attaches. `browser.autolaunch: false` (or a tool with no profile dir) restores the old attach-only error, and a non-loopback endpoint is never launched on locally. The binary resolves through `$XDEV_BROWSER_BIN` → PATH (`google-chrome`, `google-chrome-stable`, `chromium`, `chromium-browser`, `chrome`, `brave-browser`) → per-OS install dirs; a launch that never opens its port is killed and reported, bounded by the call's own deadline (the per-op timeout, floor 1s) instead of a fixed wait, and the failure is remembered so the next call reports the cause rather than relaunching. `browser.autolaunch` layers like any other key (absent = on, so an existing config keeps working). Checks: `TestEndpointPort`, `TestAutolaunchOn`, `TestUnreachableErrNamesTheRightFix`, `TestLaunchBrowserRefusesRemoteEndpoint`, `TestFindChromeEnvOverride`, `TestEnsureLaunchedFailsFast` (a fake browser that never opens its port is killed), `TestBrowserSettingsLayer`.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-18 (an idle exit for the auto-launched browser, same branch `feat/browser-autostart`; user follow-up "is this auto close after use?"). A browser xdev starts is now stopped again after five minutes with no `browser` op: `browser.idleExit: <seconds>` (default 300, `0` keeps it for the session) in `internal/browser/tool.go`, armed on launch and re-armed by every op via a single reused `time.Timer` (`startIdle`/`pingIdle`), fired into `stopIdle`, which closes the pages through Chrome's HTTP `/json/close/<id>` before killing the process xdev started (`internal/browser/launch.go` `launched`/`stop`; `ensureLaunched` now returns the live process). `Tool.Close` stops it too, so a `defer tl.Close()` releases instead of leaking. A browser the **user** started is never touched. The tool interface has no event stream, so the removal is reported the one way a tool can reach the model: the notice rides on the next `Result` text and is cleared once (`pendingIdleNotice`), and a live endpoint that is not this tool's browser clears it silently. Checks: `TestIdleTimeoutOn`, `TestPendingIdleNotice` (one-shot, and silent when something else is listening), `TestBrowserSettingsLayer` for layering `idleExit: 30` / `0`, and the opt-in real-Chrome run, which now also proves the browser is gone ~2s after the last op, comes back on the next `open`, and carries the notice exactly once. Also fixed a test-only blind spot found doing it: the liveness probe now marks itself (`?_xdev=probe`) and the fake's attach counts use `listPageHits`. PR #356, unmerged.
*Last updated: 2026-09-19 (user-reported "xdev not retry and resume — session stopped on a tool name too long"): a tool name over the provider's 64-character ceiling (from an MCP server or extension binary, which xdev does not control) made onegw/Console answer `HTTP 400 [invalid_request_error]` "`name` must be at most 64 characters, got 73". `ai.Classify` read that as `ClassBadRequest` (fail fast), so the retry ladder never re-entered the turn and the run died with no way back — retried, the next turn rebuilds the request from history and only uses tool names the model already called, which never exceed the limit. Fix: a missing-required-field 400 (`malformedRequestRe`) or a too-long tool-name 400 (`toolNameTooLongRe`) now classifies as `ClassTransient` so the ladder retries and the session resumes. Regression: `TestClassify/400_tool_name_too_long`.
*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (fix/session-not-a-turn, branch `feat/session-not-a-turn`): the session token budget is now **per-turn**, not cumulative. A session is not a turn, so capping the session ended a long task that legitimately burned millions of tokens across many turns and asked the user to say "continue" to restart it — the exact complaint behind the 2026-09-19 entry below. `DefaultTurnTokenBudget = 5_000_000` in `internal/agent/loop.go` is now a per-turn floor: the provider caps one turn's output, so a real turn cannot reach it, and when a single turn does cross it the turn wraps up with a status report inline and the **session keeps going instead of ending**. The wrap-up's tool calls run (the old "do not execute them" contract only made sense while the run was ending). Only the turn cap (`MaxTurns`, default 0 = unbounded) ends a run, and it does so with one wrap-up message rather than an error. Tests: `TestRunWrapsUpAtTurnLimit` (turn cap still wraps up and stops) and new `TestRunWrapsUpAtPerTurnTokenBudget` (per-turn cap wraps up inline and the run keeps going). The TUI message is now "· turn wrapped up — the session keeps going instead of asking you to say \"continue\"".

*Last updated: 2026-09-19 (session token cost optimization, `feat/optimize-session-cost`): three changes to cap worst-case token burn — added a hard session token budget (`DefaultSessionTokenBudget = 5_000_000` in `internal/agent/loop.go`, tracked per turn and breaking the loop before the next provider request) that fires a final wrap-up turn, made the goal token budget a hard run-stop via `Goa…[+201b]

*Last updated: 2026-09-20 (fix/model-live-switch): a `/model` (or role) switch in the TUI now takes effect immediately — the next submit runs on the new provider/model instead of the one the session started with. `setRef` in `cmd/xdev/tui.go` built the new provider but discarded it with `_ = nprov`; the `live` holder (`live.prov`/`live.model`/`live.provName`), which `startTurn`/handoff/vibe/task-tool read for every turn, kept the old values until the session restarted. The RPC path already updated its equivalent state (`rpc.go` `SetModel`); the TUI now mirrors it under `modelMu`.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-20 (fix/no-turn-cap: the 200-turn cap is removed — `effectiveMaxTurns()` returns `MaxTurns` directly, and `MaxTurns=0` (the default) means unbounded; `goalActive()` and the goal-specific exemption are gone; the session token budget (`DefaultSessionTokenBudget = 5_000_000`) remains as the hard stop)*
*Last updated: 2026-09-20 (test: double-click dock deadlock regression, `internal/tui/double_click_dock_test.go`): the TUI dock's FILES row can be double-clicked to open a full-width diff overlay; a latent lock ordering — handleMouse holds a.mu and dockClick→openDiffOverlay tried to take it again — self-deadlocked. The lock was reordered (openDiffOverlay/closeDiffOverlay hold it, callers that already own it don't re-take) and guarded by a regression test.
*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (fix/empty-turn-tui-acp): the empty-turn retry was wired into print and rpc modes only (#382), so a blank turn in the TUI (the daily driver) or over ACP (the Claude Code / Copilot CLI bridge) still died with a dead-end error. Both now rebuild context from the persisted history and re-run the agent, matching print/rpc: `cmd/xdev/tui.go` (the turn loop) and `cmd/xdev/acp.go` (the ACP session handler).*

*Last updated: 2026-09-20 (fix/403-upstream-server-error): xdev did not retry `HTTP 403 Error: 403 Upstream request failed: [server_error] Upstream response was not valid JSON` — a 403 the gateway wraps when the UPSTREAM served an invalid response. `Classify` treated every 403 as `ClassAuthFailed` and failed fast, so an upstream hiccup ended the run instead of being retried. `internal/ai/errors.go`: split the 401/403 case — a bare 403 stays `ClassAuthFailed`; a 403 whose body matches the new `serverErrorRe` (`upstream request failed.*server_error.*not valid json`) classifies `ClassTransient` and the M5 ladder backoff-retries it. `internal/ai/errors_test.go`: added `403 upstream server_error` case.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-20 (trajectory ledger — deepseek-harness `ui-trajectory` parity, branch `feat/trajectory-view`): the session's own event ledger now has a surface. `/trajectory` (alias `/traj`) opens a modal list of the session's materialized records — prompts, reasoning, tool calls and results, compactions, model switches, resets, branch summaries — one row per record (`#N kind preview`, a heavier rule above each human turn), and Enter opens the selected record's inspector: the full body with streamed reasoning under it plus a machine-fact line (tokens, cost, duration, tool exit). `internal/tui/trajectory.go` owns selection, the inspector and rendering; the rows are built in `cmd/xdev/tui.go` (`trajectoryRows`/`trajectoryRow`/`trajectoryDetail`/`trajectoryThinking`/`trajectoryMeta`) from the session store, on open, never cached — the split the hub roster already uses, so no TUI-side store machinery. Deliberate scope cut from the reference: no per-record request/response envelopes, no virtualized paging, no search index (a terminal viewport is one screen). The panel gains its first non-file clickable row: `dockTrajFold` renders `TRAJECTORY · <n> records` and `dockRowAt` now returns a row's `act` alongside its `path`, so `selection.go` opens the ledger from a click the way `dockAct` runs it; `humanTokens` was exported as `tui.HumanTokens` for the row's own token counts rather than a second formatter. Tests: `internal/tui/trajectory_test.go` (smoke open/navigate/inspect/close) plus the existing dock, help-alignment and command-uniqueness tests, all green.*


*Last updated: 2026-09-20 (fix/decode-thinking-cjk): thinking decode still landed mojibake in the thinking box for English / Mandarin / Vietnamese text — a vendor splicing invalid UTF-8 into its SSE JSON (or already substituting U+FFFD before framing) survived Go's `encoding/json` and was framed + persisted. Three guards: (1) `internal/ai/sse.Reader` rejects a data payload that is not valid UTF-8 as `ErrMalformed` so the adapter's `ErrMalformedStream` retry ladder treats the bad bytes as a transient stream; (2) `ai.CleanUTF8` drops invalid bytes and U+FFFD at every text/thinking emit (openai-completions / responses / anthropic / google) and on `ParseBlock` load so a resumed session never re-frames prior junk; (3) `tui.sanitizeOutput` drops U+FFFD so the thinking box never paints the replacement character even if a delta slipped past the wire. English, Mandarin, Vietnamese diacritics and every other real script pass through unchanged — they are valid UTF-8 without U+FFFD. Also completes the incomplete #400 merge: `AutoStartConfig` / `HealthEndpoint` on `mcpclient.ServerConfig` (the health helpers landed without their types).
*Last updated: 2026-09-20 (fix: diff overlay mouse wheel + arrow scroll): the full-width diff surface opened from a dock FILES click could not scroll. Root cause: `openDiffOverlay` set `scrollVp = len(lines)`, so `maxOff` was always 0 and `diffBodyScroll` was a no-op; the footer advertised `↑↓ scroll` but neither arrow keys nor the mouse wheel were routed to the overlay (wheel scrolled the transcript underneath). Fix: `scrollVp` is the painted body height (`panelH-3`, refreshed each frame); `handleDiffOverlayKey` owns ↑↓/PgUp/PgDn/Home/End while open; wheel and `scroll-*` keymap actions prefer the overlay when it is up. Tests in `internal/tui/diff_overlay_scroll_test.go`.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (user-reported "xdev showing the leankg unavailable" + retry-all-errors 403-upstream): two fixes this date. (1) autoStart env: `AutoStartConfig` had no `Env` field, so `autoStart.env` was silently discarded by YAML and a server that needed env (leankg: its sidecar defaults to port 8080, held by the onegw gateway) died during startup with `couldn't bind HTTP server socket`, leaving `:9699/health` refusing and xdev reporting `mcp: leankg unavailable`. Fix: `Env` field on `AutoStartConfig` (`mcp.go`), resolved through the same `${VAR}`/`!command` path in `resolveSecrets` (`config.go`), merged over `os.Environ()` at launch (`health.go`); tests `TestStartAutoPassesEnv`, `TestLoadConfigAutoStartEnv`. (2) `retry.infinite` + broader 403 matching in `ai.Classify` — auth/bad-request/unknown no longer end the turn on first failure: `oneTurnWithRecovery` rebuilds history from the current context window and re-sends, bounded by `maxEscalationRounds`.*

Last updated: 2026-09-22 (user-reported "retry everything" + 403 upstream not-valid-JSON): auth/bad-request/unknown no longer end the turn on first failure

*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (fix/agent-empty-turn-and-turn-budget — two agent-loop fixes land together): (1) **the session token budget is now per-turn, not cumulative.** A session is not a turn, so capping the session ended a long task that legitimately burned millions of tokens across many turns and asked the user to say "continue" to restart it — the exact complaint behind the 2026-09-19 entry. `DefaultTurnTokenBudget = 5_000_000` is a per-turn floor the provider caps one turn's output below, so a real turn cannot reach it; when one does, the turn wraps up inline with a status report and the **session keeps going** instead of ending — the wrap-up's tool calls run, because the session is staying alive and the model is still working. Renames: `SessionTokenBudget` → `TurnTokenBudget`, `effectiveSessionTokenBudget` → `effectiveTurnTokenBudget`, `DefaultSessionTokenBudget` → `DefaultTurnTokenBudget`; the old `budgetHit`/`break`/`spentSoFar` machinery is gone, replaced by an inline `continue`. Test: `TestRunWrapsUpAtPerTurnTokenBudget`. (2) **`retry.retryAllErrors` now retries empty turns.** A model that answers nothing after the nudge budget is spent used to end the run with `ErrEmptyTurn`. With the flag on, the loop rebuilds context from the persisted history, waits a backoff, and re-runs the ladder — so a transient upstream stall never looks like a silent death. `agent.RetryPolicy` gained `RetryAllErrors bool` (default off); `config.RetrySettings` gained `RetryAllErrors *bool` + `RetryAllErrors()` accessor (default on, explicit `false` re-bounds) and a one-way merge that only turns it on. CLI: `-retry-all-errors` forces it on for one run. The flag was already wired into print and rpc modes (#382); the agent loop itself is the missing half, so a blank turn in the TUI (the daily driver) or over ACP (the Claude Code / Copilot CLI bridge) now loops instead of dying. Tests: `TestEmptyTurnRetryAllErrors`, `TestEmptyTurnRetryAllErrorsStillStalls`.

*Last updated: 2026-09-21 (fix/retry-everything-progress — keep retrying without freezing): three freezes in the "retry everything" path are closed. (1) `RetryPolicy.withDefaults` now carries `RetryAllErrors` across the default fill-in (it already carried `Infinite`); wireAgentMode only sets the flags, never the timings, so the flag was silently dropped and empty-turn recovery never armed. (2) `delay(n)` clamps `n < 1` to 1 — callers that reset `attempt = 0` before sleeping (auth/bad-request escalation, empty-turn rebuild) used to busy-spin on `delay(0)`. (3) every unbounded recovery path now backoffs + announces on the event stream: empty-turn (`EmptyTurnRetryError` via `noticeEmptyTurnRetry`), post-content infinite with nothing retainable, and ClassEmptyTurn rebuilds. TUI/print show the notice verbatim so a long stall reads as waiting, not hung. Also: TUI prompt-continuation without `/goal` (hidden nudge when tools already ran or todos are open; cap 8; print/RPC/ACP unchanged). Tests: `TestEmptyTurnRetryAllErrorsAnnouncesOnStream`, `TestDelayClampsNonPositiveAttempt`, `TestInfiniteRidesWithDefaults` (RetryAllErrors), `TestPromptContinuationKeepsTheRunGoing`.

*Last updated: 2026-09-21 (fix/subagent-context-files — the context-file hierarchy never reached subagents: `LoadContextFiles` had one caller in the parent prompt path, so every `task` child and vibe worker ran on the bare base prose with none of the standing conventions; `SpawnChild` now appends them for the child's cwd, pinned by a test).*

*Last updated: 2026-09-21 (feat/trajectory-rich — richer /trajectory ledger): the session event ledger stopped being a thin kind+preview list. Each row now carries a machine-fact tail when it fits (latency, token split), and Enter opens an inspector with (1) full tool-call JSON payloads for assistant turns that only called tools, (2) the token split opencode/dsh keep — `↑new · ⇢cache …[+511b]

*Last updated: 2026-09-21 (feat/trajectory-rich follow-up — tool-call wall time on disk): `toolResultMsg` now stamps `Message.DurationMS` from the same `time.Since(started)` `OnToolEnd` already measured, so `/trajectory` tool rows show latency (and resume keeps it). `tool.Outcome.DurationMS` surfaces bash `durationMs` from Details as a fallback for older sessions. Tests: `TestToolResultMsgKe…[+60b]

*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (fix/goal-target-only — `/goal <target goal>` works, and the TUI's `error: goal not wired` is fixed): two user-reported `/goal` defects land together. (1) **The command no longer requires a verb.** `/goal ship the exporter` names the session's objective and starts the first turn; a bare `/goal` shows the goal and budget; `complete`/`drop` are the two words that clos…[+1172b]
*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (fix/retry-notices — the "stream error — retrying (x2)" freeze): the retain-and-continue recovery (`post-content failure` — the partial is kept, a continuation turn is injected, the ladder re-asks) was the one retry the console showed as a bare collapsed blip under the older "· provider cut off mid-message" block, so a run that kept re-asking read as hung. `agent.ContinuationRetryError` now rides `TurnHooks.OnEvent` for both post-content arms of `oneTurnWithRecovery` (retainable partial and infinite-without-partial) **before** the backoff, carrying the delay it is about to sleep — the same contract `AllTargetsDownError` / `EmptyTurnRetryError` already had. TUI and print now print it verbatim as "· provider cut off mid-message — continuation injected, retrying in 2s" instead of letting `ai.Classify` collapse it into "retrying (xN)"; both backoffs also now go through the announced delay instead of a bare `sleepBackoff`. Test: `TestContinuationRoundIsAnnouncedOnTheStream`.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (feat/edit-verification — the parse tier of post-edit diagnostics): `edit`/`write` now run `go/parser` over a Go file they just wrote and append a one-line note to the tool result when it no longer parses, mirrored into `Details.syntaxWarning` (`internal/tool/verify.go`). The staleness guard proves a patch landed where the model aimed; nothing proved the result still compiled, so the model learned that one turn later (or never) when its build failed. Reports, never vetoes: the bytes are already on disk and the note is information, not a gate. Non-Go files and files over 2 MiB are skipped (neither is the shape an edit patch makes). It is the cheap end of the diagnostics ladder on purpose — [#263](https://github.com/FreePeak/xdev/issues/263) owns the LSP `publishDiagnostics` tier, and the import direction forbids the other order (`internal/lsp/tool.go` imports `internal/tool`, not the reverse), which the sibling issue is now blocked on noting.)
*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (docs: ZCode cross-check — [research/zcode-internals.md](research/zcode-internals.md) + §5.5): a same-scope, opposite-stack peer (TypeScript/Node, SQLite event ledger, Vercel AI SDK, session residency pool, hard 400-line file ceiling) was read at primary source and compared mechanic by mechanic. Five findings change a stated design assumption and became issues: **a tool's capabilities belong in its declaration** (`readOnly`/`concurrentSafe`/`sideEffectScope`/`needsApproval`/`stopTurnOnSuccess` read by plan mode + scheduler + approval gate, replacing xdev's three parallel name-keyed tables and closing parity T3 #27/#28's fail-open plan list); **compaction needs a breaker** (`MAX_CONSECUTIVE_RAPID_REFILLS = 3` — compaction that does not buy space is a failure mode, and xdev counts nothing); **read-only tools can run during the stream** (gated on the manifest, with an end-of-stream fallback and preserved results on a mid-stream model failure); **a stream read must be abortable while the provider is wedged** (raced against the turn signal, with an attempt-escalated idle window) alongside xdev's source-side liveness judgement — both properties, one watchdog; **the prompt must state its own contract** (mid-turn text may be invisible; compaction is coming, do not wrap up early; how permissions/hooks/system turns behave). §3.2/§3.3/§3.4/§3.6/§3.9 carry the deltas inline; §5.5 records the verified-and-rejected items too (no SQLite session store, no AI SDK, no hard file ceiling).
*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (fix/tool-capabilities-420 — #420 tool capability manifests): one `tool.Caps` declaration (`internal/tool/caps.go`) replaces the parallel name tables. `planReadOnlyTools` is gone; plan mode fails closed on undeclared tools via `AllowedInPlan`. `Classify` still names only the core four so `Decide`'s TierExec default for dynamic tools is unchanged. `ConcurrentOK` is the stream-time / scheduler gate for #421. Table-diff + undeclared fail-closed tests pin the behaviour.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-22 (fix/sidebar-title — the dock's title slot wraps instead of clipping): a user-reported defect — a long session title ended in "…" in the context dock's top slot, so the one string that names the conversation could not be read whole. `drawDock` painted `dockClip(a.dockTitle())`, a hard 38-cell cut with no second row. The slot now wraps: `dockState.titleLines` holds the session's name wrapped to the panel's interior (`dockTitleLines`, reusing the package's `wrap`, sanitizing through the same `sanitizeOutput` the rest of the panel uses), `titleRows()` is the geometry every row index below the slot shifts by (`layout`'s budget, `drawDock`, `dockRowAt`, `selDockRowsForPaint`), and the content budget is charged those rows so the panel never paints a section over its own name. `dockTitleMax` (3) bounds what the name may cost the work it names; past the cap the last row carries the clip's "…" again, so a title that is too long even wrapped still admits the cut rather than silently dropping text. Regression tests: `TestDockTitleWrapsNotClips` (a 66-cell title takes 2 rows, row 0 carries no "…", every row fits `dockInner`, and the first section still starts under the slot on screen) and `TestDockTitleBoundedAndClickMapFollows` (a 1000-cell title stops at the cap with the "…" back, the sections keep their rows, and the click map follows the shifted rows).*
*Last updated: 2026-09-21 (feat/settings-overlay — `Alt+,` opens a settings panel): the TUI's settings surface was a listing (`/settings`) plus two one-off toggles, so a user who wanted to change what the panel showed had to leave the session and run `xdev config set`. `Alt+,` (and `/settings overlay`) now opens a modal panel over the composer: a category tab strip, one row per setting with its live value, `↑↓`/`jk` to move, `Enter` to change the row under the cursor, `Tab`/`←→` to switch category, `r` to re-read from disk, click to select-and-change, `q`/`Esc` to close. Rows are `toggle` (● on / ○ off in the success/error accents) or `select` (cycles its options); a change is persisted to the global layer through the same `config.Set` path `xdev config set` uses — so a bad value fails in the panel instead of quarantining the config on the next start — and the ones the running session can apply live (`showThinking`, `sidebarMode`, `debugMouse`) take effect on the spot, exactly what `/settings showThinking` and `Alt+S` already did. `thinking` goes through the level ladder behind `ThinkingOps.Set`, so a level set in the panel and one set by `/thinking` cannot disagree about the wire. `keyName` now names punctuation, because `A-,` arrives as ESC + a comma and a chord the runtime cannot resolve is a listed binding that does nothing. Tests: `internal/tui/settings_test.go` (toggle applies and persists; the panel paints its rows; a category tab moves the highlight *and* the action together; `r` picks up an external write; the default chord resolves; an unreadable boolean is never painted "off"; a squeezed window keeps the selection inside the painted rows). Driven end to end against the built binary: `Alt+,` paints the panel, `Enter` writes `showThinking: false` to the config file, and the next process reads it back. `internal/tui/settings.go` (new), `internal/tui/app.go`, `internal/tui/commands.go`, `internal/tui/keymap.go`, `cmd/xdev/tui.go`, README §TUI.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-21 (fix/retry-delay-overflow — the crash after a long retry or thinking loop): `RetryPolicy.delay` computed `base * time.Duration(1<<uint(n-1))`, and the counters that feed `n` (`escalation`, `emptyRetries`) are unbounded because `retry.infinite` and `retry.retryAllErrors` are both default-on. At n = 36 with the default 500ms base the multiply overflows int64 into a negative duration, the `MaxDelay` clamp cannot catch it, and the 25% jitter hands that negative to `rand.Int64N` — **panic: invalid argument to Int64N**, which kills the process and takes the TUI with it. `n >= 57` wrapped the same multiply to zero, which `sleepBackoff` reads as "nothing to wait for" — the busy-spin half of the same bug. The exponential now saturates by doubling up to `MaxDelay` (or `BaseDelay` when a policy sets no cap), so every `n` returns a positive backoff at or below the cap; `TestDelayCannotOverflowAtUnboundedRounds` fails on the old code at n = 36 with the exact panic.
*Last updated: 2026-09-21 (fix/handle-mouse-deadlock — the dock FILES click froze the whole TUI): `handleMouse`'s non-wheel branch in `app.go` runs the entire gesture with `a.mu` held, and the diff overlay's click helpers took `a.mu` again underneath it: `closeDiffOverlayOnClick` → `closeDiffOverlay` (and `dockToggleDiff`), which wedged the UI thread — keys, drawing and the quit chord all dead, process alive, exactly the "xdev froze" report. The state patch is now split from the lock: unexported `closeDiffOverlayLocked` does the work for callers that already hold the lock, `closeDiffOverlay` keeps its own lock for the callers that do not. `openDiffOverlay` was already lock-free (documented in place so it stays that way). Pinned by `TestDiffOverlayClickCannotDeadlock` and `TestDiffOverlayCloseIsSafeUnderTheLock`, both with a 5s watchdog: the first fails on the pre-fix code at the click, and the third helper (`dockToggleDiff`'s Esc chord, reached through `handleKey`) is covered by the second. Found while fixing #435; the 2026-09-20 11:13 stall dump had goroutine 1 parked on exactly this lock at `dock.go:859`.
*Last updated: 2026-09-21 (fix/bash-tui-crash — the "xdev crash when running bash" report): a session whose `bash` call took seconds longer than usual read as a crash because the UI loop froze and recovered without leaving a trace. Two defects, each with a test that fails against the old line:

1. **A recovered slow iteration left no evidence.** The UI-loop watchdog (`internal/tui/stall.go`, c3985bc) writes `dumps/tui-stall-<ts>.txt` only for a loop that never comes back — every dump on disk is that shape. The reported freeze always recovered (the user typed "continue config" afterwards), so nothing recorded it, and 14 dumps from the same morning document only the never-returning kind. `Run`'s loop now closes each iteration through `beatDone(phase, start)`: past `slowIteration` (shipped 10s) it writes `xdev: UI loop iteration took 45.2s (event)` on stderr, naming the phase (`event` = key handling, `dirty` = repaint request, `tick` = background frame) so the next report is a diagnosis rather than a symptom. The heartbeat is unchanged, so the dump and the line stay complementary. `TestBeatDoneNamesASlowIteration` pins exactly one line per slow iteration, the named phase, and the beating.

2. **The bash call row could paint a torn rune.** `toolDetail` (`internal/tui/blocks.go`) is the one render path with no `sanitizeOutput` behind it — its return goes straight into `SetContent` — and it truncated the naming argument at a raw byte 400. For any non-ASCII command (a Vietnamese or CJK `command`/`input` argument: `len("ệ")` is 3 bytes) that cut lands inside a rune and emits a lone continuation byte, in a row every consumer below assumes is runes (width math, `wrap`, the terminal). The 400-byte window now cuts on a rune boundary (`runeBoundary`) and the decoded field is passed through `utf8Only` first; invalid bytes are dropped rather than replaced with U+FFFD, because the replacement char is one cell wide where the torn bytes were zero and a row whose width is a lie paints its box border in the wrong column. The unparseable-args fallback is sanitized too — that path had the identical defect, and `TestToolDetailNeverReturnsTornUTF8` drives both plus the second truncation in `toolSummary` at four widths.

Verified: `go build ./...` clean, `gofmt` clean on all five files, `go test ./internal/tui/` passes. Both new tests fail against `git stash`ed HEAD. The one `internal/tool` failure (`TestSecurityScanNoGoModule`) reproduces on a clean tree — a missing `go` on the child `PATH`, unrelated. Recorded honestly: the user's own freeze left no trace and is not reproduced here; this wave ships what will name it, not a manufactured root cause. The prime suspect remains the documented one at `draw()` (a synchronous `Show()` flush blocking on a suspended tty, #283), which is exactly a recovering multi-second iteration.

*Last updated: 2026-09-23 (fix/bashshell-crash follow-up — repeated quit chords no longer crash xdev): the TUI can receive two Ctrl+C bytes in one terminal burst. `Run` drains the already-queued keys before it returns to its select, so the first quit closes `quitCh` and the second reaches `App.Quit` again; the unguarded `close` then panicked with `close of closed channel` and killed the whole process. `Quit` is now idempotent via `sync.Once`, pinned by `TestRepeatedQuitChordDoesNotPanic`. This is a process-exit defect, distinct from the earlier recovered slow bash/UI iteration report in `fix/bash-tui-crash`; no shell command or secret is involved.
*Last updated: 2026-09-24 (fix/tui-clickable-urls — assistant Markdown URLs open from the terminal): Markdown rendered explicit HTTP(S) links without their destination and did not autolink bare URLs, so a visible answer could not be clicked. The renderer now keeps each target on the painted run (including headings, emphasis, wrapped/table layouts, balanced destinations, and bare URLs); `paint` publishes per-frame hit rectangles; the existing primary-button gesture opens one only on press+release with no drag and revalidates the target against the current frame. Modal overlays mask hidden transcript links, Shift-drag and drag selection are unchanged, timestamps cannot steal a hit, and opener children are reaped asynchronously. `go test ./internal/tui/` and `go vet ./internal/tui/` pass; CGO-free Linux/amd64, Darwin/arm64, and Windows/amd64 TUI test binaries compile. The two repository-wide `cmd/xdev` failures are environment-dependent and unrelated (a local credential changes the connect picker; global skills defeat the empty-repo fixture).

*Last updated: 2026-09-24 (fix/opaque-400-retry): an onegw/OpenCode stream-start HTTP 400 whose body only said `Upstream request failed: [invalid_request_error] invalid request` was classified as `ClassBadRequest` but then fell through the recovery switch's default branch; with the shipped `retry.infinite` policy this replayed the same rejected request indefinitely and printed the misleading `all targets down` notice. `oneTurnWithRecovery` now returns `ClassBadRequest` directly, preserving the provider error without retrying it; recognized recoverable 400s (missing field, tool-name length, explicit upstream refusal) remain transient. Regression coverage: `TestBadRequestMissingOutputRetriesInsteadOfEndingTheRun` and `TestOpaqueBadRequestDoesNotUseAllTargetsDownNotice` in `internal/agent/emptyoutput_test.go`; focused `go test ./internal/agent ./internal/ai ./internal/config -count=1` and `go vet` pass. The full suite retains the two pre-existing `cmd/xdev` failures: `TestConnectPickerItems` and `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills`.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-24 (fix/retry-health-check — the pre-turn provider health check bypassed the recovery ladder): a refused connection at `127.0.0.1:8080/v1/models` exhausted the fallback chain and returned immediately with `agent: health check failed and all targets drained`, even though stream transport errors already used `retry.infinite`. Health-check failures now fail over first, then use an independent bounded/infinite health-check retry budget with cancellable backoff, visible round notices, and fallback-cooldown reversion. Tests: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/agent/...`, `gofmt`, `go test ./internal/agent -count=1`, focused repeated tests, and `go test -race ./internal/agent -count=1`; a live local API outage/recovery interaction completed successfully. The full `go test ./...` run retains two unrelated host-environment-dependent `cmd/xdev` failures; both pass with an isolated `HOME`/`XDEV_AGENT_DIR`.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-27 (`perf/agent-turn-cost`): three changes against the measured turn-count tax in `research/2026-09-15-xdev-slow-session-rca.md` and `research/2026-09-15-omp-vs-xdev-speed-bench.html` (per-turn cost is at parity — 8.5s vs 9.1s — so the gap is turns, not speed). (1) §1 Goal 4's prompt was 685 tokens against its own 1,000 budget, and the slack was spent on nothing: one ignored "use read not cat" line (bash:read ran 15:1 vs omp's 3.9:1) and **no delegation guidance at all**, for a harness already carrying `maxBatchParallel=8`, five agent definitions and depth-2 nesting. `SystemPromptBase` gains two scoped sections (context-gathering, delegating) and now measures 854 tokens — still inside Goal 4, with 146 tokens of headroom left so `maxPromptTokens` keeps biting. `TestBasePromptCarriesToolDiscipline` pins each rule, and every needle is verified absent from the pre-change prompt. The task tool's description gains the same constraints, and its schema now advertises the `{context, tasks[]}` batch shape that has been parsed since parity finding T3 #1 but was invisible — `required:["prompt"]` additionally made a batch unsendable on strict providers. (2) MCP tools were un-deferrable, so a connected server's full schema rode every request; `mcpclient.Register` now defers past `DeferThreshold = 12`, leaving small surfaces untouched, and indexes each tool with its server name as a search tag. (3) `part of #296`: a background subagent's result now reaches an idle parent — `Hub.AddSettleListener` is additive alongside the dock's `SetNotify`, and `StartHubNoticeDelivery` reuses the schedule-reminder shape (idle gate, claim the turn, persist to the store, start the turn) because `Run` takes `history` by value and an idle session has no Agent to drain a queue. New: `internal/agent/hubnotice.go`, 2 test files (12 tests). `go build`, `go vet`, `gofmt` (changed files), `go test -count=1 ./...` and `go test -race -count=1 ./...` pass except the two pre-existing `cmd/xdev` failures noted above; cross-compiles for all six linux/darwin/windows × amd64/arm64 targets with `CGO_ENABLED=0`. Ceiling: the notice is TUI-only, and #296's addressing half (`list_agents`, children messaging each other) is still open.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-28 (`fix/confirm-first` — the agent started implementing before confirming the requirement, even when the prompt already said to fix it). Field report: session `df12bbc8` in x-trader, on the prompt *"Find the root causes and fix why the jev decisions held at gates · EDGE_HURDLE_UNREACHABLE"*, answered with 12 turns of `read`/`grep`/`curl` across four files before anyone stated what the request was read to mean — and the model's own first thought was that the repo was xdev, not the repo the session was in. The rule already existed, but only in the global `~/.xdev/agent/AGENTS.md` context file, which is injected under a hard cap and framed as "not authoritative" — a convention that lives only there gets truncated and discounted. The fix moves it where it binds: a `Before you act:` block in `SystemPromptBase` (`internal/agent/prompt.go`), naming the triggers (ambiguous, non-trivial, spans repos, touches files outside the working directory), the mechanism (`ask`, not a prose question), and the boundary (a clear explicit instruction is its own confirmation; one confirmation covers the run). Deliberately a prompt rule, not the `write` approval gate — the default stays YOLO per §1 Goal 4, and the shipped approval modes are untouched. Base prompt 1,907 runes against its own 2,000 ceiling; `TestBasePromptCarriesConfirmFirst` is the guard, with every needle verified absent from the pre-change prompt. `go build`, `go vet`, `gofmt` (changed files), `go test -count=1 ./internal/agent ./internal/tool` and `go test -count=1 ./cmd/xdev -run TestBundledPrompt` pass; `cmd/xdev` still has the one pre-existing `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills` failure, unchanged by this branch.*

*Last updated: 2026-09-28 (`fix/bash-row-pairing` — the TUI showed no output for a bash call that DID run: two `bash` calls in one batch, finishing out of order, swapped results). Field report: session `1f32044b`, where every turn emitted a pair of `bash` calls (the model reads and shells in parallel) and the transcript boxes read as "the command never ran". Root cause: `internal/tui.FinishTool` identified a tool row by TOOL NAME only — the newest running row with that name — while `agent.runTools` executes same-batch calls concurrently (`MaxToolWorkers=6`, `internal/agent/loop.go`), so whichever result returned first closed the second call's row: one command's output painted under the other command's name, and the row still spinning over the swapped output reads as a hang. The provider's call id was already on the hook (`ai.ToolCallBlock.ID`, which `cmd/xdev/acp.go` uses and the TUI dropped). Fix: `Block.CallID` + `AddToolBlock`/`FinishTool` take the call id and match on it (name only as the fallback for idless callers, so replayed history and bang mode keep pairing and a replay cannot steal a live row); bang runs get their own `bang-N` id; the status row's "● bash" indicator becomes a depth counter (`BeginActiveCommand`/`EndActiveCommand`) so the first of two concurrent calls to finish no longer clears it. Pinned by `TestConcurrentSameNamedCallsPairByCallID`, `TestIdlessFinishDoesNotStealALiveRow` and `TestActiveCommandIsACounter` in `internal/tui/tool_row_pairing_test.go`; `go build`, `go vet`, `gofmt` (changed files) and `go test -count=1 ./internal/tui` pass, and `cmd/xdev` still has the two pre-existing failures (`TestConnectPickerItems`, `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills`), unchanged by this branch.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-28 (`perf/mcp-autostart-off-thread` — the autoStart health probe and launch wait were the last blocking network+subprocess step left on the TUI startup path). `attachMCP` deferred the MCP connect to `go finishMCP` but ran the `autoStart` probe inline, before `app.Run()`: a local server that was down cost an HTTP probe plus a 500ms poll loop of up to `healthTimeoutSec` (30s on the leankg recipe in `<dataDir>/mcp.yml`) of an undrawn screen. The probe now runs inside `finishMCP`, so it lands on the same background goroutine as the connect it gates; `print` mode, which passes `wait=true` and needs the tools before the first turn, is unchanged. Covered by `TestAttachMCPDoesNotHealthProbeOnTheCallersGoroutine` (`cmd/xdev/mcpautostart_test.go`), which fails at 8s against the old inline path and passes in 0.00s now.*
*Last updated: 2026-09-28 (perf/loop-cheap-wins — three bounds the loop did not have, ported from a mechanic-by-mechanic read of the deepseek-harness reference at `21638c56`; full gap analysis in [research/2026-09-28-deepseek-harness-gap.md](research/2026-09-28-deepseek-harness-gap.md)):* a read of the TypeScript reference produced 3.9k lines of file:line evidence (session core, loop, tools…[+342b]
A model that has stopped making progress usually looks like one thing: the same tool, the same arguments, several turns running — each costing a full model round trip to re-derive a conclusion it already had. dsh's `guard/repeat-tool-reminder` (`src/index.ts:191-211`) counts that and injects a notice; it never vetoes, because a guard that blocks a call the model genuinely needs is worse tha…[+101b]
Three details carry the value, and all three are cheaper in Go than they were in TypeScript:
1. **Key order is not identity.** dsh hand-writes a deep key-sort (`sortJsonValue`) to canonicalize arguments. Go's `json.Marshal` sorts map keys itself, so the whole function is the standard library. What stdlib does *not* do is preserve numeric fidelity, so the decoder uses `UseNumber`: without it `12345678901234567890` decodes to `1.2345678901234567e+19` and two calls with genuinely differ…[+174b]
2. **Counting happens where the result is built**, so a *denied* call counts too. A model hammering a denied call is the loop most worth breaking, and a counter that only saw successes would miss it.
3. **A steering message ends the chain.** Repetition around a new human instruction is compliance, not a loop; the chain resets at the drain (`loop.go`, the step-boundary seam) rather than at the guard.
The chain advances in **model order, not worker-finish order**: `runTools` fans out to a bounded pool and joins, then the guard runs single-goroutine over the committed results. A batch whose workers finished out of order must still count as the model wrote it — that ordering is the reason the guard lives at the loop boundary instead of inside `runOneTool`.
Thresholds are dsh's `[3, 5, 8]`: the first is a gentle nudge, later ones name the tool, the run length, and the arguments. The quote is capped at 500 chars (`argumentsPreviewChars` upstream) because a `write` body is a plausible repeat argument and quoting it whole would spend the context the notice exists to protect — the cap bounds what the model *reads*, never what is *compared*, so det…[+21b]
Also settled in this wave, from checking the reference directly: **dsh does no latency engineering at all.** Zero `preconnect`/`keepAlive`/`warmup` in `packages/llm`, no `coalesc*` anywhere in the streaming path (the hits are all in `packages/experimental/`), no partial-JSON tool dispatch, and a three-line `BENCHMARK.md` that points at a Python SDK guide. What it optimizes is *turn count* —…[+672b]
**Verified:** `go build ./...` clean, `gofmt` clean, `go vet` clean, `go test` green for `internal/agent`, `internal/session`, `internal/ai/...`, `internal/tui`. `TestLoopNoticesARepeatedToolCall` was confirmed to fail with the loop wiring removed (0 notices over 5 identical calls).
