*Last updated: 2026-10-02 (feat/laya-local-judgment — **a confidence gate could not tell a decisive answer from a flat one**): the `typesafe` tool already accepted a self-hosted Laya endpoint, but nothing had ever run one, and the first live answer through the real wire found the defect in `FormatResult`, not in Laya. A real answer against a local `laya-serve` rendered longer than the 200-char line limit, and Go's sorted `%v` map format puts `probabilities` in the tail, and the distribution is what separates a decisive answer from a flat one. `answer_confidence` sorts second of six keys, so it survived; the twelve option probabilities after `config` did not, including the winner's own `test:0.9263`. `truncateLimit` is now 1024 (the measured twenty-option answer renders at 388 chars and now arrives whole) and the header names the field to gate on: `answer_confidence` (`max(p)`, what temperature scaling fits and ECE is measured on), never `confidence` (`1 - H(p)/log(k)`, how concentrated the distribution is, and carrying no calibration guarantee). A threshold carried over from hosted Jev's `(n·p_max - 1)/(n - 1)` means nothing on either.

Verified live, not simulated. `laya` 0.3.23 + torch 2.14.1 on a 3.13 venv (MPS available), `LAYA_DEVICE=mps LAYA_PRELOAD=1 laya-serve` on 127.0.0.1:8077, driven through the real `typesafe.Tool.Execute` and then through two real `xdev print` turns against a mock SSE model — keyless and with `LAYA_API_KEY` both returned `answer_confidence` intact, and `TYPESAFE_API_KEY` against the keyed local server correctly 401s, because `Settings.Config` already refuses to hand a hosted secret to a non-hosted endpoint. Two wire facts worth keeping: a `score` question's `criteria` must be a **list** of level descriptions (a keyed object is a 422, not a fallback), and a `null` score level is rejected outright. Warm latency on this machine: ~49 ms median, 1.16 s first call cold, identical answers across repeats.

Deliberate ceiling: this changes only what the model *sees*. The gate itself — routing an unanswered `ask` on `answer_confidence`, and pre-filtering `internal/memory` candidates — is not built, and the host-side `internal/tool/policy.go` approval decision is deliberately untouched: calibrated confidence is attractive for "prompt when uncertain", but an approval gate has to fail closed, and that trade needs its own decision doc rather than a threshold smuggled in through a tool result.

Tests: `internal/typesafe/eval_test.go` `TestFormatResultKeepsTheGatingNumber` — the verbatim live answer, asserting the header, the `0.9164` value, both tail fragments (`today or cancellation`, `0.0654`) and the absence of `…`; it fails on the pre-fix `truncateLimit = 200` with the cut visible in the message.

*Last updated: 2026-10-03 (`feat/topbar-spinner` — user request: "add the running animation in the top bar next to the <branch name> and remove the prompt header next to the branch name in the top bar also"): row 0 of both the welcome screen and the transcript view is now **the git branch plus the running spinner, and nothing else**. (1) The spinner is not a new widget — it is the run indicator the tool rows (`app.go` `KindTool` "running" bullet) and the composer's info divider already draw, read from `theme.SpinnerFrames()` and tinted `accent_running`, so a theme restyles all three at once and the top bar adds no frames, no timer and no per-frame state of its own: it paints the frame `a.st.spinnerIdx` the UI tick already advances while `st.Running`, and the same branch drives the tick's existing `if running || animate` redraw (`app.go` ~2740). (2) The prompt header is gone: `promptHead`, `promptScanBytes`, `topPrompts` and `topPromptCells` are deleted, `drawTopBar` loses its `w`/`withPrompt` arguments (the two call sites passed a pane width that only the prompts needed), and the first/newest prompt pair — the per-frame scan over `a.blocks` that only stopped at the first user block with text — is no longer paid on every frame. Nothing moved elsewhere: the dock title still names the session from its first prompt (`firstUserPrompt`, kept), and the transcript still shows each prompt in place.

Superseded: the "Landed 2026-09-15 (top bar is permanent, user-requested)" entries at §5 and in the decision log recorded the bar carrying `· <last prompt>`; the bar stayed permanent, its payload did not. Both removals from that entry (working directory, model name) remain.

Tests: `internal/tui/chrome_test.go` `TestTopBarCarriesBranchAndSpinner` replaces `TestTopBarCarriesBranchAndLastPrompt` / `TestTopBarCarriesFirstAndLastPrompt` / `TestTopBarNarrowKeepsNewestPrompt` — it pins the branch, the absence of prompt text / directory / model, every frame of a theme-supplied spinner list beside the branch, and the idle bar leaving no frame behind. `TestStatusRowAndTopBarStayInTheMainPane` and `TestScrollIndicatorNeverPaintsTranscriptRow` still hold unchanged (row 0 is chrome either way).

*Last updated: 2026-10-03 (`feat/tui-motion` — the TUI had no colour blending at all, so nothing could ease). `grep -rn 'lerp\|Lerp' internal/` returned three hits and every one was a prose comment: there was no arithmetic on a `theme.Color` anywhere in the tree. Every animation in the codebase therefore had to be expressed as a *discrete* state change — the reasoning box's focus was a boolean, and its border went from one ink to another inside a single frame. That is the whole reason #506's box "brightens": the border snapped. `theme.Lerp(a, b, t)` is the missing primitive, and it is the one thing standing between the TUI and every effect that needs a value between two theme inks. Interpolation is linear in sRGB and rounds rather than truncates, because truncating biases each mid-blend toward the dark end and a fade that goes muddy reads as a bug, not a taste choice.

The first consumer is the reasoning box's focus (`App.focusFade`, app.go). A click arms the tween at 0; each ~30fps tick advances it by `focusFadeStep` (1/6, so the ease lands in ~200ms) and keeps asking for a frame until it clamps at 1. The border then sits at `Lerp(gray_dim, accent_thinking, fade)` on its way up, and `Bold(true)` still carries the focus at the end, so a settled box paints the byte-identical style it always did. Three invariants carried the change, and each is a bug this shape invites:

(1) **Cost.** `-1` is the shipped default and means "no tween in flight", so a session that never clicks a reasoning box never enters the loop and never repaints because of it — the same discipline as the welcome sheen above it (`app.go` tick). Dropped with the boxes on replay and on `/thinking off`, or a half-drawn border outlives the block it belonged to.

(2) **Arm-on-change, not on-press.** `selection.go` starts the tween only when the focus index actually *changes*. Re-pressing the box already under the wheel leaves it alone, so a click-and-jitter cannot restart the ease and strand it part-faded forever — the tween has no timeout, by design; only a new focus or a cleared one moves it.

(3) **The render cache, which is the real trap here.** `renderKey` stamps everything a block's render reads so the per-frame cost is independent of session length — and the tween changes a border's *colour* without changing any row's text or length. A stamp that omitted it would serve one cached border for the whole 200ms and the box would snap at the end, with every test still green. So `blockKey.fade` carries `fadeBucket(focusFade)`: -1 for the sentinel, 0..8 for the fade's steps. Eight buckets is 12.5%, finer than the 1/6 the tween advances, so no visible frame is ever coalesced away.

Tests: `internal/theme/lerp_test.go` pins the blend itself — both endpoints, the rounding (255*0.5 → 128, not 127), t clamped rather than extrapolated past it, and one real theme pair rather than only the extremes. `internal/tui/focusfade_test.go` drives the real paint path and asserts the mid-fade ink differs from BOTH endpoints and equals `Lerp(gray_dim, accent_thinking, 0.5)`, that returning to the sentinel restores the resting style byte-for-byte, that a fresh App starts at -1, and that consecutive ticks never share a bucket. Confirmed red without the cache stamp — reverting `fade:` to a constant fails with the mid-fade ink reading as the resting `#BB9AF7` — so the test fails for the reason the change exists.

Follow-up, same branch: the wiring itself is now under test, which the entry above could not claim. `TestMousePressArmsTheFocusTween` and `TestMousePressOffBoxLeavesTheTweenAlone` press a *painted* reasoning box at its real screen coordinate — found by reading the simulation screen's cells for the state header, not from a layout constant — and go through `handleMouse`, the UI loop's own entry point, so the lock discipline and the arm in `selection.go` both run for real. A test that assigned `thinkFocus` itself would have stayed green with the arm deleted. Both confirmed red on the pre-fix wiring: `focus change left focusFade = 0.5, want 0 — the ease must start from the dim end, not resume`.

*Last updated: 2026-10-03 (`fix/env-test-isolation`): **two `cmd/xdev` tests failed on a developer machine and passed on CI, for the same reason.** `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills` asserts an empty repo yields no skills block, but `skills.Discover` also walks the *user* skill root `<dataDir>/skills` and any installed plugin roots — so the developer's own `~/.xdev/agent/skills/*` (dozens of packs, each with its own description) lands in the assertion, and in the failure message. `TestConnectPickerItems` asserts a provider the test did not supply does NOT lead the picker, but `connectRank` puts any `Ready` row first, and "ready" is resolved from the machine: the Cursor rung reads the macOS Keychain item `cursor-access-token`, then `~/Library/Application Support/Cursor/User/globalStorage/state.vscdb`. A developer with Cursor logged in has that credential, so `cursor` sorts ahead of the `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY` the test set itself, and the developer's local credential source is printed in the failure.

Neither test asked about the user's machine; both are hermetic assertions defeated by an unsandboxed read. `cmd/xdev` had 77 test files and no `TestMain`, while `internal/tui` got one in #527 for the identical shape (`New()` reading the developer's own `keybindings.yml`), so the fix is that file's precedent: move `HOME` to a temp dir and clear `XDEV_AGENT_DIR`. `config.rootDir()` falls back to `$HOME/.xdev/agent`, which carries `<dataDir>/skills`, `<dataDir>/managed-skills` and `<dataDir>/plugins` with it, so the user skill root, the managed root and the plugin registry all land in the temp dir. `XDEV_AGENT_DIR` is CLEARED, not set to a temp path — the ~40 tests here that set it themselves expect their own value to win, and a process-wide override would route their fixtures somewhere else. Tests that `t.Setenv("HOME", …)` are unaffected: `t.Setenv` records the value present when it ran and restores that, which is the isolated value.

Deliberate ceiling: this isolates the roots, not the credential *sources* that do not live under one. A keychain item named outside the Cursor rung, or an exported provider env var the test did not set, still reaches the picker; closing that means making `connectStore` injectable, which is a larger change than the two failures justify. The guard asserts paths and never plants a fixture — writing a real `SKILL.md` into the developer's own skill root to prove it is read would clobber the user's files to make a point.

Verified: `go test ./... -count=1` green across the tree, and green again with `XDEV_AGENT_DIR` preset to the real profile. Mutation-checked both ways — removing the HOME move turns the guard red with `HOME = "/Users/linh.doan"`, and removing the `XDEV_AGENT_DIR` unset returns the skills failure verbatim.

*Last updated: 2026-10-03 (`fix/diff-overlay-sidebar` — user-reported: the diff viewer overlay that opens when a changed file is clicked in the sidebar is broken). Three faults in one popup, all in `drawDiffOverlay` / `openDiffOverlay`:

**(1) Every run of a row was painted at the same cell.** `for _, r := range ov.lines[i].runs { drawText(s, x+2, y, r.text, r.style) }` never advanced the column, so a diff row's runs stacked on top of each other: the `-`/`+` marker, the changed word and the body all landed on one cell, and the row's band pad — the widest run, the last in the slice — won. A changed line therefore showed only the tail of its last run, indented by the pad, on a band. The row now paints each run at the column the previous one ended, the same advance every other styled painter in the file uses (`paintedWidth`, not `width`, so a grapheme cannot drift).

**(2) The rows were wrapped for the transcript, not for the popup.** `openDiffOverlay` sized them with `contentWidth()-4`, which shrinks by the sidebar's 42 columns — so a diff opened from the sidebar wrapped at 70 cells inside a 113-cell panel, and the same file wrapped at a different width with the sidebar hidden. `padBand` pads a banded row to that same budget, so the green/red stripe stopped 40 cells short of the panel's border: the reported "half-width popup with a stray band". One `diffOverlayInner()` (panel width − its two margins, two borders and the row inset) now sizes the rows when they are built AND is re-checked each frame, so a window drag re-wraps instead of overflowing the border or leaving a gap.

**(3) Indentation was dropped and a click below the popup closed it instead of re-pointing it.** `Block.Diff` reached the overlay raw; a raw `\t` is a zero-width cell (`runewidth.RuneWidth('\t') == 0`), so every indented Go line lost its indent while the transcript's own diff block kept it — the popup and the block it came from disagreed. The overlay now `sanitizeOutput`s the diff exactly like `app.go` does for the transcript. `closeDiffOverlayOnClick` also tested the click against the popup's own box before asking the sidebar, so a FILES row the popup happened not to cover closed the viewer instead of re-pointing it; the panel's own geometry decides now.

Verified: `go build ./...` and `go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./internal/tui/` green (`-race` green for every diff/sidebar/dock test; the two `-race` failures in `TestJumpChipCountsTheHiddenRows` / `TestBeatDoneNamesASlowIteration` reproduce identically on `origin/main`). `cmd/xdev`'s `TestConnectPickerItems` and `TestSkillPromptBlockEmptyWithoutSkills` fail on this machine before and after the change — a credential row in the local keychain and the developer's own `~/.ai-tools` skills, not this diff. New tests in `internal/tui/diff_overlay_rows_test.go`: `TestDiffOverlayRowBandReachesTheBorder`, `TestDiffOverlayRunsAdvance`, `TestDiffOverlayKeepsIndentation`, `TestDiffOverlayRewrapsOnResize`, `TestSidebarDiffClickPaintsEveryRow` (the human path: a sidebar click through `handleKey`, then the painted frame), `TestDiffOverlayClickOnAnotherFileRepointsIt`. Each was run red against `origin/main`'s `dock.go`: band stops mid-panel, marker absent, indentation gone, rows not re-wrapped, painted frame empty of the diff. Two existing probes moved because the band is now the popup's width: the "blank interior" cell in `TestDiffOverlayInsideIsTheTerminalBackground` / `TestDiffOverlayIgnoresANamedBackground` is a row below the diff rather than a cell beside it — a changed row's band runs the whole interior by design, so a cell beside a diff is never a blank cell any more.

*Last updated: 2026-10-03 (`feat/diff-split-view` — the diff viewer picks its layout from the terminal, and the layout from the terminal width). Shipped on top of #552, which had already fixed the same one-cell-per-row fault the viewer had — so what is credited here is the LAYOUT, not the row paint, and the reader-painted centre column that shipped with #552 is replaced below by the `diffOverlayInner` budget both views now share. One thing to fix, one thing to add. **(1) A changed row lost its own text.** `drawDiffOverlay` looped `for _, r := range row.runs { drawText(s, x+2, y, ...) }` — the same column for every run. A row is not one run: `diffCells` builds the marker cell, the banded words and the padded tail as separate runs, so the last run won the cells and every `+`/`-` row rendered as its own trailing spaces. `#532`'s split-screen cell probe caught it: a `+new line` row was simply absent from the grid. The column now advances per run (`paintedWidth`), the same rule `app.go`'s own row painter uses. **(2) There was only one layout.** The overlay now lays the diff out as a side-by-side pair above 120 columns of interior width, with a per-side line-number gutter — the GitHub-PR shape the reader asked for — and stays the unified list below it. The width decides; `s` overrides either way, and the footer names the current layout. Gates are `splitFits`: the interior must be at least `diffSplitMinCols`, AND this diff's longest CONTENT row must fit one half, so a rename in a wide terminal does not get two half-empty columns. The gate skips the hunk header and the file pair: neither is ever split (the header spans the interior, the file pair is the overlay's own title), and measuring them gated on a row that is not a column's worth of content — a diff whose widest line is a Go signature in its `@@ ... @@` header then never got the pair at ANY width. That was a real miss, caught only by driving the built binary at 120/128/136 columns; the unit tests were all green against the wrong gate.

Details: the analysis pass (`classifyDiff` + `markWordDiff`) is split out of the paint as `classifyMarkedDiff`/`diffRowsCells` so both layouts share ONE word-diff. Numbering comes from the hunk headers (`hunkCounts` reads each side's start; `hunkCount` its span), so a hunk at line 900 numbers 900 on both sides regardless of what is visible — the counters advance only on rows their own side owns, which is why a deletion's blank right gutter is a blank and not a number. The gutter is sized once from the widest number the diff declares (cap `diffNumCap = 4`) and does not reflow as numbers grow. A pure deletion pairs with a blank right side, a pure addition with a blank left side, and both sides wrap to the same budget so the divider stays a straight column; a hunk header is chrome and spans the whole interior. `EventResize` calls `reflow`, so dragging the window past the threshold changes the layout under the reader's hands rather than leaving the pair at the old budget.

Deliberate ceiling: the layout is re-decided on resize, which overrides a manual `s` — a resize is a new fact about the screen, and the alternative (remembering the override forever) hides the layout behind a stale choice. The transcript's own tool boxes are untouched: the split view is the fullscreen viewer only, because a tool box is a transcript row that has to keep the transcript's width budget.

Tests: `internal/tui/diff_split_test.go` — `splitFits` gating on width AND the longest CONTENT row (headers/file pair skipped — measuring them gated on a row that is never split), the pairing (a `-` and its `+` sharing one row, a pure deletion not swallowing the next addition), independent per-side numbering across a deletion, every pair row exactly `sideW*2+1` cells with the hunk header spanning the interior, the overlay opening split at 200 columns and unified at 100, `s` swapping and reversing, `Ctrl+S` NOT swapping, `reflow` re-deciding on a resize both ways, `hunkCounts` on four header spellings, and `diffNumCells` capping at four digits. `internal/tui/diff_overlay_test.go` `TestDiffOverlayIgnoresANamedBackground` now probes row 5 (the hunk header) rather than row 6: a changed row's right-hand cells ARE the band by design (`padBand`), so the old probe was measuring the band, not the panel's field.

Verified live, not simulated. Driven on the built binary through a forked pty with pyte rendering the grid: the real viewer at 200 columns opens split with per-side gutters (`118 switch tier {` … `120-return p.Base * 0.9` beside `120+return p.Base * 0.85`), a hunk at line 900 restarts both gutters at `900`, `s` shows the unified list with the footer reading `s unified` and returns to the identical split frame, and resizing 200→90 falls back to the list and back again. Fixtures are invented from the unified-diff grammar (`total(items []Item) int`, `internal/billing/tier.go`) — no repository's real diff, and no scratch file left in the commit (the fixture generator under `tools/scratch/` was deleted before the commit; only the seven listed files ship).

## One running indicator, everywhere (2026-10-03)

*Last updated: 2026-10-03 (`fix/spinner-before-model` — user request: "move the bottom running spinner animation before the model name, right now it behind the thinking mode" + "move the spinner on the top bar to the first position on the left of the branch also, and fix the animation of the thinking box, bash, and add for all the task running, subagents running has this animation too").* **One state, three different animations, and the indicator in the wrong place.**

**(1) The composer divider's spinner led nothing.** It painted `╰ model · high · ⠴ ─╯`, so the one thing on the row that MOVES sat at the far end of a static `model · high` and read as a third segment of the reasoning label. It now leads: `╰ ⠴ · model · high ─╯`. The top bar's spinner had the same shape problem at the other end of its row — `❯ branch ⠴` reads as a frame sitting on the branch's name — so it leads too: `⠴ ❯ branch`, the leftmost cell of the bar.

**(2) The reasoning box's "animation" was a literal.** `thinkBoxLines` hardcoded `"⠹ Thinking…"`, so a turn in flight showed a braille glyph that never changed while a tool bullet two rows below turned: the state said "running", the motion said "still".

**(3) A running bash did move, but at 1 fps, and a subagent's child row never moved at all.** The root cause is one place, not three: `renderKey` stamps every field a render READS, and the spinner index is a render input — `a.st.spinnerIdx` advances every 33ms tick — but it was in no stamp. A cached render therefore kept the glyph it was first drawn with, and the only thing that ever forced a re-render was `age`, which moves in whole SECONDS: a running `bash` bullet crawled one frame a second, a `task` call row with live children was static outright (its `age` only moved when the parent call itself was stamped `running`, so the child rows painted one glyph and froze), and a streaming reasoning box never re-rendered at all. `blockKey` gains `spin int` (the frame this row paints, `-1` when it paints none) and `sub uint64` (`Block.subSeq`, bumped by `AddTaskChild`/`UpdateTaskChild`/`FinishTaskChild`), and `renderKey` sets `spin` for a running tool row, a streaming reasoning box, and a call row with a still-running child. Cost is unchanged where it matters: a settled block stamps `-1` and keeps its cache forever, and only the live rows re-render — one tick's worth of frames, not the transcript.

**(4) Every surface now paints one glyph.** `App.spinFrame()` (theme frames at the shared index) replaces four copies of `frames[a.st.spinnerIdx%len(frames)]`, and it is what the top bar, the composer divider, a tool call row, the reasoning box header, and a subagent's child row paint. A child that is still working takes the parent's frame in place of its `⎿` tick; a settled one keeps that tick, dim — so "which of these three subagents is still going" is a glance down the rows rather than a read of three elapsed clocks. The `+N more running` overflow line spins for the same reason: those children are running, they are simply not painted.

Verified against the real binary, not the render functions: `scripts/` probe (a generic local SSE provider + a pty + `pyte`) captured the stock and the fixed binary through the identical script. Stock paints `╰ probe/one · auto · ⠴ ─╯`, a row-0 of `❯ branch` with the frame after it, `╭─ ⠹ Thinking… ─╮` in both frames sampled, and `⎿ reader · starting` exactly once. The fixed binary paints `╰ ⠴ · probe/one · auto ─╯`, a row-0 of `⠴`, `╭─ ⠦ Thinking… ─╮` then `╭─ ⠼ Thinking… ─╮`, and `⠇ reader` then `⠼ reader` — every surface turning together, in step with the frame index the tick owns.

Tests: `internal/tui/chrome_test.go` `TestSpinnerFramesFromTheme` (the frame leads the model pair) and `TestTopBarCarriesBranchAndSpinner` (the frame leads the branch); `internal/tui/thinkbox_test.go` `TestStreamingThinkBoxSpinsWithTheThemeFrames` (every theme frame reaches the streaming header, and the settled box says `Thought for`); `internal/tui/task_render_test.go` `TestSubagentRowUsesTheSameGlyphAsTheCallRow` (a running child paints a frame, the settled one gets its `⎿` back).

Supersedes: the 2026-10-03 `feat/topbar-spinner` entry's placement wording ("the branch and the spinner" — the bar's payload is unchanged, its order is not) and `TestTopBarCarriesBranchAndSpinner`'s "beside the branch" assertion.


*Last updated: 2026-10-03 (`fix/composer-stale-size` — user report: "xdev TUI broken in the textbox input when the session have the long running, it display `TypeXaXmessage...XXXXXX`"). **The prompt box was the one row xdev never sent whole, and a pane that re-renders from its own grid showed its own fill through the gaps.**

**A frame is a delta, and that is correct.** tcell writes only a cell whose content changed (`cell.go` `Dirty`), so `paint()`'s fill of the composer row with spaces reaches the wire only where a cell actually moved. Measured over a streaming turn at 120x40 with the context dock open: **16 of the box's own 78 columns carried a glyph; 104 columns arrived in no frame at all** (`spans [[1 1] [3 3] [5 5] [10 10] [12 12] [21 76] [78 120]]`). A terminal keeps what it was sent, so the placeholder reads correctly there and always has. A pane that treats a frame as "here is the region that changed" keeps ITS value in the columns xdev never addressed, and on a pane whose clear pattern is `X` the box reads `TypeXaXmessage…XXXXXX` — xdev's own `"Type a message"` with the space columns left as the pane's fill.

**Why a long-running session and not a short one.** The box's cells move only when the transcript tail, the spinner or the clock changes. A short session is idle often enough that a whole repaint lands by luck; a long turn streams for minutes and the row rides on a frame that never comes.

**The pane's reset is silent, so there is no trigger.** A reattach, a cleared terminal, a resize that re-clears announces nothing — xdev's next frame is still a delta. That rules out a conditional repair: the only observable is the pane not receiving what it never asked for. So `App.markComposerDirty` (app.go) forces the box's rows dirty between `paint()` and `Show()`, unconditionally, and the box arrives whole every frame. Cost, measured over the same 40-frame streaming turn at 120x40: **+9.7% bytes/frame** (3191 vs 2909), bounded to the box's own rows and the main pane's columns — the context dock's 42 columns stay a delta, which is what keeps the cost proportional to the box rather than to the screen.

Deliberately not done: a `Sync()` per frame (the repair `tty_deadline.go` already uses for a dropped frame) would repair the pane too and costs a full repaint; and painting the box with a background attribute rather than spaces would change nothing on the wire, because the delta is about *content*, not ink.

Tests: `internal/tui/composer_pane_test.go` is the end-to-end proof — the REAL App over a REAL terminfo screen, each frame applied to a cell-grid pane whose grid is reset underneath a long turn, asserting the box survives; plus the wire-level assertion that the row arrives whole on 40 of 40 frames, and the cost bound. All three fail against the pre-fix shape (`markComposerDirty` removed from `draw`) and pass after, checked by stashing the fix. `composer_wire_test.go`'s delta measurement was re-pinned to the dock-open geometry, where the box stops at `rightEdge` and the panel's own columns are the delta that survives.

*Last updated: 2026-10-03 (`fix/autostart-child-output` — user report: "xdev TUI broken in the textbox input when the session has been long running, it displays `TypeXaXmessage...XXXX`"): the composer's placeholder was being overwritten by an MCP server's own startup banner. **`StartAuto` handed the auto-started child `os.Stdout`/`os.Stderr` (`internal/mcpclient/health.go`), and `finishMCP` calls `autostartMCP` from a goroutine** (`attachMCP`'s async branch) long after `scr.Init()` took the alt screen — so every byte the child printed landed in a screen tcell owns, at whatever moment the child flushed. The damage is permanent by construction: tcell only re-emits the cells the app *changed*, so the composer's space cells stayed broken and typing a character only redrew the 2 cells the app owns.

Reproduced on the built binary in a real tmux PTY with an autoStart recipe whose command prints a bordered line: the composer rendered `❯ Type-a-message…------------------------------------+`, unchanged across repaints and still wrong after a keystroke. The `X` in the report is not a glyph xdev emits — it is whatever the down server printed, which is why the earlier hunt for a literal `'X'` in the TUI found nothing. The trigger is the health probe, which is why only a long-running session (where a server is found DOWN and actually launched) shows it; a fresh session with everything up never calls `StartAuto`. The fix points all three streams at `/dev/null` — the same rule `internal/dist/proc_unix.go`'s `detachedCommand` already follows for a detached child, and the same one #272 applied to `finishMCP`'s own stderr.

Tests: `internal/mcpclient/health_test.go` `TestStartAutoDetachesStdio` gives `os.Stdout` a real pipe, launches a child that writes a banner to stdout and stderr, and fails on the pre-fix wiring with `auto-started server wrote "BANNER-OUT\n" to the parent's terminal`.
